_**Round 892 (2026-08-11) — (WARM.18b): THE FAMILY ROUND 891 REFUSED IS **CONVERTED** — `CtaFrame`'s localTypes+declNodes+shadowed AND the narrowing frame's `EpochMap`, **1,214,236 ENTRIES COPIED PER REBUILD -> 28,695 UNDO RECORDS OVER AN IDENTICAL POPULATION** — AND ALL THREE REFUSAL REASONS DISSOLVED UNDER THE INSTRUMENT ROUND 891 QUEUED BUT DID NOT BUILD.**_ Round 891 named the instrument this family needed — *"a counting facade over `CtaFrame.localTypes`, run for one census, BEFORE any code"* — and this round built exactly that, first. **THE CENSUS** (throwaway, over EVERY write path, reverted before a line of the fix; a hook anywhere else is round 891's own 2-of-3-paths hook, which read 6.3x optimistic): **28,695 writes needing an undo record against 1,089,527 entries copied = 2.6%**, plus 567 free root writes, **zero** `putAll` outside the seed and **zero** `clear()` calls. **THE THREE REFUSALS, AND TWO WERE ARTEFACTS OF NOT HAVING MEASURED:** (i) the ratio now exists; (ii) **`ambiguousNames` RESETS rather than shadows — true, and IRRELEVANT: it is not in the family.** A fn frame gives it a FRESH EMPTY set, O(1); it was never COPIED, so there is nothing to replace — **the refusal treated a sibling FIELD as a sibling COST**; (iii) the narrowing frame's `EpochMap` was the one real reason, and its answer is to convert it TOO, onto the SAME stack — not optional, since a function declared in a then-branch takes its base from `ctaFrames.last()`, and worth another 124,709 entries. **CONDITIONS re-audited over all 423 references:** strictly LIFO with a per-file `reset`; **no `.keys`/`.values`/`.entries`/`.forEach`/`.iterator`/`.sorted`/`.toMutableMap` reader and no `for (x in map)` anywhere**; 0 `clear()` measured; and the retention question resolves — 37 local POINTER SWAPS (object identity stable) plus exactly two FIELD retentions, both capturing the PRE-SPINE resting map, never a cta frame's. One stated divergence: the `onMutate` epoch bump is a SUPERSET of what the replaced maps did, which is the safe direction for a probe-only fence. **12-ARM ABLATION, every non-zero red set DISTINCT, five arms with a uniquely-their-own pin — AND ITS REAL PRODUCT IS THAT IT FOUND A MISSING PIN: A9, the arm for the invariant that DECIDED the round, reddened NOTHING** (no fixture had an `if`); the first replacement pin then FAILED ON HEAD because this compiler emits nothing for `const s: string = <string | undefined>`, so the working shape (`typeof` over `string | number`) was verified against an un-narrowed control in the CLI loop before either assertion was written. **A10/A11 still redden nothing for STRUCTURAL reasons** (no fixture drives declNodes/shadowedNames across a fn boundary; `diagnose()` is single-file so a cross-FILE leak is inexpressible), and **12 of 32 pins are reddened by no arm and are recorded as INVARIANT GUARDS, not counted as coverage**. **NO WALL-TIME A/B IS CLAIMED** — ~0.8-1.1%, inside what this box settles. **GATES: suite 14,308 -> 14,324 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 16 new pins (7 more with the ablation's repair); compiler-profile `--listAll` digest `59d930db849399aea5e03e25fedb8e4e` over 46 errors; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 694 classes.** `docs/perf/cta-frame-copy-families.md` §§ 6-11._

_**Round 891 (2026-08-11) — (WARM.18): THE `CtaFrame.varTypes` PER-SCOPE COPY BECOMES AN UNDO LOG — **1,145,523 ENTRIES COPIED PER REBUILD -> 16,182 UNDO RECORDS OVER AN IDENTICAL POPULATION** — AND THE OTHER THREE FAMILIES ARE REFUSED WITH A PRICED CONDITION TABLE, INCLUDING THE ONE THAT IS THE BIGGER PRIZE.**_ Round 869 took two of the six per-scope copy families and stopped, because the rest are where a wrong scope does not crash — it silently resolves a name to an OUTER binding. _**Round 889 (2026-08-11) — (HASH.1)(a): THE CO-ACCESS CENSUS ANSWERS **NO** — tsgo's `LinkStore` IS **PRICED AND REFUSED**, NOT UNSTARTED — AND THE CENSUS FOUND THE HASH FAMILY'S REAL DEFECT ON THE WAY PAST: `nodeKey` HASHES TO `pos xor end`, i.e. TO **THE SET OF NODE LENGTHS**, SO `nodeToFlow` WAS NOT A HASH TABLE BUT A HANDFUL OF RED-BLACK TREES.**_ Round 886 recorded hash probing at 24.3-24.6% of compile-thread samples and named tsgo's ~25 narrow `core.LinkStore[K,V]`s — each ONE probe returning a struct of CO-ACCESSED fields, where ours is one map per FACT — as a checker-wide refactor it deliberately did not start. **THE CENSUS** (`scripts/round889_coaccess.py`, over the round-809 length-preserving stripper; clusters ranked by the hash-family samples their enclosing function OWNS, per round 732's law) finds **254 clusters**, and the two that are exactly tsc's `NodeLinks`/`SymbolLinks` shape — a type cache plus its in-progress sentinel, same key, same site — are the **TOP cluster at 0.49%** (`getTypeFromTypeNodeCore`) and the **BOTTOM at 1 sample** (`getTypeOfSymbol`); a merge saves one probe on the MISS path only, since a HIT never touches the sentinel. **The `LinkStore` shape does not port and it is not a near miss** — and the key-shape census agrees from the other side: the family's largest identified mechanism is **per-scope map COPYING at 18.0%**, then String-keyed name resolution, not per-entity fact probing. **A CORRECTION TO ROUND 886's OWN NUMBER IS WHERE THE ROUND TURNS:** its family pattern `"java.util.HashMap."` never matches `HashMap$TreeNode.find`, so the family is **26.42%** and the missing **6.46% of all samples is TREEIFIED-BUCKET work** — the part with a mechanism, invisible for two rounds behind one absent `$`. **THE MECHANISM:** `Long.hashCode` is `(int)(v xor (v ushr 32))`, so `(pos shl 32) or end` hashes to **exactly `pos xor end`**, and since `end` is `pos` plus the node's LENGTH that range is "the set of node lengths in this file" — a few hundred values **however many nodes there are**. Modelled on a 20,000-node file (`nodeToFlow` is per FILE): **278 of 32,768 buckets, max bucket 1,765, 98.3% of keys past the treeify threshold**; program-wide, 399 of 524,288 with max 23,118. `nodeToFlow` is **2.02% of samples with 79% of it treeified**, `Relation.cache` 0.97% with 56%. **LANDED — ONE LINE:** a multiplicative finalizer by the golden-ratio odd constant `LongKeyMap` already uses; multiplication by an ODD constant mod 2^64 is a **BIJECTION**, so the key stays exact and only its bit pattern changes — **14,896 buckets, max 6, ZERO treeified** — fixing `nodeToFlow`, `nodeToSymbol` and `moduleInstanceStates` at once. **SOUNDNESS IS THAT NOTHING MAY DEPEND ON THE KEY'S VALUE, AND TWO THINGS COULD:** nothing unpacks `pos`/`end` from a node key (the only unpacking site is `PassTiming`'s site-id pair), and all three containers are `mutableMapOf`, i.e. **LinkedHashMaps iterating in INSERTION order**, so the one iterated `nodeToSymbol` cannot move — a plain `HashMap` would have made this an iteration-ORDER change, the rounds-754/776/778 hazard no output diff sees. **THE ABLATION IS HONEST — 3 OF 5 PINS DISCRIMINATE, AND THE REPAIR IS WORTH MORE THAN THE COUNT:** one pin stayed GREEN with the finalizer stripped because the rich fixture is small enough that `pos xor end` still spreads over it — **it was measuring the FIXTURE, not the packing** — and was rewritten to compute the un-mixed packing LONGHAND inside the pin, so the ablation makes both sides the same expression and the inequality fails by construction; the two injectivity pins cannot discriminate (both packings are bijections) and are kept as INVARIANT GUARDS with **no claim attached** (round 807). **NO WALL-TIME NUMBER IS CLAIMED** — ~1.6% is at the edge of what two batches settle here (rounds 840(c)/858/886) — the claim is the deterministic bucket arithmetic, and `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20** is the EXPECTED control (round 876) that no decision moved. **QUEUED (HASH.1)(b):** the same finalizer for `Relation.packKey` and the checker's other packed id-pair keys, none of which is iterated. **GATES: suite 14,286 -> 14,291 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 5 new `NodeKeyHashSpreadTest` pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 690 classes.** `docs/perf/hash-key-spread.md`._

_**Round 890 (2026-08-11) — (HASH.1)(b): THE PACKED-KEY SWEEP, RUN ON THE **REAL** KEY POPULATIONS RATHER THAN A MODEL — **13 SITES CENSUSED: 2 DEGENERATE AND FIXED, 4 MEASURED ALREADY-FINE, 2 REFUSED** — AND THE MECHANISM IS SHARPER THAN ROUND 889 COULD STATE IT.**_ A THROWAWAY census (an `add(name, key)` at every packed-Long write site, dumped under `--passTiming`, **reverted before a line of the fix was written**) captured the ACTUAL key population of every such container on the compiler profile; `scripts/round890_bucket_model.py` then ran `java.util.HashMap`'s bucket arithmetic over each, as packed and after the golden-ratio finalizer. **THE MECHANISM, NAMED EXACTLY:** `Relation.cache`'s **43,080 real keys collapse onto 18,201 hashes, 1,140 of them in ONE bucket** — because type ids are minted SEQUENTIALLY and the pairs a relation asks about are overwhelmingly **NEIGHBOURS** (an instantiation against its target, a union against a member it was built from), so `a xor b` for `(2k, 2k+1)` is `1` for every k; **21% of queried pairs have `|src-tgt| <= 64`**, and the diagonal is the degenerate limit (`a xor a == 0` puts every `(T,T)` identity query in bucket 0). **FIXED: `Relation.cache` 17,486 of 65,536 buckets / max 1,140 / 27.3% treeified -> 31,532 / max 6 / ZERO**, and `resolvedPropertyTypes` 10,482 keys max 10 / 2.1% -> max 6 / zero — both now route through one `packIdPair`. **A DISTINCTION A MODEL WOULD HAVE GOT WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS:** ranked by total distinct keys `relationComparisonStack` (51,447) looks like the worst offender and `resolvedPropertyTypes` like a rounding error — but the census's `maxLive` column says the stack never holds more than **27 entries at once**, so its table never passes 64 and it CANNOT treeify however bad its hash is, while the grow-only cache does. **A key population is not a map population.** **FOUR SITES ARE ALREADY FINE AND SAYING SO CLOSES THE FAMILY:** `internKey` packs a per-file SALT against an AST position (uncorrelated halves, max bucket 4), `walkMemoKey`'s `* 31` folds already spread it (max 6), and the three M0.3(iii) intern caches are `LongKeyMap`s — **which have applied this very finalizer inside `bucket()` all along**. **TWO ARE REFUSED, ONE ON A SOUNDNESS OBLIGATION:** `SpineDispatch.nodeKey` feeds `[CENSUS]`-only sets, and **`PassTiming.redundantPairNanos` is the repo's one key that is UNPACKED** — a finalizer there would silently mis-attribute the probe's own table, i.e. round 889's first obligation actually biting something. The second obligation was the deciding one here: **four of the seven routed containers are plain `HashMap`/`HashSet`, not `mutableMapOf`**, so had any been iterated this would have been a rounds-754/776/778 program-order change no output diff can see (none is). **NO WALL-TIME NUMBER IS CLAIMED** — the recoverable part is ~0.5% of compile-thread samples ≈ ~30 ms of a ~5.9 s warm rebuild, inside what this box settles; the claim is the deterministic bucket arithmetic on the REAL populations, and `cost_gate.py` +0.00% is the EXPECTED control (round 876). **AND WHAT THE PINS DO NOT GUARD, STATED PLAINLY: they pin `packIdPair` itself — a future site that hand-rolls `(a shl 32) or b` again is invisible to every pin in this repo.** **GATES: suite 14,291 -> 14,295 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 4 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 690 classes.** `docs/perf/hash-key-spread.md` § 5._

Pins RENAMED to output-equivalence pins per round 807. **GATES: suite 14,270 -> 14,274 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 4 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 688 classes.** `docs/perf/tsgo-portability-census.md`._

_**Round 884 (2026-08-11) — (PERF.HW.k): tsc AND tsgo BOTH SOLVE THIS, THE SHAPE GATE IS **SUPERSEDED**, AND THE CLONE ALONE IS **MEASURED INSUFFICIENT** — THE CORPUS NAMED THE THREE TESTS THAT PROVE IT.**_ Both reference compilers are now on the box — tsc's sources at `build/bench/tsc-project-*/src/compiler/` and **TypeScript 7.0.2's Go sources at `typescript-go-repo/`** (tag `typescript/v7.0.2`, gitignored) — so this is read, not recalled. **NEITHER MUTATES BINDER OUTPUT:** tsc clones a non-transient merge target, writes the clone back (`target.set(id, merged)`) and forwards through `mergedSymbols[source.mergeId]`/`getMergedSymbol` — motivated NOT by parallelism (tsc is single-threaded) but by a bound `SourceFile` being reused across `Program` instances in watch/incremental/the language service. **tsgo keeps the clone and changes exactly one thing** — `mergedSymbols map[*ast.Symbol]*ast.Symbol`, per-`Checker`, keyed by IDENTITY, where tsc writes `mergeId` ONTO the shared symbol — so tsgo writes nothing to a binder `Symbol` and N checkers share one bind unconditionally. **THE CLONE WAS IMPLEMENTED AND THE CORPUS FALSIFIED THE CHEAP VERSION:** round 883's own recommendation was to try it WITHOUT the forwarding table and let the corpus decide; it decided — **`extendGenericArray`, `extendGenericArray2` (expected diagnostics, none produced) and `jsExportMemberMergedWithModuleAugmentation` (TS2353 where TS2741 belongs)**. **WHY, AND IT IS THE USEFUL PART: OUR ALIASING IS LOAD-BEARING** — because `globals[name]` IS the binder's object, every reader reaching a symbol through `BinderResult.locals`/`nodeToSymbol` sees the merged declarations FOR FREE; copy it (at adoption or on first write, both break identically) and they get the un-merged original. That is the hole `getMergedSymbol` fills, and the three failures are now the cheapest map of the read sites needing it. **SHIPPED OFF BY DEFAULT** (`MergeClone.enabled = false`; shipped path bit-identical, `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 — with the clone ON, `globals.misses` moves by 5, which is how the flag's reach is visible at all), so the next session lands ONE thing: the id-keyed `IntKeyMap` forwarding table, priced at **0.24-0.35%** against the **-5%** it unlocks. **BENCHES NOW CITE THE SHIPPED RELEASE:** TS 7.0 ships as the `typescript` package itself (`latest = 7.0.2`; `@typescript/native-preview` is the retired preview channel), and 7.0.2 measures **1,774 ms** against the dev build's 1,788 with the same 65 diagnostics — statistically identical, so **the bench series stays continuous**; `bench.yml` defaults `tsgo_spec` to `typescript@7` and installs the two references into SEPARATE npm roots (they share a package NAME now, and the bin name differs by spec). **GATES: suite 14,265 -> 14,270 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 5 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 688 classes; compiler profile byte-identical sequential and `--workers 4 --shareBind` alike.** `docs/parallel-bind-sharing.md` § 2d._

_**Round 883 (2026-08-11) — (PERF.HW.i): ONE SHARED BIND FOR ALL WORKERS — **-5% WARM AT w4, REPLICATED IN TWO BATCHES WITH THE ROTATION REVERSED AND THE TWO ARMS' RUNS COMPLETELY SEPARATE** — AND IT **REFUTES A LAW THIS ARC DOCUMENTED TWO ROUNDS AGO**.**_ Round 882 showed the checker mutates zero binder-owned `Symbol`s on an all-module program, so the N independent binds can become one; `--shareBind` binds ONCE on the caller thread and hands every worker the same `BinderResult`s. **THE PREDICTION WAS WRITTEN DOWN FIRST AND WAS WRONG:** rounds 879/880 concluded concurrent duplicated work costs no wall, so the KDoc said this would buy ZERO from the bind and that any win would be a measurement of the +37% contention term. It won. **batch 1 — on 3,364/3,435 vs off 3,559/3,565 = -162 ms (-4.6%); batch 2 (rotation reversed) — on 3,245/3,395 vs off 3,492/3,557 = -205 ms (-5.8%); all four `on` runs beat all four `off` runs, ranges non-overlapping** (round 858: one sign-consistent batch is not a result). **THE LAW'S REFINEMENT:** "concurrent duplication is free" assumes CORES ARE ABUNDANT, and they are not — four binder threads run beside four JIT threads on 8 cores, so four simultaneous whole-program binds cost more wall than the 515 ms a single bind costs. **OPT-IN because soundness is a property of the PROGRAM**: safe only while nothing merges a program symbol into `globals`, which INV.3(d) gives for an all-module program and not otherwise. Id safety is structural — the shared bind runs on the CALLER thread, below every worker's rebased slice (>= 1e9). Verified by 3 `--listAll` runs at 46 errors / `59d930db…`, a suite equivalence pin, and the corpus. **NEXT: the SHAPE GATE that would make it default must REUSE the checker's own merge predicate, not re-derive it** — a re-derivation wrong in the permissive direction corrupts silently. **GATES: suite 14,267 -> 14,268 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 1 new pin; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 686 classes; the flag defaults OFF.**_

_**MOD.7 (2026-08-10) — OWNER-DIRECTED MODULE SPLIT: THE GRAALVM NATIVE IMAGE STOPS CARRYING A DAEMON IT CAN NEVER BE.** The image was built by the DAEMON module, so a one-shot binary dragged ktor-network, slf4j and `-api` through closed-world analysis for a transport it can never use. A new module `:xemantic-typescript-compiler-cli` holds a LEAN entry point plus the `nativeImage` task — **jvmRuntimeClasspath 23 → 15 modules** (gone: `io.ktor:ktor-network`/`ktor-io`/`ktor-utils` + `-jvm` variants, `org.slf4j:slf4j-api`, `:…-api`). **The entry point is NOT the bare `…compiler.MainKt`, and that is the whole design**: the argument loop ends in `else -> if (!a.startsWith("-")) o.project = a`, so round 840 measured an image built that way answering `--serve --socket /tmp/x.sock` by binding no socket, compiling the SOCKET PATH, emitting 173 files and exiting **0**. `…compiler.cli.MainKt` REFUSES `--serve`/`--daemon`/`--socket` (exit 2) and delegates everything else verbatim. **The three shipped entry points now deliberately DISAGREE**, so round 840(b)'s "pin them equal" test was inverted: the daemon now pins that no code line of its build script mentions `nativeImage`, and the CLI module's `NativeImageEntryPointTest` RESOLVES the class named in the build script and checks for a `public static main` — strictly stronger than the string comparison it succeeds. **`scripts/xtsc` is unaffected and still runs the dispatcher.** Two new passthroughs for the PGO cycle: `-PnativeImageArgs="…"` and `-PnativeImageOutput=name`. **FOUR SINGLE-MISTAKE ABLATIONS, EVERY ARM A DISTINCT RED SET:** delete the refusal → 6 pins (negative controls green); image ← dispatcher → 3; re-add the `-api` dependency → 2; re-add `nativeImage` to the daemon → 1. **SAID RATHER THAN IMPLIED:** kotlinx-serialization does NOT go away (the core parses tsconfig with it) and a pin asserts its PRESENCE so the claim cannot rot; and **nothing native was built or measured** — GraalVM is not on this box, so size/build time/run time are unknown and the task was verified by `--dry-run` only. **GATES: suite 14,234 → 14,252 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** over all five modules = exactly the 18 new pins; warning-clean under a forced recompile; `cost_gate.py`/`huge_methods.py` not required (no core `commonMain` change). `docs/perf/aot-native-image.md` § 0 header._

_**Round 881 (2026-08-11) — (PERF.HW.g): THE BIND-SHARING REFACTOR OPENED (owner-directed, over flipping the `--workers` default), AND STAGE 1's CENSUS SAYS THE BLOCKER IS **406 OBJECTS**, NOT A SYMBOL GRAPH.**_ `Checker.mergeSingleSymbol` touches binder-owned state two ways: its `else` **ADOPTS** — `target[name] = symbol` puts the BINDER's own `Symbol` into `globals`, which is why a bind is CONSUMED by a checker rather than read by one — and its `if` MUTATES in place, where `declarations.addAll` is not idempotent, so a second checker over the same tables appends duplicates. **`--mergeCensus` on the compiler profile: adopts 406, mutates 175 (164 reaching an adopted symbol), declarations appended 175** — against the ~105 k symbols a worker mints, because **INV.3(d) already retired the merge for module-only names**, so every module file's locals are never merged, never adopted, never mutated. Stage 2 (copy on adoption) is therefore a surgical change to one branch. **"Small" is not "easy"**: those 406 are exactly the objects whose identity `globals` hands out, so copying them moves id allocation (drift reshuffles ~350 boundary tests) and collides with INV.3(c)'s merged-instance invariant and `canonicalEnumSymbol`'s frozen verdicts — and the census watches ONE site, so "the checker mutates a binder Symbol nowhere else" is still open. **THE PRICE IS RESTATED SO IT IS NOT RE-DERIVED OPTIMISTICALLY: sharing alone buys ZERO wall** (a per-worker fixed cost hoisted to the serial prefix leaves `F + A/N`); the prize is parallelising the single shared bind (**~386 ms at N=4**) plus an UNMEASURED fraction of the +37% contention term. **TWO TRAPS PAID FOR:** the adopted-id set was first declared ~9,600 lines down and was null through every init pass (CLAUDE.md's Kotlin ordering trap, tell = an impossible NPE on a non-nullable `val`), and the new flag went into the usage text and `ACCEPTED_FLAGS` in the same edit (round 874). **GATES: suite 14,263 -> 14,265 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 2 new pins (the census is REACHED, plus a negative control that it is silent unarmed — round 849's law); `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 681 classes.** `docs/parallel-bind-sharing.md`._

_**Rounds 879-880 (2026-08-11) — (PERF.HW.e) **RETRACTED BEFORE IT WAS BUILT** AND (PERF.HW.f): THE PARALLEL PATH IS NOW DECOMPOSED AND ITS FLOOR NAMED — `wall(N) = 1,447 ms + 7,717/N` WARM, WHERE THE 1,447 IS A **PER-WORKER FIXED COST THAT SHARING CANNOT REDUCE**. GC AND THE JIT ARE MEASURED AND BOTH ACQUITTED. NO SOURCE CHANGE; MEASUREMENTS ONLY.**_ **879 killed its own queue item on arithmetic that should have come first:** concurrent duplicated work does not cost wall (`wall = D + A/N` whether D runs in one worker or in all N), so hoisting the ~400 tail passes to the SERIAL prefix is unchanged at best. Measured with the PassLab (no recompile): the tail is **4,342 ms** of a sequential compile but only **~1.3-2.7 s** of w4 wall (ABBA x2: ON 15,723/16,190, OFF 12,398/14,098), because it is largely partition-scoped ALREADY — with it off, w0 (1 file) drops 1,194 ms while w1 (26 files) drops 3,651, i.e. ~1.1 s duplicated per worker plus ~98 ms per ASSIGNED FILE. **Hoisting would have added 4.3 s of serial time to save ~1.9 s per worker: a net ~2.4 s LOSS.** **Round 878's D = 9.4-11.2 s is RETRACTED** — it fitted across worker LEVELS, where contention differs (the same single file costs 13,503 ms at w4 and 15,885 at w6), so the slope absorbed the contention into the intercept; within a level it is ~3.7 s. **880 then closed the remaining question.** GC: 734 ms at w1 / 1,338 at w4 and **heap-INSENSITIVE** (`-Xmx4g` 14,896 vs `-Xmx8g` **14,865 ms**). JIT/core saturation — the leading hypothesis, since a "single-threaded" run already takes ~4.17 of 8 cores — is refuted because the effect PERSISTS WARM: w0 **3,892 ms** for ONE file against 3,157-3,310 for 25-26 files. The warm fit gives **r = 0.776 ms/k-char against a sequential 0.565 (+37% contention, not 50%)** and **fixed = 1,447 ms = re-bind 515 + ~930 ms program-wide**. **CONSEQUENCE FOR `docs/parallel-caching.md` PHASE 1: resolving the lib slice once and freezing it buys ZERO WALL** — a per-worker fixed cost hoisted to the prefix leaves `F + A/N`, identical. N -> infinity gives a **4.35x ceiling** contention keeps out of reach. **So the concurrency arc is at its useful limit on this program at warm w4 = 1.95x**; what remains are not scheduling changes — an immutable-binder-output refactor, or making the ~930 ms program-wide, one pass at a time._

_**Round 878 (2026-08-11) — (PERF.HW.d): THE PER-WORKER CENSUS, AND IT **REFUTES ROUND 877's MODEL OF WHAT IS LEFT**. AT w4 THE WORKERS ARE BALANCED TO **101%** AND A WORKER ASSIGNED **ONE FILE COSTS THE SAME AS ONE ASSIGNED 26** — SO THE REMAINING LIMITER IS NOT THE PARTITION AT ALL BUT **DUPLICATED, ASSIGNMENT-INDEPENDENT WORK: D ~ 9.4-11.2 s PER WORKER AGAINST A ~24.6 s SEQUENTIAL COMPILE, i.e. EACH WORKER REDOES 40-45% OF THE WHOLE COMPILE.**_ Round 877 was right about its fix and wrong about the residue — the balance it bought is real (warm w4 1.531x -> 1.947x) and the ceiling it computed from source lengths was the wrong ceiling. `FrontEnd.workerNanos`/`workerFiles`/`workerChars` (three stores per WORKER, index `w` written by worker `w` alone, read only after every join — race-free by construction, not by luck) are printed by `--frontEnd`. **THE TABLE:** w2 15,530/15,512 ms = **100%**; w4 13,503/13,164/13,168/13,160 = **101%**, w0 holding `checker.ts` ALONE against 26 files each elsewhere; w6 15,885 vs ~11,650 = **128%**, the one level where the indivisible giant does bind. **FITTING `worker = D + k x assigned chars` ACROSS LEVELS GIVES D = 9.4-11.2 s** — the full re-bind plus the ~318 program-wide collectors every checker runs regardless of `assignedFileNames` — **which caps file-level parallelism near 2.4x NO MATTER HOW MANY CORES, and is exactly why the ladder plateaus**. Round 877's 3.16x was the ceiling of the ASSIGNMENT, and the assignment is not what is left. **A SECOND MEASURED REASON NOT TO SPEND MORE WORKERS: the SAME single file costs 13,503 ms at w4 and 15,885 ms at w6** — past 4, workers contend on 8 cores and make the CRITICAL worker slower, which no partition can answer. RSS agrees: w1 1,393 MB, w4 1,468 MB (**+5%**), w6 2,445 MB (+75%). **THE EMIT BLIND SPOT IS CLOSED FOR THE PARALLEL PATH FOR THE FIRST TIME** — CLAUDE.md warns that every instrument here is `--noEmit`, and every `--workers` capture ever taken was too: a full `--outDir` build at w1 and w4 produces **78 files each and `diff -r` is clean**. **NEXT, AND NOW PRICED RATHER THAN ARGUED:** `docs/parallel-caching.md`'s Phase 1 — hoist the assignment-independent Tier-1 work into the single-threaded prefix and share it read-only — is worth up to D, the largest single number in the parallel path. **GATES: suite 14,261 -> 14,263 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 2 new census pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 679 classes.**_

_**Round 877 (2026-08-11) — (PERF.HW.c): THE `--workers` FILE PARTITION WAS `i % workers == w` OVER FILES SPANNING THREE ORDERS OF MAGNITUDE, AND ON THE COMPILER PROFILE **`checker.ts` ALONE IS 31.6% OF THE PROGRAM** — SO THE PARTITION, BEFORE ANY CHECKER WORK, CAPPED THE ACHIEVABLE SPEEDUP AT 2.10x / 2.63x / 2.41x AT 4 / 6 / 8 WORKERS. LONGEST-PROCESSING-TIME-FIRST TAKES WARM w4 **4,112 -> 3,232 ms (1.531x -> 1.947x)** AND COLD w4 **19,980 -> 15,438 ms (-22.7%)**.**_ **THE CENSUS FIRST (round 801's order), and it is arithmetic, not a profile:** the heaviest round-robin bucket carried **1.90x / 2.28x / 3.32x** the mean at w4/w6/w8 — the wall of a parallel phase is the SLOWEST worker, so those numbers ARE the ceiling, and the 3.32x at w8 is why 8 workers came out worse than 6 in every ladder ever taken here. LPT (files in descending size, each to the currently lightest bucket; within 4/3 of optimal for any input) takes the ceiling to **3.16x at every level >= 4** — and that number is then the REAL ceiling of file-level parallelism on this program, because one file being 31.6% of the input means no assignment whatever can beat 1/0.316. **THE OPTIMUM MOVED**: warm w6 was the best level under round-robin (1.636x) and is now WORSE than w4 (1.809x vs **1.947x**) — balancing removed the reason to spend more workers, so the recommended level is 4 again, this time for a measured reason. **THE COLD ARM MOVED TOO, WHICH ROUND 876's DID NOT**: w2 21,254 -> 17,004, w4 19,980 -> **15,438**, w8 23,562 -> 20,022 ms — a 23% cold delta is far outside round 858's batch drift, and unlike a JIT-warming effect a partition change cannot be paid back by the ramp. **THE GAP TO tsgo, MEASURED IN ONE SESSION ON ONE BOX** (tsgo 7.0.0-dev, same profile, median of 5 cold runs = **1,740 ms**): warm sequential **3.62x** -> w4 round-robin **2.36x** -> w4 balanced **1.86x**. Stated with its caveat: tsgo pays full process startup inside its 1,740 ms while xtsc's 3,232 ms is a warm in-JVM rebuild, so the comparison already flatters xtsc. **SOURCE LENGTH IS A PROXY FOR CHECKING COST AND IS SAID TO BE ONE** — it is used because it is exact, free, and available BEFORE any checking, which a true cost measure is not; a wrong proxy costs balance, never correctness. **THE ORDER IS TOTAL ON PURPOSE** (size desc, ties on `fileName`): a partition that depended on iteration order would make diagnostics depend on it, and byte-identical output is this project's entire verification method. **CORRECTNESS: 10 `--listAll` runs at w1/w2/w4/w8 — all 46 errors, all digest `59d930db…`**, identical to both pre-change arms. **GATES: suite 14,255 -> 14,261 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 6 new `BalancedFilePartitionTest` pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 679 classes.**_

_**Round 876 (2026-08-11) — (PERF.HW.b): UNDER `--workers N` THE CORE WAS BINDING THE WHOLE PROGRAM ON ITS **SEQUENTIAL PREFIX** AND THROWING THE RESULT AWAY. WARM, THAT IS A **515 ms** ROW GOING TO **0**, AND THE FIRST WARM PARALLEL NUMBER THIS PROJECT HAS: w4 **6,294 -> 4,112 ms = 1.531x**, AGAINST THE **1.361x** ROUND 826 MEASURED COLD.**_ `cpcBindAndCheck` bound every file into `binderResults` before branching, and that value is read by the SEQUENTIAL branch and by nobody else — each worker binds the whole program again for itself, as it must, because `Checker` init mutates the symbols it is given. So a parallel compile paid one whole-program `Binder.bind` straight into Amdahl's R, the one term worker count cannot buy back. **THE DEFECT WAS INVISIBLE TO EVERY GATE IN THIS REPO AND THAT IS THE GENERAL LESSON**: a redundant computation whose result is DROPPED changes no diagnostic, no emitted byte and no `cost_gate.py` counter — only the wall, where 515 ms sits under the run-to-run spread of a parallel compile. Hence the pin is a COUNT, `FrontEnd.sequentialFileBinds`, written only from the caller thread and asserted exactly (2 sequential, **0** parallel), never a timing ratio (round 868). **THE COLD ARM MEASURED NOTHING AND IS REPORTED, NOT BURIED:** w4 medians 19,518 -> 19,980 ms across two non-interleaved batches, i.e. no gain and inside round 858's batch drift — the plausible mechanism is that the discarded bind was also WARMING the binder for the workers, which is (JIT.1) exactly: a cold A/B is structurally blind to a warm-path effect and here it is worse than blind. **THE INSTRUMENT WAS THE UNBLOCKER AND IS THE ROUND'S DURABLE HALF:** every `--workers` number this project has recorded (rounds 740/824/826) is COLD and every warm number is SEQUENTIAL, so the two regimes had **never met**; `BenchMain` gained a `workers<N>` 6th argument and `ab-warm.sh` a `WORKERS` knob, both set ONCE per process because round 867's law is sharper for worker levels than for anything it was written about — two levels in one JVM share every compiled method in the binder and the checker. **CORRECTNESS: 20 `--listAll` runs, both arms, w1/w2/w4/w8 — all 46 errors, all one digest `59d930db…`**, the documented `grep 'error TS' | sort` lineage. **GATES: suite 14,252 -> 14,255 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 3 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters** (expected and itself a control — the gate runs the SEQUENTIAL path, where this change is a strict no-op); `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit**, 677 classes. **NOT MEASURED, AND NOT CLAIMED: the paired warm A/B against the pre-change binary** — what is measured is the attribution (a 515 ms serial row going to zero) plus the two warm medians, which is not a paired delta._

_**Round 875 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.22): THE INV.4 REACH MACHINERY, CENSUSED AS ONE POPULATION AND **PRICED NEGATIVE IN EVERY MECHANISM IT CONTAINS**. Round 874 § 29 handed over ~338 ms = 5.5% of a warm rebuild spread over 43 classifiers whose largest is 0.86%, as ONE DESIGN QUESTION: can the pull-based memoized ancestor climb be shared, hoisted, or replaced by state the spine already has? **It cannot, and the three candidate answers are now measured rather than argued.**_ **THE CENSUS FIRST (round 801's order), and it is the instrument § 29 commissioned:** `ReachCensus` (`--reachCensus`, tier `reach`) counts consults / memo hits / ascents / EDGE EVALUATIONS per classifier, injected at the two anchors every classifier carries verbatim by a script that also GENERATES the id table so the two cannot drift. Per rebuild: **1,909,715 consultations, 1,740,677 ascents, 3,324,977 edge evaluations, and a memo that answers 8%** — with **23 of the 43 classifiers at exactly 0%**, which is not a defect: a classifier is consulted at most ONCE PER NODE, so the memo can never answer the query it was written for and its only job is to TERMINATE AN ASCENT (which it does — folds/consult is 1.74, not the tree depth). **THE INSTRUMENT WAS WRONG FIRST AND A PIN SAID SO:** `folds >= misses` failed on its first run against the five classifiers whose ascent evaluates the edge and pushes only when it CONTINUES, where `chain.size` is one short per ascent — the wrong counter read 2,956,401 edge evaluations, the right one 3,324,977. **(a) PUSH-BASED IS NEGATIVE BY ARITHMETIC, 11.1x:** a push scheme must compute a status for every classifier at every node it descends through (43 x 856,962 = **36.9 M** edge evaluations) because it cannot know which handler will ask, where the pull scheme performs **3.32 M** — 16 of the 43 are consulted under 1,000 times a rebuild and one is consulted ONCE; at 13.3 ns that is **+447 ms on a family that costs 361**. **(c) THE TABLESWITCH CONVERSION IS THE ONE A STATIC READING WOULD HAVE COMMITTED AN ARC TO, AND IT IS DEAD:** every edge predicate is a `when (parent) { is X -> }` LINEAR `instanceof` chain of 41-119 arms, `kindId` exists for exactly this (M0.2) and round 803 split `forEachChild` on that key for -3.93% — but `--reachAmp N` (r EXTRA evaluations of the SAME edge under ONE pair, two r values cancelling the boundary, round 759) measures **13.3 ns per evaluation**, so the ENTIRE compute of every edge predicate in the family is **3.32 M x 13.3 ns = 44 ms = 0.67%** — and **106 arms costs the SAME as 49** (Ce 11.5/13.3/14.7, Fp 10.6/13.5/13.9/13.2 across two processes), so the slope in arms is indistinguishable from zero and a tableswitch cannot take most of even that. **The fit's own falsifier did its job**: seven of eight pairs imply a boundary of 73-132 ns, inside round 850's independently measured 97-202 ns, and the eighth implies **1.7 ns** — physically impossible, so that draw is discarded by a criterion unrelated to the answer it would have given. **(b) PACKING THE 43 PER-FILE nodeId-KEYED MEMOS** is the only survivor and is still **<= ~0.8%** (2.23 classifiers per node, so a transposition lets that many consultations share a cache line; it also removes 36.9 MB of ByteArray zeroed per rebuild) — QUEUED with that number attached, not taken, because it rewrites the memo access of all 43 classifiers and every one decides whether a diagnostic is CONSIDERED. **A5 REDDENED NOTHING ON ITS FIRST RUN** — it perturbed a classifier whose pass emits nothing on the fixture, so the equivalence pins were BLIND (round 813 on schedule); the fixture now carries a deliberate TS2588 and TS2693 and the pins assert those codes are PRESENT, since two empty lists agree. **GATES: suite 14,220 -> 14,229 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** over all four modules = exactly the 9 new pins; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit**, 677 classes; SEVEN single-mistake ablations, every arm reddening a distinct set (A5/A6 shared by design, A6 existing to show the equivalence pins are not a property of one classifier), one pin reported as UN-ABLATED. `docs/perf/reach-machinery.md`._

_**Round 874 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.21): THE WARM LEAF PROFILE RE-TAKEN A THIRD TIME. BY ROWS THE VEIN IS EXHAUSTED — NOTHING NEW REPLICATES ABOVE 1% AND ROWS 21-60 ARE ALL 19-32 ms. BY **FAMILIES** IT IS NOT, AND THAT READING IS THE ROUND'S REAL INSTRUMENT: THE LARGEST THING IN THE WARM COMPILE IS THE INV.4 MIGRATION'S OWN **REACH MACHINERY — 458.8 ms = 6.95% OVER 66 OWNERS** OF WHICH THE BIGGEST IS 0.57%. ITS ONE COHERENT SUB-FAMILY IS TAKEN: THE TAV PASS RUNS **381,670 TIMES TO EMIT ZERO DIAGNOSTICS**, 99% OF WHAT IT REACHES CANNOT EMIT, AND A PER-FILE NAME-CANDIDATE GATE TAKES ITS CONTROLLED ROW **156-159 -> 34-43 ms = 121 ms = 1.84%**.**_ **VALIDITY FIRST, AND ALL FOUR PRIOR FIXES APPEAR AS FALLEN ROWS:** `checkSpine` INCLUSIVE **74.09/74.05%** against the known ~74%; `computeTypeParamInfo` **99.9 -> 11.7 ms** (round 870 predicted a 15 ms residue); the round-868 star walks and round-869 copy pushers still 0.00%. **Round 871's parse cache needed a DIFFERENT reading and is the sharpest of the four** — the crawl parses on worker threads, which this instrument filters out by design, so counted over ALL threads samples whose stack contains `Parser` go **251/285 -> 265/282 -> 0/0**, not one in 16,413. **THE ROW READING SAYS "DONE" AND WOULD HAVE MISSED THE ROUND:** `ciaMutualFnDecls$resolve` fails replication for the SECOND round running (1.56% vs 0.48% on one binary), `objectLiteralSatisfiesAugmentationMergedInterface` has sat at 0.70%/46 ms through three rounds — so the table was aggregated by MECHANISM, which is what round 868's (C1) had been (three rows that were one barrel search, caught only because they were adjacent and shared a prefix). **THE CENSUS (round 801's order):** 381,670 dispatches at 44.5% of all nodes, **695,014** reach hops, **798,020** parent hops each testing seven node classes, **467,085** chain probes — and `emitted 0`; **151,137 of 152,636 reached identifiers (99.0%) are INERT**, their name in no visible typeOnly/nsOnly set and not a type keyword, and this pass owns only TS2693/TS2708 which both require one. **`emitted 0` is also round 793 arriving on schedule** — the OFF arm's obvious falsifier (the error count moves) is DEAD on the very profile the prize is measured on. **THE WALL IS DISCARDED, NOT QUOTED:** `plain` vs `tavoff` ABBA x4 read 124 ms with a per-arm sd of **3.0%** and **2 of 4 pairs the wrong way** (round 840(c)). The price is a CONTROLLED ROW — one pair per dispatched identifier, the gate returning early INSIDE it, both arms in ONE binary (`--tavGateOff`, round 795), so the boundary count is identical and cancels: **159 -> 38** and **156 -> 34 ms** in two processes, **121 and 122 ms, 0.8% apart**, against the profile's independent 139.6 ms INCLUSIVE. **WHAT LANDED:** `spineTavCandidates`, a deliberate SUPERSET of the four sources exhaustive over `tavBuildLevel` — the eager file root, `TYPE_ONLY_KEYWORDS` (folded in so the gate is ONE probe), type-PARAMETER names and a module block's interface/type-alias/namespace names — the last two collected by the spine at `TypeParameter`/`ModuleBlock` ENTER, **keyed on the node KIND so completeness is structural**, with `tavModuleLevel`'s filters deliberately NOT reproduced because a wider gate can only let more through. Row CONTROLLED: dispatches identical, **gate refused 365,784 = 95.8%**, hops 695,014 -> 24,058 and 798,020 -> 9,773, probes 467,085 -> 2,720. **THE GRID NEEDED ITS OWN CONTROL for the same reason:** two do-nothing arms would agree trivially on a pass that emits nothing, so both arms run with `--frontEnd` and every profile records that the gated arm refused **365,784-562,693** and the ungated arm **0**, the script FAILING if a gated arm refuses under 1,000 — **added=0 removed=0 on all eight, both directions**. **A CASCADE WORTH ITS GOTCHA:** the first suite run CRASHED the test JVM after 1,237 tests with ten failures in six unrelated classes including `ArrayIndexOutOfBounds` inside census `ArrayList`s — one cause, two undocumented CLI flags failing `CliModeRestoreTest` BEFORE its `ledger.restore()`, leaving every swept mode armed for the rest of the JVM. **GATES: suite 14,205 -> 14,220 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** over all four modules = exactly the 15 new pins; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit**, 672 classes; EIGHT single-mistake ablations, every arm reddening a DISTINCT set and A8's being exactly the union of A3-A6's; two pins recorded as UN-ABLATED because every arm narrows the gate and a narrower gate can only lose an emission, never invent one. **THE ARC'S CLOSING STATEMENT is in the doc (§ 29):** three takes, four fixes, **~800 ms off a warm rebuild that started at ~7.8 s**, and the successor is no longer candidate-hunting — the remaining **~338 ms** of reach machinery is ONE design question. `docs/perf/warm-leaf-profile.md` §§ 21-29._

_**Round 873 (2026-08-09) — (SERVE.2): THE FIRST DELIBERATE SWEEP OF THE CLI-vs-DAEMON ANSWER BOUNDARY — 31 CELLS, 45 INVOCATION PAIRS. THE WHOLE **SEQUENCE** AXIS WAS GREEN ON THE FIRST RUN; THE FOUR CELLS THAT WERE NOT ARE ONE ROOT CAUSE — **A REQUEST NEVER SAID WHERE IT WAS TYPED** — PLUS A HANG THE SWEEP TRIPPED OVER: `xtsc /nonexistent` CRAWLS THE WHOLE FILESYSTEM, AND UNDER `--serve` THAT WEDGES THE DAEMON FOR GOOD.**_ Two divergences had surfaced by accident and neither by a test (round 848's fifteen process-global mode flags; round 872's daemon-served compile exiting 0 where the CLI exits 1), so the axes were enumerated rather than guessed: outcome x invocation FORM x observable (exit code, stdout, stderr, and the whole emitted TREE by sha256), plus the axis a one-shot CLI structurally cannot have — repeat, A-B-A, mode-then-plain, fail-then-pass, refused-then-normal, **edit-then-request**. **THE DEFECT:** `CompileRequest` carried `args` and NOTHING ELSE while the daemon runs elsewhere and **a JVM cannot change its own cwd**, so both clients had grown the same unfixable heuristic (*rewrite an argument that names something existing here* — which cannot tell a project path from the `4` in `--workers 4`, and never sees the path a user did not type). Measured: **`xtsc --daemon --noEmit` in a project full of errors compiled the DAEMON's own project and exited 0**, and **`--outDir out` wrote the user's `a.js` into the daemon's directory**. Fixed by `CompileRequest.workingDirectory` (**protocol version 2** — the bump is load-bearing, a v1 daemon would answer a v2 request against its own cwd) installed as `SystemVfs.workingDirectory` for one request, install-and-restore on the one compile thread exactly like round 848's ledger; that is THE funnel, since `ProjectCompiler.build` absolutizes both the project path and a `--outDir` override through it. Both clients' rewriting is DELETED and the native client is finally the strict pass-through its KDoc claimed. **THE HANG:** an unreadable config fell back to the everything-glob with `configDir = dirname` of a path nobody confirmed = `/` — **over 30 minutes of CPU** walking the filesystem, and a `--serve` daemon serves sequentially on one thread, so it never recovers and every later client blocks with no timeout. A config that does not EXIST now includes nothing; one that exists and does not PARSE keeps the default. **THE DRIVER CANNOT BE BLIND:** a comparator `--selftest` that refuses to sweep if it misses a synthetic divergence; a **served-by-the-daemon** control per step (the server's own count +1 AND no in-process fallback message), without which a dead daemon would read as parity; ERROR-not-PASS on any raise or timeout, with a daemon restart so one wedge cannot void the rest; the daemon runs in a DECOY project; and the *this cell may differ* marker was **deleted** after the ablation caught it absorbing an unrelated divergence. **POSITIVE CONTROL, one mistake at a time:** A1 (round 872's exit-code bug) reddens **20 of 31**, A2 (this round's fix removed) **7 of 31** — exactly the cwd-dependent cells — and A3 (a stale-answer daemon) **2 of 31**, the edit cells, which is the sequence axis's own uniquely-its-own failure. **GATES: suite 14,188 -> 14,205 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** over all four modules = exactly the 17 new pins; core `commonMain` DID change so both ran — `cost_gate.py` **every counter +0.00%** (46 errors / 78 files) and `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit**; no daemon left running. `docs/serve-parity.md`._

_**Round 872 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.20): THE **279 ms** CLIENT JVM A REQUEST WAITS ON IS **7.0 ms** OF KOTLIN/NATIVE — AND THE "THIN CLIENT" WAS NEVER ABOUT THE COMPILER JAR. ON THE 3-FILE PROJECT AN EDITOR ACTUALLY GENERATES, A REQUEST GOES **369 -> 105 ms**; SWAPPING THE ARMS FOUND A CI FALSE-GREEN AND A DAEMON THE SUITE WAS LEAVING RUNNING.**_ Round 857's rule first — the `-client` module has shipped since MOD.4 with a jvm target, opt-in `linuxX64`/`macosArm64` executables and a GraalVM task, and **nothing had ever invoked any of it** (its `clientLib` staging is not wired into `assemble`, unlike the daemon's `xtscLib`); the K/N client links in **1m23s** to 3.26 MB, and the GraalVM arm cannot be built on this box. **THE ARM TABLE**, one warm daemon, round 871's constant-time refused request so the compile cancels out, 12 reps interleaved and rotated, `EPOCHREALTIME` not `date +%s%N` (the fast arms are single-digit ms and `date` is a fork+exec per timestamp): fork+exec floor **0.9**, **native 7.0**, thin JVM+AOT **105.2**, fat JVM+AOT **277.1**, thin JVM **278.1**, JVM dispatcher **286.9 ms** — round 871's 279 reproduced. **THE PREDICTION THAT FAILED: "thin client" named the DEPENDENCY EDGE, and the edge is worth 3%** — class loading is lazy, so the dispatcher never touches the 5.6 MB compiler jar when the daemon answers; the cost is the JVM. What the edge is worth is that it makes a NATIVE binary affordable, and that is the other 97%. Both AOT arms verified loading from the cache (`-Xlog:class+load`, 1,702 / 2,657 classes), so the fat arm's ~0 gain is a result and not an uncached run (round 842). **END TO END, both arms through the LAUNCHER**: 3-file project **369.2 -> 105.3 ms** (72-74% of the wait), compiler profile 7,195 -> 6,881 ms — **inside its own +-5% spread and NOT claimed as a measured effect**; the FIXED cost is what was measured, and 279 ms of 7,150 is 3.9% by arithmetic. Same errors and same digest on both (`8ccb2942`, `4090b73e`). **SHIPPED:** `scripts/xtsc` routes a `--daemon` request through the native client, **with a JVM FALLBACK on `XTSC_CLIENT_UNAVAILABLE` (3)** — the code documented to mean *the request never ran*, never *it ran and found errors*, which is what makes re-running it safe; a fresh checkout, a `clean` or any platform with no binary still gets a working `xtsc`. **TWO DEFECTS FELL OUT AND ARE THE REAL YIELD:** (1) a `--daemon` compile **served by a daemon** exited **0** on a failing project where the one-shot CLI and the in-process fallback both exit 1 — `runAsClient` propagated `XTSC_REFUSED` and dropped every other code, so `xtsc`'s answer again depended on whether a daemon happened to be running and CI read a failing compile as a pass; `ExitCodeParityTest` pins the code the server puts IN the response, which was always right, and the defect was one layer up. (2) The arm's first build let the client **auto-spawn** a daemon — `AotCacheGuardTest` failed on the missing "no compile server on" message and **a daemon was still running after the suite**; `--no-spawn` makes the swap a latency change and nothing else. **GATES: suite 14,165 -> 14,188 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** over all four modules = exactly the 23 new pins; `cost_gate.py` and `huge_methods.py` **not required and not run** (nothing in core's `commonMain` changed); piped invocation returns on the shipped path and on the client's own spawn path (MOD.5's `dup2`); 8-arm single-mistake ablation, every arm with its own failing set. `docs/perf/warm-serve-request-attribution.md` § 10._


_**Round 871 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.19): THE FIRST MEASUREMENT IN THIS ARC TAKEN THROUGH THE ARTIFACT THAT SHIPS. A `--serve` REQUEST PAYS **2 ms** OVER AN IN-PROCESS REBUILD; THE **279 ms** A CLIENT ADDITIONALLY WAITS IS A FRESH CLIENT JVM; AND WHAT A REQUEST RE-DID ACROSS REQUESTS WAS **78 READS + 78 PARSES**, IDENTICALLY FOR AN IDENTICAL REQUEST AND A ONE-FILE-CHANGED ONE — THE PARSE HALF IS NOW SHARED, **133 ms = 1.9%**, AND THE EDITOR WORKLOAD PARSES **ONE FILE PER EDIT** INSTEAD OF 78.**_ Every warm number rounds 843-870 produced came from `BenchMain`, an IN-PROCESS rebuild; round 843 compared the two TOTALS and called it agreement, before this arc removed ~15% of the rebuild. Three NESTED brackets the shipping binary already prints (client wall > server `elapsedMs` > the compiler's own `time:`) put **everything `runCli` does around the build** — argument parse, the round-848 mode ledger, diagnostic formatting, stdout capture, JSON encode — at **1-3 ms warm**; the client side, ISOLATED with a request the server refuses in constant time rather than differenced, is **279 ms** of which the bash launcher is **9 ms**, i.e. **3.9% of the 7.15 s a user waits, paid outside the server** and exactly what the native thin client exists to remove. **THE PER-REQUEST CENSUS** (`--frontEnd` from inside a request, 8 warm draws, 2 daemon processes): config **3 ms**, crawl WALL **153 ms (2.2%)**, bind **385 ms (5.5%)**, check **6,450 ms (92%)** — and on EVERY request `files read: 78   core parse loop: 78 reused / 0 fresh`, i.e. **INV.1(e) reusing WITHIN the request while the crawl parses all 78 again** (`preParsed` is a `build()` local; only the LIB parses have crossed requests, since M2.1(c)). **The one-file-changed ladder produced IDENTICAL counters** — the daemon workload is 77/78 unchanged and the compiler was treating it as 0/78. **PRICED BY AMPLIFICATION**, because the crawl is a concurrent pipeline whose read+parse CPU sums to 6-9x its wall and which carries a fixed floor: new `--parseAmp N`, two processes, rotations `0,1,2,3,3,2,1,0` and `3,2,1,0,0,1,2,3` -> **crawl(r) = 180 + 128.3r** and **171 + 137.7r**, one parse round **133 ms**, floor **33-52 ms**, arithmetic falsifier exact on every draw. **WHAT LANDED:** `CrawlParseCache` keys on **content + ParserFlags** — the bytes are in hand because the crawl reads every file anyway, so **staleness is not expressible**; no mtime, no size, no stat. Sharing a tree rests on three properties none of them new (the parse is pure in `(source, fileName, flags)`, the AST is written only by `indexSourceFile`, and **`RealLibSnapshots` has done exactly this since M2.1(c)** — the parse is shared, the BIND is not). Memory bounded at one entry per path, MEASURED at **78 paths held** over a 7-edit ladder; `lookup` read-only on the crawl workers, `store` only in the single-threaded post-frontier fold (round 825). **CAPTURED 122-133 ms by three instruments** (blocked-arm controlled row 138 -> 14 ms, pre-change binary 153 -> 24, the amplifier 133) — **and a trap: a single OFF request inside a run of ON ones reads 896 ms**, the parser having gone cold, so these arms must be BLOCKED and not interleaved. **THE GRID IS A DAEMON GRID ON PURPOSE** (a one-shot CLI builds once and can never register a hit): the 8 profiles each compiled twice through ONE daemon, all-MISS vs all-HIT, **added=0 removed=0 on all eight**; cross-PROJECT reuse fell out and was observed. **PINS + ABLATION:** `CrawlParseCacheTest` (15), 7 single-mistake arms, all uniquely their own after a re-cut — **A1 and A3 initially shared a red set** (the "byte difference" pin used a same-LENGTH edit) and **A1 reddened no EDIT pin at all, which is a finding about the compiler: the core re-checks content at `ParsedSource`, so a mis-keyed hit degrades to a redundant parse and a CORRECT type-check — the place with no second gate is the CRAWL, whose stale `moduleSpecifiers` lose a whole FILE**; an 11th pin was cut for exactly that and A1 now reddens it. **GATES: suite 14,150 -> 14,165 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**, `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters** twice, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit**, 8-profile daemon grid added=0 removed=0, 33 ladder requests over 3 daemon processes all 46 errors / digest `84bbe7f0`. `docs/perf/warm-serve-request-attribution.md`._

_**Round 869 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.16): THE PER-SCOPE WHOLE-MAP COPY, PRICED — **205 ms = 2.80%** OF A WARM REBUILD FOR THE WHOLE FAMILY, AND THE TWO FAMILIES WHERE THE FALSE-POSITIVE HAZARD IS STRUCTURALLY ABSENT (**129.7 ms = 1.74%**) NOW COPY NOTHING.** Census first (round 801): 159,630 scope pushes copy **5,834,632 entries to serve 89,307 writes = 1.5%** — a copy costs O(size), an undo log O(writes). Priced by AMPLIFICATION with no timestamp pair anywhere (round 759 — at a mean copy of 16-114 entries one warm boundary, 97-202 ns, would BE the measurement), ABBA-rotated because the first instrumented rebuild in a process is worth up to 15%; falsifier ARITHMETIC and exact on all 24 rebuilds. `AnnScopeStack` replaces the `spineOs`/`spinePd` annotation copies with one live map + an undo log replayed in REVERSE: **3,088,334 entries -> 34,454 undo records over an IDENTICAL population** (pushes and writes match to the unit), and the amplifier re-run on the after-arm finds nothing left to amplify. The other four families are `currentLocalTypes` and were deliberately left alone. **The whole-rebuild `ab-warm.sh` A/B is DISCARDED, not quoted**: batch 1 -4.42% 2/2, batch 2 -1.04% 1/2 NOISE-DOMINATED — round 840(c) exactly. (C3) `ArrayList.clear` left explicitly UNPRICED (round 623's bias class). Suite **14,139 / 0 / 3**; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20; grid both arms rebuilt, added=0 removed=0 on all eight. Pins `AnnScopeStackTest` (11), NINE single-mistake ablations, every pin reddened by at least one arm._

_**Round 868 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.15): THE FIRST **LEAF-LEVEL** WARM PROFILE OF THIS COMPILER, AND ITS BEST NEW CANDIDATE LANDED — THE `export *` BARREL WALKS GO **672 -> 91 ms** WARM (9.0% -> 1.2% of a warm rebuild). PLUS THE FLAKY `PostCheckerPartitionTest` RE-CUT AS A DETERMINISTIC ORDERING.**_ Every instrument this arc owns is a PASS or SECTION probe and finds only cost somebody thought to bracket — which is why rounds 860-864's four wins were all wrongly-shaped work INSIDE a named region, and why anything diffuse is invisible to all of them. A JFR `ExecutionSample` profile of a steady-state `BenchMain` window (two processes, 8,026/8,010 compile-thread samples) is the complement, and it pays for that by being unable to PRICE anything: round 623 eliminated a 5.3%-of-samples leaf here and measured **-0.3%**. So its output is a CANDIDATE LIST, said in the doc's headline. **THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN TRAP COST THE FIRST PASS: `jfr print` TRUNCATES EVERY STACK TO ITS TOP 5 FRAMES, silently, and `scripts/aggregate_jfr.py` does not pass `--stack-depth`** — its INCLUSIVE table is inclusive-within-five-frames, and `checkSpine` read 15.6% against this arc's 74%. Two more: ~3% of samples are the JFR thread and the crawl's workers, and **leaf attribution is NOT stable across processes** (`HashMap.getNode` 9.66% vs 3.70% on the SAME binary — C2 inlined it in the second), so every table is by nearest non-stdlib OWNER. **SHAPE: 26-27% of warm samples are inside a hash map/set, 6% string, 4% list — and `java.util.regex` is 0.0%, not one sample in 16,036**, confirming round 863's exhaustion claim with an instrument not built to look for it; `computeLineStarts` (round 623's refuted lever) is not in the top 200 and `LinkedHashMap.afterNodeInsertion` (round 483) is ONE sample. **THE CANDIDATE TAKEN, AFTER DISCARDING EVERY ALREADY-ATTRIBUTED ROW:** four mutually recursive `export *` walks, **5.45% self / 7.8% inclusive**, entered from `getCalleeType` / `importedTopLevelVarAnnotationType` / `checkCircularInferenceImplicitAny`. **PRICED INDEPENDENTLY** by a re-entrancy-counted counter+span census (`FrontEnd.STAR`) at **672 ms = 9.0% of a warm rebuild** — 8,754 outermost walks, 290,117 file visits, **25,291,521 top-level statements scanned (2,889 per question asked)**, 28% of walks answering nothing; the profile's own inclusive share is 570-585 ms, two instruments agreeing within 15%. **THE FIX IS AN INDEX** (`StarExportIndex`: exported functions by name, exported variables first-wins, exported interface names, descendable re-export edges with targets resolved — computed once per file), and **the row comparison is CONTROLLED under round 793's law because it moves no boundary and no population: walks 8,754, visits 290,117, answered 6,298, null 2,456 are IDENTICAL on both arms and only the scan width moves, 25,291,521 -> 638,464 (39.6x)**. Row **672 -> 91 ms**; `ab-warm.sh` corroborates at **-5.58% and -4.15%, B wins 4/4** over two batches (quoted as corroboration, not as the number — per-arm sd 1.2-1.9% on n=2, said out loud). **THE FLAKY PIN:** `residue * 4 < post` over a **272 us** region is a coin flip, so `FrontEnd.close` now records `firstAt`/`lastAt` and the partition is asserted STRUCTURALLY (records once, `nanos == lastAt - firstAt`, nested, ordered, disjoint) — more on structure, less on one thing (an unattributed GAP), both said in the KDoc. **6/6 consecutive FULL-SUITE runs green.** **PINS + ABLATION:** `StarExportIndexTest` (8), seven single-mistake ablations, **seven discriminated**, one pin recorded as UN-ablatable (type-enforced) rather than claimed; A3's first form was a COMPILE ERROR printing `ran 0, failed 0` — round 808's trap, re-cut. **GATES: suite 14,120 -> 14,128 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**, `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters** (twice), `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, **8-profile grid added=0 removed=0 on all eight with BOTH arms rebuilt from source**. `docs/perf/warm-leaf-profile.md`._

_**Round 867 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.14): `s_p` MEASURED, AND THE PER-KIND DISPATCH-TABLE QUESTION IS SETTLED AT ~1%.** The production cost of ONE handler consultation that is entered and immediately declines is **`s_p` = 2.286 ns [envelope 2.148-2.512]**, so **`R` = 32.0 M x `s_p` = **73.2 ms [68.7-80.4] = 1.06% of a warm rebuild [1.00-1.17%]**, 1.47% of the `checkSpine` row. Round 866's `[0, 352] ms` becomes `[69, 80]` and its 187 ms point estimate — the `d = d'` uniform-tax corner — was **2.6x too high**; the lever is REAL and MARGINAL, one band, at the edge of what `ab-warm.sh` (+-1.0%) can confirm. **INSTRUMENT: round 759's AMPLIFICATION, because at 2 ns the quantity is two orders of magnitude below a warm probe boundary (97-202 ns).** Per node, `r` extra passes over **exactly the (handler, kind) pairs the derived table would SKIP** — the `enterTable`/`leaveTable` complement as a `Long` bitmask — under ONE bracket, so two `r` values cancel the boundary; a CONTROL arm (negative `reps`) runs the identical loop with every consultation suppressed and cancels the skeleton too. **The population is MEASURED, not inherited, and lands on the arc's own numbers to the unit: 856,962 bracketed nodes, 32,006,965 would-consult slots, S = 37.3493 against round 732's 37.35.** **FALSIFIED BY ARITHMETIC, NOT BY TIMING (round 759's law), which matters here more than usual: a rejecting consultation is exactly what a JIT can prove side-effect-free and DELETE, and that failure reads as `s_p ~ 0`, i.e. it CONFIRMS the closed direction for the wrong reason.** Green on **48/48 rebuilds**: `consults == r x expected` EXACTLY at every `r` (3,072,668,640 = 96 x 32,006,965), with `expected` counted by a different code path from the one the passes count with; the control at **0** over a population `> 0`; and **`spineAmpPass` at 1,429 bytecodes = 4.4x HotSpot's 325-byte `FreqInlineSize`, so it cannot be inlined into the `r` loop and cross-iteration hoisting is STRUCTURALLY impossible** — a 307-byte leave-only second method was merged in for exactly that reason. **THREE INDEPENDENT `r`-PAIRS AGREE TO 2.4%** (2.253 / 2.308 / 2.286 at `r` = 16/48/96, per-arm sd 2.0-5.0%) **AND A SECOND BATCH OF A DIFFERENT DESIGN REPLICATES IT** (batch 1, `r` = 8/16/32: median 2.262 — 1% apart). **Batch 1's design was wrong and its own numbers said so:** both arms in ONE process share one compiled `spineAmpPass`, so the arm that runs first writes the branch profile the other is compiled against and an arm profiled as NEVER TAKEN pays an uncommon trap per consultation (per-arm sd 16-38%); batch 2 gives each arm its own JVM and discards a leading throwaway amp rebuild. **DECISION: nothing was built.** (DISPATCH.1) is neither confirmed closed warm nor a 2.7% prize; (WARM.13) re-opens as **(WARM.13b)**, an implementation item, with one new number that changes its outlook — **the CONTROL arm directly measures the proposed per-kind `Long`-bitmask dispatch skeleton (a mask load plus 59 register-resident bit tests) at 13.16 ns per node = ~11.3 ms whole-compile, 15% of the prize**, so round 866's `+715 ms` GATED tax was the `IntArray` walk, the tableswitch and the extra frame, never the idea (net ~62-73 ms). **THE BIAS THE INSTRUMENT CANNOT REACH, NAMED:** the amplified consultation happens at `spineAmpPass`'s call site, and a census says 36 of the 59 handlers are under `FreqInlineSize` (median 257 bytecodes), so for those the two sites' inlining decisions could differ in an unknown direction. **GATES: suite 14,120 / 1 / 3** (`xml.etree`, all four modules) — the one failure is `PostCheckerPartitionTest`'s residue ratio over a **272 us** region, green in isolation and structurally unreachable from a spine-prologue change; `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0; the 8-profile grid is **vacuous by construction** (the only `commonMain` behaviour change is gated on `SpineAmp.reps != 0`). Ten new pins (`SpineAmpProbeTest` 5, `BenchAmpTierTest` 5), five single-mistake ablations each with a uniquely-its-own failure — and one pin recorded as NOT discriminating rather than claimed. `docs/perf/dispatch-table.md` § 9._

_**Round 866 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.13): THE PER-KIND SPINE DISPATCH TABLE, RE-PRICED WARM — AND THE VERDICT IS **UNIDENTIFIED, NOT CLOSED**: the prize is bounded in **0-352 ms (0-5.1% of a warm rebuild)**, point estimate **187 ms = 2.7%** under one stated assumption, and `--dispatchGated` is STRUCTURALLY unable to narrow it because what it measures is `G - R` — its own machinery's price MINUS the prize.**_ The brief's premise (a cold-only verdict) was half false: **round 847 § 5 had already re-taken the probe's UPPER bound warm** — 352 ms, 10-11 ns per skipped consultation, warming **2.95x** against the spine's 3.38x — but that finding lives in `warm-spine-attribution.md` and **not in `dispatch-table.md`, the file a next agent greps**, which is half of what this round fixes. **`--dispatchGated` IS A LOWER-BOUND INSTRUMENT:** its five lines replace 46 inlined `if (spineXxActive)` call sites with a loop over `enterTable[kid]` through a 46-arm `when(h)` tableswitch, so it is faithful in SEMANTICS (byte-identical corpus and profile — which is what makes the derived table's soundness a fact) and pessimistic in COST by an amount nothing measured. **SO A THIRD ARM WAS BUILT:** `gatedfull` runs the SAME machinery over a table holding every handler for every kind, skipping **nothing** by construction, and prices the machinery alone at **+715.5 ms = +14.45% of the warm `checkSpine` row, 8/8 sign-consistent over two batches** — large enough to hide the whole prize, and the explanation of round 732's cold +4.9%/+7.4%. `gatedrows` reads **+75.2 ms median (5/8 slower, -276 to +283)**: GATED breaks EVEN warm where it lost 5-7% cold. **THE IDENTITY IS `R = A + K*d - delta(gatedrows)`** with `d` the machinery's tax on a KEPT consultation, identified by no arm: `d = d'` gives 187 ms, `d = 0` gives ~0, and `d <= d'` is all that can be argued. **Cross-check that makes both instruments believable:** `S*(s_p + d')` reads **20.00 ns** per skipped consultation against round 847's probe-calibrated **10-11 ns** for the same consultation with tableswitch and call removed. **THE WALL ARM IS THIS ARC'S THIRD ROUND-840(c) INSTANCE:** batch 1 **-1.49%, gated faster 3/4**; batch 2 **+3.53%, 0/4** — the batches disagree in SIGN, which is why the round moved to the row (the wall carries ~34% of a rebuild that is pure drift here). **WHAT WOULD SETTLE IT, PROMOTED AS (WARM.14):** `s_p`, the production cost of ONE rejecting consultation — `R = 32.0 M x s_p`, so the 1% floor is cleared at **2.2 ns**, an order of magnitude below the warm boundary cost, so the instrument is round 759's amplification and its hazard (a JIT can prove a rejecting consultation side-effect-free and DELETE it) is stated in the item. **Nothing was optimized, nothing under `commonMain` changed:** four `commonTest` tiers (`gated`, `plain`, `gatedrows`, `gatedfull`), two harnesses, two analyzers, 10 pins. **GATES: suite 14,100 -> 14,110 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**, `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit** (659 classes / 14,670 methods); the 8-profile grid is vacuous by construction and was not run. `docs/perf/dispatch-table.md` § 8._

_**Round 855 (2026-08-08) — (NARROW.2)(f) CLOSED AS A MEASURED NEGATIVE: THE CANDIDATE PRE-TEST REFUSES **0 OF 14,117** OPENINGS, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN A TUNING FAILURE.** Round 854 priced round 852's narrowed-`any` opening at 1.91% of a warm rebuild with 85.6% of it spent on receivers the flow never narrows, called 1.63% a CEILING rather than a yield, and queued (f) with one instruction: **census the pre-test before building it.** That instruction is the whole value of this round — the design looked worth ~1% and is worth exactly nothing, and one probe run said so for the price of one build. **THE CENSUS, cold, compiler profile, probe HONOURING NOTHING** (predicate evaluated, verdict recorded, walk runs anyway — so the population is bit-identical to round 854's and every rep prints the profile's usual 46 diagnostics): `openings=14117 narrowed=1345 accepted=1051 walkOnly=385/406/360ms` and `refused=0 (noPath=0) kept=14117 refusedNarrowed=0 refusedAccepted=0 keptNarrowed=1345 refusedWalkOnly=0ms preTestCost=211/162/150ms`; re-taken on the COMMITTED binary after the pins went green, `refused=0 … preTestCost=168/128ms`, identical population. **THE STRUCTURAL REASON, which is what must survive: every `VariableDeclaration` / `Parameter` / `BindingElement` mints a `FlowAssignment` whose subtree contains the declared name, so EVERY root DECLARED in a file is in that file's narrowable set BY CONSTRUCTION** — including a `declare const g: any` nothing ever tests. The set can only refuse a root with NO declaration in the file (an import, a cross-file ambient), and tsc's own `any` receivers are locals and parameters. There is no coarser or finer variant to try, because the declaration that makes a name exist is itself one of the narrowing nodes the set is built from. **A NEGATIVE WITH A PRICE ATTACHED:** the predicate's own wall is **150–211 ms against a `walkOnly` of 360–406 ms** for the entire population it was meant to shrink — mostly the one-off per-file `narrowableRoots()` construction, which a shipped gate pays exactly as the probe does. Go/no-go was ~70%; measured **0%**. **THE INSTRUMENT IS ALIVE (round 790):** on the same class dir that produced the profile reading, a two-file CLI project gives `refused=1` for an imported root and `refused=0` once one `if (imported)` is added. **WHAT THREE EARLY PIN FAILURES MEANT — the pins were wrong, not the implementation or the design reading:** `diagnose()` is SINGLE-FILE, an unresolved import is not `anyType`, so **no opening runs at all** (the failing subexpression was `openings > 0` reading `0`) and the refusing population is inexpressible there; the fix was to SPLIT the pins by level — the SET unit-pinned directly, the CONSUMER census-pinned — not to weaken them. 10 pins green alongside round 854's 4. **Round 854's 1.63% remains a ceiling no per-file name-keyed predicate can realise, so (NARROW.2)(c)'s 1.91% stands as measured and ACCEPTED, not as an open regression** (it bought `types/any` 3 failing of 9 → 1 with a 0/0 eight-profile grid and a clean corpus); a future attempt needs a path-and-position oracle, which is the walk itself. **STATED RATHER THAN IMPLIED: this round did NOT run the suite, `cost_gate.py`, `huge_methods.py` or its own ablation** — the ablation harness lost its run to a bash default-args bug (`"${@:-A1 A2 A3 A4}"` expands as ONE word, so every arm hit `unknown arm` while the script still printed `complete; tree restored`), now fixed. The change is probe-only and `PassTiming.detailed`-gated, so production behaviour and counters should be untouched — but "should" is not a gate reading, and **(NARROW.2)(f2) is queued at the top to close all four before any new work**. Commits `bc2495a7`, `d7461d4c`._

_**Round 854 (2026-08-08) — (NARROW.2)(e) CLOSED AS OUTCOME (c): ROUND 852's +79% NARROWING WALKS ARE 1.91% OF A WARM REBUILD AND A QUARTER OF ALL NARROWING WORK IN THE COMPILER, AND 85.6% OF IT IS SPENT ON `any` RECEIVERS THE FLOW NEVER NARROWS.** The first priced POSITIVE after six consecutive priced negatives; the round stops at the measurement and queues the fix as (NARROW.2)(f) with a CEILING rather than a promise. **THE OBVIOUS INSTRUMENT WAS ABANDONED IN THE FIRST TEN MINUTES:** the queue said to difference `--passTiming`'s `narrowWalk` row between an ablated build and HEAD, and that row reads **1,423 / 1,460 / 1,602 ms across three runs of the SAME binary** (+-6-9%), the same order as the object — so the span was taken where the cost is incurred instead: one timestamp pair around `cmamNarrowedAnyReceiverType`'s flow read, plus the delta of `narrowWalkNanos` across it (the walk body ALONE, excluding the tier-3 shadow bookkeeping that exists only under the probe), plus the produced-vs-consumed counts round 801 demands before any timing is read as a prize. **THE POPULATION, deterministic and bit-identical over six instrumented runs: `openings=14117 narrowed=1345 (9.5%) accepted=1051 (7.4%)` — and `openings` MATCHES round 853's `+14,110` walk delta**, so the ablation-attributed counter and this census are one population measured two ways. **THE PRICE.** Cold (3 reps): span **435 ms**, walkOnly **381 ms**, against a whole `narrowWalks` row of 1,451 ms — **26.3% of every narrowing walk the compiler performs**, and that RATIO (26.3/26.7/25.9%) is far steadier than either absolute. Warm, the shipping regime (round 843), probe-free median rebuild **7,664 ms**, every iteration 78 files / 46 errors: walkOnly **146 ms = 1.91%**, span 185 ms = 2.41%, **24.5% of warm narrowing** vs 26.3% cold — two regimes, one answer, including the wasted share (85.6% warm vs 85.4% cold). **BOTH STANDING LAWS HAD TO BE CHECKED RATHER THAN CITED.** Round 788 does NOT rescue it: `narrow.memoServed` moved **42,766 -> 42,867 (+101)** for +14,110 walks, so the work is ADDED, not moved, and deleting it deletes it. And **round 759's law runs the OTHER way here** — its "what you could skip cheaply is what was already cheap" holds only when the exit predicate and the cost share a cause, and here the predicate is *did the flow narrow* while the cost is *how far the walk traversed*, so the **90.5% that answer `any` carry 85.4% of the cost**. **WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** no pre-test was built — 1.63% warm is a CEILING (a perfect oracle), while the concrete design (a per-file set of root names reachable by any narrowing flow node) is COARSER, and against a +-1.0% band that gap decides the item; (f) carries the honours-nothing census, the soundness argument, a ~70% go/no-go threshold and a round-788 memo re-check. Landing a real gate on this function without that number would also walk into round 792's law head-first (a whole-function pre-gate here measured 0 emitting calls in a 22,187-call skip set on this very profile and still killed 7 corpus baselines). **LANDED:** `PassTiming.cmamAny*`, `scripts/round854-narrow-price.sh` (committed FIRST, round 789), `NarrowedAnyCensusTest` (4 pins incl. the REFUSED population, which is invisible in the compiler's output and is the whole point), `docs/perf/narrowed-any-opening-price.md`. **ABLATION: three arms, one mistake at a time, all three DISCRIMINATE** — drop the `accepted` increment -> 1 red, drop the whole span record -> 3 red (every counter-reading pin, with the disabled-run control correctly GREEN), invert the `narrowed` predicate -> 2 red, the two population pins that assert `narrowed > 0` and `narrowed == 0` and so can only both fail to an inversion. **GATES: suite 14,020 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (14,016 + 4 new pins), `cost_gate.py` **all 20 counters +0.00%** — a FALSIFIABLE zero now that round 853 wired the gate to the real binary — `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **exit 0**, census 649 classes / 14,547 methods / 0 over the limit. No 8-profile grid: nothing here can change a diagnostic._

_**Round 882 (2026-08-11) — (PERF.HW.h): STAGE 1 **CLOSED**, AND THE ANSWER INVERTS THE CODE'S OWN COMMENT — ON AN ALL-MODULE PROGRAM THE CHECKER MUTATES **ZERO** OF THE 15,580 BINDER-OWNED `Symbol`s.**_ Round 881 watched ONE site; `Checker.kt` has **150 others** (`flags` 4, `valueDeclaration` 30, `members` 19, `exports` 23, `parent` 11, `declarations.add` 63 — `Symbol.target` is written **0** times, having moved to the LinkStore), and a grep cannot classify them because it cannot tell a binder-owned receiver from a checker-minted one. **So the instrument is the opposite of an audit:** `--bindMutationCheck` fingerprints every `Symbol` reachable from the `BinderResult`s (locals + `nodeToSymbol`, recursing through `members`/`exports`, identity-keyed — sound because `Symbol` is a plain class, so round 471's deep-`hashCode` hazard does not apply) immediately before the `Checker` constructor, where the whole check runs, and re-compares after — seeing every write site, including ones nobody grepped. **`binder Symbols checked 15580, changed 0` on every field, in the SAME run as `mergeSingleSymbol: adopts 406, mutates 175`** — both true because those merges land on **LIB** symbols (`bindRealLibs`/`init:mergeLibGlobals`), which are in no `BinderResult`. **Program-file binder output is ALREADY immutable with respect to the checker**, and `PartitionCheck`'s "a worker must never reuse an already-checked bind" is true of the lib tables, not the program's. **THE ZERO HAS A POSITIVE CONTROL and that is the only reason it is worth anything** (round 849): the pin drives two GLOBAL SCRIPT files — no import, no export, so neither is a module — declaring one name, forcing a merge onto a program symbol, and asserts `declarationsChanged > 0`; it passes. **THE SCOPE IS THE WHOLE CAVEAT:** the zero holds because every file there is a MODULE and INV.3(d) keeps a module's locals out of `globals`, so nothing merges; a program with script files DOES mutate binder output, which is what the control shows. Hence sharing one bind is sound **for an all-module program with no further work**, needs stage 2 otherwise, and — the useful part — **the condition is cheap to test at runtime, so a shared-bind path can be GATED ON THE PROGRAM'S SHAPE rather than blocked on the refactor**. That gate is the next step and is far smaller than stages 2-4. **GATES: suite 14,265 -> 14,267 / 0 failures / 3 skipped = exactly the 2 new pins; `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 20 counters; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` 0 over the limit, 684 classes.** `docs/parallel-bind-sharing.md` § 2b._

_**Round 862 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.8)(c) LANDED, AND THE QUEUE ITEM WAS THE SMALLER HALF OF ITS OWN PRIZE: `cpcRequireOnlyOrphans` GOES **138.7 ms -> 0.0005 ms** AND THE WHOLE POST-CHECKER REGION **141.9 -> 2.84 ms = 139.1 ms = 1.82% OF A WARM REBUILD, 98.0% OF THE REGION** — OF WHICH **136.4 ms (1.79%) IS AN EXACT REWRITE THAT HELPS EMIT MODE IDENTICALLY** AND ONLY **2.3 ms (0.03%)** IS THE CHECK-ONLY GATE THE ITEM NAMED.** The one-consumer claim was RE-ESTABLISHED, not inherited, and held: four `grep` hits for the value and ONE write site for `jsOutputMap`, inside `cpcTransformAndEmit`, whose loop iterates `emptyList()` under `skipEmitOutputs` — so the census is provably dead work in a check-only compile, gated on `CompilerOptions.skipEmitOutputs` and never on `options.noEmit` (round 738; 440 corpus baselines depend on that distinction). **But the round turned on the item's own open question** ("whether the walk itself can be made cheap … a second, larger question"): sub-partitioned into its three per-FILE scans — three abutting level-3 `FrontEnd` blocks that sum to the block EXACTLY — the function is **87.36% ONE REGEX** (the `declare … require` probe, **121.15 ms**) against 12.13% for the `import("…")` scan and 0.269% for the statement walk, with a census of **78 files / 9,977,097 characters / 0 accepted**, bit-identical in all four draws. **THE MECHANISM IS A `java.util.regex` FACT WITH ITS OWN CONTROLLED COMPARISON:** `\bdeclare\s+…` begins with a ZERO-WIDTH ASSERTION, so `Pattern.compile` cannot hand it to `BnM.optimize` (which needs a leading literal `Slice`) and it is attempted at every one of the 9,977,097 positions; the sibling pattern in the SAME loop over the SAME text, `import\s*\(…`, begins with a literal and costs **a seventh as much**. Second time in three rounds that the most expensive thing in a region is a whole-program regex matching nothing. **LANDED:** an exact hand-written scan anchored on the literal `require` (571 occurrences → one linear sweep plus 571 constant-time rejections), the pattern kept LIVE as the specification a 7,776-shape differential compares against; a DEFERRAL of the `import("…")` pass behind the candidate sets (sound because `staticallyReferenced` is purely subtractive); then the gate. **MEASURED** (`BenchMain <proj> 3 8 frontend,frontend`, 2 processes x 2 draws per arm, all 12 rebuilds answering 78/46): HEAD **138.68 ms** (spread 11.8%) → after the rewrite **2.310 ms** (spread 4.8%) → after the gate **0.0005 ms**; `POST` 141.94 → 5.59 → 2.84. Round 793: the only vanished boundaries are 78 worth **7.6-15.8 us**. Round 801: nothing MOVES — the ratio is 0/1, not 1.000. Round 846: first-draw-is-slowest reproduces **2/2 in the before arm** and vanishes once the row is 2 ms. **THE WARM A/B IS QUOTED AND SET ASIDE** — `VERDICT: WIN of 2.3% (B wins 3/3) — outside the +/-1.0% warm band` — because **arm A's printed sd is 2.76%** against B's 0.46%, and round 774's rule discards such an arm however clean its median; the rows are the evidence. **WHAT THE ROUND CANNOT SAY:** the 8-profile grid is STRUCTURALLY BLIND here (the value reaches nothing but the list of emitted JS files) and was run as a control — green, `added=0 removed=0` both directions on all eight, 46x7/94, from two class dirs at 651 vs 652 classes; the gate that CAN see it is the EMIT one, **78 files from each arm, `diff -r` identical**. No cold measurement was taken and the emit-mode saving was not TIMED, only shown to be the same code path. **ABLATION, one mistake at a time, 20 pins per arm: M1** (drop `require\b`) **3 RED**, **M2** (gate inert) **1**, **M3** (gate widened to `options.noEmit`) **1**, **M4** (pass 2 always skipped) **2** — four DISJOINT failing sets, no redundant guard; M4 is the one that matters, since skipping pass 2 unconditionally is silently WRONG rather than merely slow. **GATES: suite 14,061 → 14,072 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**, `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters** at every sub-step, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit**.
