and the new population inherits `cmamCheckResolvedObjectType`'s whole existing firewall rather than bypassing it. **ABLATION: nine, one mistake at a time, and THREE came back undiscriminated with the cause named** — two pins are shielded by a second gate of my own (redundant guard, round 807; they redden only under the combined ablation), the `narrowed === rawType` early-out changes nothing because an un-narrowed `any` is a `Type.Intrinsic` the next test already refuses, and **the `Type.Reference` and `error`/`unknown` refusals are INERT on the whole corpus (14,016 / 0 with either deleted)** — kept as cheap conservatism, recorded as unproven rather than claimed as firewall. **PROCESS FINDING, the round's most expensive: `scripts/grid838.sh` was reading a PRE-MODULE-SPLIT `build/bench/xtsc-classpath.txt`** whose only class dir was the root project's stale one (582 classes vs core's 649, missing `ModeLedger`/`LibTypeCensus`/`CcetSections` — a pre-848 compiler), so every capture it produced ran the wrong binary with exit 0 and no tell; _**Round 850 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.4)(b) CLOSED: THE WARM COST INSIDE THE BIG SPINE HANDLERS IS STRUCTURAL, NOT RESTRUCTURABLE — 94-98% OF IT IS THE PASSES' OWN CHECKING WORK, AND NOTHING IN THE 18.75% OF THE ARTIFACT THESE THREE PROBES ATTRIBUTE CLEARS THE +-1.0% WARM BAND.** An instrument-and-verdict round; nothing optimized, no `src/` change (the tree delta is `scripts/round850_analyze.py` + `docs/perf/warm-intra-handler.md` + notes), so the suite was not required — stated rather than skipped silently; the harness ran `cost_gate.py` and `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` before measuring anyway, both **exit 0**. **THE VERDICT THE ARC WAS OWED:** opened warm, `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` is **94% its four checking calls and 6% scaffolding** (44 ms of 649 = **0.62% of a warm rebuild**) and `checkPropertyAccessInExpr` is **98% / 2%** (7 ms of 369 = **0.10%**) — round 733's cold 88.4% reproduces warm and *tighter*. Two independent probes, same answer: traversal and bookkeeping are at or under the measurement floor; the cost is the calls. **THE TABLES** (mean probe-free warm rebuild 7,076.4 ms; **24** instrumented rebuilds, every one 78 files / 46 errors, every deterministic counter bit-identical across a tier's 4 draws): `cta` level A = `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` **649 ms = 9.17%**; `cpa` level P = `checkPropertyAccessInExpr` **369 ms = 5.21%** (Q `checkSinglePropertyAccess` 4.38%, R `checkMemberAccessMissing` 2.57%); `arg` = `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` **309 ms = 4.37%**; **combined and disjoint 1,327 ms = 18.75%**. **PARTITION CHECKS, PLAINLY: 87% (`cta`) and 68% (`cpa`)** against round 847's dispatch probe, both sides as shares of their OWN round's rebuild — weaker than 847's 99.3%/102.6%, and three things are known about the gap: these probes cost **+100 to +900 ms** against 847's **+4,482 to +5,670**, so where they disagree the cheaper instrument is the better artifact-share estimate; `arg` is a CONTAINMENT check (`checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` is **40% of `ccetSpineLeave`**, so ~60% of the largest warm handler is still un-partitioned); and `cpa`'s 2.4 points are genuinely open. **THE BOUNDARY, AND THE ESTIMATOR MISTAKE IT EXPOSES: warm 127 ns (`cta`) / 97 (`cpa`) / 202 (`arg`), with `ArgSections`' own in-situ steady state at 134 ns, against 501 ns COLD** — so a cold `net` column over-subtracts warm by **2.5-5x** and every row here was recomputed from raw nanos, never inherited. The non-obvious part: **these partitions are LAYERED** (cta's B/C/D/E open inside level A's rows; cpa is a chain P > Q > R), so a deep boundary executes inside every level above it and the obvious estimator — sum of level deltas over sum of level extra-boundaries — **overstates the price by 1.7-2.2x (215 vs 97 ns for `cpa`)**; the estimator that counts each boundary once is the OUTERMOST level's delta over ALL extra boundaries, and the evidence is not circular for the inner levels: two arms whose boundary counts differ **2.5x** then land within **0-5% on every level** (cta A 649 = 649, cpa P 369 = 369, exactly). **NO LEVER CLEARS THE BAND — the fifth consecutive priced negative in the warm arc, prize measured FIRST each time.** Best candidate: the **nine pre-emission probes inside `checkSinglePropertyAccess`**, every one `exits 0`, every one paid at all 66,747 property accesses — **70 ms = 0.99%**, at the band edge, own draw spread 16%, behind a `ccetPrologueMayFire`-shaped correspondence trap and round 792's law (a "cannot fire" gate on this very function killed 7 corpus baselines while measuring 0 emitting calls on this very profile). Everything at or above 1% is the type system doing its job. **THE SHARPEST SUB-OBJECT, from an exit census that cost no new boundary (round 796): 91% of the argument-typing cost is spent on arguments that never reach the assignability relation** — round 759 measured 89% COLD; the warm price is **191 ms = 2.70% of a rebuild**, inside which **851 narrowing walks at 57 us each = 0.69%** and **357 of 953 narrowing calls return the INPUT type unchanged** (0.32%). **WHAT DID NOT WORK:** the first reduction subtracted the naive estimator and printed the eligibility gate at **-123 ms** (round 734's negative-net tell — cause was the layering, not the method); `BenchMain`'s `overheadMs` is far too noisy to calibrate with (arg cross-check 684 ns vs the partition's 202; per-draw overheads range -201 to +680 ms); and **a `*coarse` table prints `mode: ON`** because `BenchMain` clears the probe mode before dumping — data unaffected, arm unambiguous from its row set and boundary count, label always wrong, fix queued as a `commonTest` reorder. **A QUEUE NUMBER CORRECTED:** "these three probes cover 48.7% of the warm artifact" is 48.7% of the warm **SPINE** = **29.0%** of the artifact; what they directly attribute is **18.75%**. `docs/perf/warm-intra-handler.md`._

_**Round 849 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.3) CLOSED AS A MEASURED NEGATIVE AT ~0.014-0.08%, AND THE CENSUS THAT MEASURED IT FIRST REPORTED A CONVINCING ZERO FOR THE WRONG REASON.** A prize measurement, taken before any design, exactly as the queue item demanded; nothing optimized. **THE VERDICT: the entire per-request lib-TYPE re-derivation is 71 outermost mint spans costing 1-6 ms against a 7,139-7,316 ms warm rebuild = 0.01-0.08%** — an order of magnitude below even (WARM.2)'s 0.13% and two orders below the ~1% close threshold. **Instrument:** `LibTypeCensus` hooked at the two boundaries the checker ALREADY guards — `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol`'s `declaredTypes` hit/miss and `resolveStructuredTypeMembers`' `properties != null` early return — so round 801's produced-vs-consumed ratio comes FREE rather than as a second instrument; only the OUTERMOST mint is timed, through a depth counter SHARED by both hooks (they recurse into each other, so per-hook depths would double-count), which makes a span INCLUSIVE of everything it caused, i.e. exactly what a served entry would delete. **THE NUMBERS, bit-identical across all four warm draws AND the cold run: 119 lib mints (63 declaredTypes + 56 member tables) against 14,766 non-lib — the lib surface is 0.80% of all mints — producing 15,932 consumptions, a ratio of 133.9 : 1.** So the derivation is already fully amortized WITHIN one request and sharing across requests could only ever delete the production side. A lib mint is genuinely expensive (**93,464 ns/span warm vs 4,606 for a non-lib one, 20x**); there are just 71 of them — round 716's law by COUNT, not by unit cost. **THE STRONGER NEGATIVE, FREE FROM THE SAME RUN, RETIRES THE WHOLE DIRECTION AND NOT ONLY THE LIB SLICE: the entire mint boundary, lib plus user code, is 13,988 spans costing 38-66 ms warm = 0.5-0.9% of the artifact**, so NO cache placed at those two functions can clear the +-1.0% warm A/B band whatever it is keyed on, even in the impossible limit where user types were shareable. Type derivation is simply not where a warm rebuild's time is; round 847 already located that (`checkSpine` 66.0%, top four handlers 33.4%). **The hazard analysis the queue entry demanded is MOOT at this price and must not be re-opened**: round 778's first-touch instantiation context (failure mode byte-identical output — round 607's 51 corpus failures with `--listAll` unchanged), thread-local `Type` id sequences crossing the request boundary (the round-825 `--workers` mechanism), and round 844's 175 merge collisions. **THE ROUND'S BEST FINDING IS THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN DEFECT, CAUGHT BY ITS OWN PIN:** the consumed-side hook first went into `resolveStructuredTypeMembersCore`, whose `properties != null` guard is DUPLICATED one frame up in the wrapper — so the wrapper absorbed every hit, the hooked branch was dead, and the census read `memHitLib = 0`, which would have been written up as *'nothing ever reads a derived lib type back'*: the exact OPPOSITE of the truth (133.9 : 1), and a conclusion that happens to agree with the correct verdict for an entirely wrong reason. **A produced-vs-consumed census keyed on a boundary the CALLER already short-circuits measures nothing at all and reports it as a zero** — the counter-form of round 786's 'open on the WRAPPER' law; CLAUDE.md entry added. **ALSO LANDED, UNRUN: (WARM.4)(b)'s harness.** `BenchMain` now takes `cta`/`cpa`/`arg` tiers plus a `*coarse` twin each, so the three intra-handler section probes — all of them COLD one-shots on record, covering 48.7% of the warm artifact — can be re-taken warm with the boundary priced DIFFERENTIALLY inside one process (round 734). A next round starts at `bash scripts/round849-warm-sections.sh 2`; phase 1 validated that script's whole build -> cost-gate -> JIT-gate -> daemon-stop -> measure path. Calibration it must carry: this round's in-situ empty pair read **96-209 ns warm against 501 ns cold**, independently reproducing round 847's 'a probe boundary is ~1.85x more expensive cold'. **GATES: suite 13,984 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (13,979 baseline + the 5 new pins), counted per module with `xml.etree` over a fully wiped results dir; `cost_gate.py` **all 18 counters +0.00%**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **exit 0**, census still zero. `docs/perf/lib-type-rederivation.md`._

_**Round 848 (2026-08-08) — (SERVE.1) CLOSED: THE CLI ARGUMENT LOOP NO LONGER LEAKS DEBUG MODES ACROSS `--serve` REQUESTS, AND THE QUEUE ENTRY'S "SEVERAL OF THESE CHANGE WHAT THE COMPILER DECIDES" IS NOW MEASURED — WITH A DIFFERENT ANSWER.** `runCli` wraps its whole body in `try { … } finally { modes.restore() }` over a `ModeLedger`: `modes.set(Obj::field, v)` records the value the field HELD, so the restore is exact by construction (round 619: never assign a default back — `ArgNarrowGate.mode` defaults to ON, not to its zero value) and there is no second list to keep in step. **The queue named six unrestored flags; the audit found FIFTEEN**, including two nobody had listed — `SpineDispatch.mode == GATED` (only `PROBE` was ever cleared) and `CallSections.preGateProbe`, which forces the call prologue to run for every call. Pins: `ModeLedgerTest` (4), `CliModeRestoreTest` (2 — JVM reflection over every declared non-final field of all 16 mode objects, non-vacuous three ways), `CompileServerModeLeakTest` (2 — end to end through `CompileServer.respondTo`, reading the flag OBJECTS). Five one-mistake-at-a-time ablations, each attributed to the pin it reddens. Gates: **jvmTest 13,979 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (13,971 + 8 new pins), `huge_methods --fail-over 0` census **0** (`parseCliArgs` 4,306 bytecodes), `cost_gate.py` +0.00% on all 18 counters. **[CORRECTION, round 853, 2026-08-08: the md5 `4090b73e` above was captured through a stale pre-module-split classpath AND under a fourth digest recipe. Round 853 re-ran all 14 arms on a guard-verified binary — every one 46 lines, added=0 removed=0, md5 `84bbe7f0…` — so the finding STANDS; and `4090b73e` reproduces exactly on that verified capture under `s#.*/src/#src/#`, so the number was never wrong either, only unlabelled.]**_

_**Round 846 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.1)(c) CLOSED: `--passTiming` NOW HAS THREE TIERS, THE `rows` TIER IS FREE (+0.25% cold, 0.0% warm), AND THE PROBE'S LANDING SITE IS NOW MEASURED RATHER THAN BRACKETED.** An instrument round — nothing optimized; the deliverable is a warm attribution a lever can be sized against. None of the probe's ~2.6 s is the ~417 `pass()` boundaries: it is the per-CALL bookkeeping (574,620 `getTypeOfExpression` hooks each doing a distinct-keyed `HashSet` add + a by-pass `HashMap` + a shadow-memo probe + a timestamp pair; the `InstrumentedSymbolTable` on the hottest map in the program; every fenced-setter no-op; two BOXED `HashMap<Int,Long>` entries per node over 856,962 nodes). Partition check: the `spine` tier's sub-rows sum to 5,299 ms against the `rows` tier's 5,335.9 ms `checkSpine` row = **99.3%**; the tier's own boundaries are 513 ms = **599 ns/node**. **GATES: suite 13,971 / 0 / 3** (`xml.etree`, wiped dirs; core 13,896 + api 27 + client 18 + daemon 30 — **+7 exactly the new `PassTimingTierTest` pins**), `cost_gate.py` **every counter +0.00%**, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit**; all 6 `full`-tier runs across three JVMs report identical deterministic counters and every run answered 78 files / 46 errors. `docs/perf/warm-jvm-attribution.md` § 10._











_**Round 845 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.1)(b) CONFIRMED: THE (JIT.1) ARC BOUGHT 1.5x ON THE WARM ARTIFACT WHILE ITS OWN COLD INSTRUMENT READ IT AS NOISE.** Round 843 left this an explicit hypothesis; one isolated warm A/B settled it, and the answer changes how a standing gate is read. **THE INTERVAL WAS ISOLATED, which is what makes this an ATTRIBUTION and not a "since round 802" figure:** arm A = `93ff3195`, the immediate PARENT of the first split `d194baca` (census landed, zero splits applied); arm B = `d8ff69b5`, the arc's close. Every `perf(...)` commit in between is a `(JIT.1)` split (the rest are docs, split pins, bench chores), `git diff --name-only` across the interval shows ZERO build/gradle/toml/properties changes, and both arms predate the MOD module split. **The arms were verified in the BYTECODE before being measured: A = exactly 19 methods over 8,000 (matching round 802's census), B = 0.** **THE NUMBER: 4 interleaved pairs, 3 warm-up + 8 measured, per-process medians — A 10,088.0 ms (sd 0.58%) -> B 6,702.9 ms (sd 1.31%), -3,385.1 ms = -33.56%, B wins 4/4, 1.505x**, all 64 measured iterations in both arms at `files=78, errors=46` and both series flat 1->8. Against which the arc's own recorded verdict was round 803's falsifier: **`+0.08%, B wins 3/5, NOISE-DOMINATED`.** **The ~1% quiet-box sd rule was OVERRIDDEN EXPLICITLY, not ignored** — arm B is 1.31%, but the effect is **38x the larger arm sd**, every pair clears it by ~35x, and dropping B's one low outlier gives sd 0.38% and -33.46%; that rule is calibrated for the +-1% regime and this is not it. **THE RESIDUAL keeps round 843 honest:** batch 2, r821 vs HEAD, 3 pairs — 6,481.7 -> 6,620.9 ms, +2.15%, HEAD wins 0/3, i.e. at 1.9x the arm sd **zero warm compute movement between round 821 and today**. So 843's -39.5% decomposes as **~10% r771->r802 (NOT attributable to code — it spans the 4-core -> 8-core box change), -33.6% r802->r821 ((JIT.1)), ~0% r821->HEAD**; the same r821 binary read 6,702.9 in batch 1 and 6,481.7 in batch 2, a cross-batch drift 3x the within-batch band, which is why only within-batch paired deltas are quoted. **WHAT IT LICENSES, a standing change: `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` is a WARM-PATH gate** — it protects the daemon/`--serve`/AOT artifact far more than the CLI, because a method over 8,000 is never JIT-compiled and so its cost cannot improve with warm-up, which is exactly why the COLD protocol is structurally blind to the class. **Any change that grows a hot method past 8,000 must be A/B'd with `ab-warm.sh`, never `ab-interleaved.sh`** (CLAUDE.md entry added). Incidental, n=1, NOT quotable: cold smoke samples read A 24.7 s vs B 21.7 s, so there is likely a real but much smaller cold component too. **The reusable artifact that made a 40-round-apart warm A/B expressible at all is `WarmBench.java`** — a reflection clone of `BenchMain` linking against ANY arm's `ProjectCompiler` regardless of its `build(...)` arity; `BenchMain` is compiled against one arm's signature and cannot do this, and THAT, not the build, was the real obstacle to ever running this comparison. **GATES: none run and none required** — nothing under `src/` changed; two builds in a throwaway worktree and a measurement. `docs/perf/warm-jvm-attribution.md` § 2.2._

_**Round 844 (2026-08-08) — (WARM.2): SHARING THE BOUND LIB STATE ACROSS DAEMON REQUESTS IS WORTH 8.65 ms = 0.13% OF A WARM REBUILD. MEASURED, NOT ESTIMATED; NOTHING BUILT; STOP.** A warm-ONLY candidate, invisible to every attribution table this repo has (all cold): the daemon rebuilds a fresh program per request and `RealLibSnapshots.parseCache` reuses the PARSED libs process-wide, but BINDING them is per-program — free cold, paid on every request warm. Round 772's "retained program state is worthless" verdict is about the USER's files (`export *` barrels ⇒ 77-of-78 closures) and says nothing about the libs. **Priced before anything was designed**, per the round-716 rule that killed three caches in one round. **THE PRIZE:** whole real-lib region **8.65 / 9.86 ms** against a **6,846 / 6,663 ms** rebuild = **0.126% / 0.148%** — `RealLibResolver.resolve` 2.0 (a pure function of `(libNames, target)`, run TWICE per program), `bindLibFiles` **4.9**, identity stamping 1.5, `mergeSymbolTable` 0.09; 45 lib files, 379 top-level statements, 185 merged symbols. Cross-checked by an independent microbench of `bindLibFiles` alone (median **4.53 ms**, 10 runs). Lib-derived setup passes add ≤4.7 ms, so the **ceiling on everything lib-shaped is ~13.4 ms = 0.20%**. **WHY SO SMALL: the prize was already harvested** — `parseCache` shares the *parse*, the expensive half; what is left is a top-level bind over ~400 KB of body-less `.d.ts`. **IT DOES NOT MERELY MOVE** (round 788 does not apply: the type caches are `CheckerState` fields, i.e. per-`Checker`), which is what makes the number quotable — it is genuinely deletable and genuinely tiny. **HAZARDS MEASURED, NOT THEORIZED, and one is worse than predicted: 175 merge collisions per compile, 20 of them MUTATING a lib symbol** (`Symbol`, `ImportAttributes`) — so request 2 would inherit request 1's `flags |=` and accumulated `declarations`, unbounded — and that is a LOWER bound (nested `exports`/`members` writes and `mergeModuleAugmentations`' `globals[…] =` are uncounted). The id hazard is **ORDER, not collision** (one daemon thread ⇒ no interleaving), but skipping the bind shifts every later program's symbol ids by ~185, and round 607's sibling failure was **51 corpus failures whose `--listAll` output stays IDENTICAL**. A safe version is barely expressible — the merge target set is discovered dynamically, so safety means copy-on-write over every lib symbol, re-doing the allocation the bind was doing; only `resolve` + stamping are safely shareable, **3.5 ms**. **VERDICT: not worth a round** — 0.13-0.20% is an order of magnitude inside BOTH A/B bands (cold ±2.0%, warm ±1.0%), i.e. unmeasurable by the instruments that would confirm it, while carrying a hazard class that produces byte-identical wrong output. `RealLibSnapshots`' "binding is deliberately per-consumer" KDoc is right and now has a price: **4.9 ms warm**. **RULED IN BY ELIMINATION, queued as (WARM.3):** the compile is 91.8% checker warm, so the only lib-shaped candidate left is per-request re-derivation of lib **TYPES** — unmeasured, inside `checkSpine`, and strictly MORE hazardous (`Type` ids, interning, instantiation contexts). It gets a prize measurement, not a design. **GATES: none run, none required** — nothing under `src/` changed, probe reverted, tree verified clean; the probe was 3 timestamp pairs per PROGRAM (~0.3 µs against 6.8 s) and its walls sat at or below round 843's probe-free medians._

_**Round 842 (2026-08-07) — (AOT.5)(f): THE AOT TRAINING DRAW IS WORTH UP TO 2.4%, AND EVERY AOT RESULT ON RECORD WAS MEASURED WITH ONE TRAINED CACHE PER ARM.** Five caches trained by an IDENTICAL command (`scripts/xtsc-aot train`, nothing changed between runs), **168 whole-project compiles in seven rotated batches**. Paired best-vs-worst draw: **+322 ms (+2.4%), 10/12**, and the ordering **REPLICATES** — batches D and E, run separately with different rotations hours apart, put the same three draws in the same first three places — so this is a persistent property of a cache FILE, not run noise. **The methodological result: "two batches" is the standing rule for a MEASUREMENT and does NOT cover a TRAINING claim**, because a second batch re-uses the same cache files and so replicates the measurement draw while leaving the training draw a between-arm confound. **New rule: an A/B on a training configuration needs >=2 independently trained caches per arm.** **It overturns no DECISION and costs one NUMBER its precision:** round 840(c)'s SHIPPED emit-training win is -1,132 ms (-5.4%) against a draw term of ~1.2-1.6% on that same workload (>3x, safe), while round 840(d)'s rejected `--workers 4` arm was a *null* on the path it was meant to help plus a cost on the default — a draw term cannot turn a null into a win — but its **+3.5%** is only ~1.5x a term it modelled as zero and must stop being quoted to three digits. **STRUCK OUTRIGHT: 840(d) § 10.3's reading of that cache's "+82 KB" as evidence of a different recorded profile** — five identical-command draws span **212,992 B**, 2.6x that, and size does NOT order speed (the largest of the five is second-slowest, the smallest is slowest). **Batch A was a textbook false positive for the third round running** (medians spread 483 ms = 3.5%, bigger than the effect that survived; batch B put a different draw first). The emit half adds the finer point: the EXTREMES carry across workloads (draw 1 fast, draw 5 slow on both) while the fine ordering does not. **Nothing ships — that is arithmetic, not caution:** "train twice, keep the faster" needs ~10 paired compiles to resolve a 2.4% difference worth ~0.3 s per run, paying back after ~800 compiles, and it makes the installed cache non-reproducible by the documented command. **(AOT.5)(a) closed in passing:** the shipped `~/.cache/xtsc` cache, un-retrained since round 840's fingerprint change, was retrained (54,079,488 B, verifies `USE`) and **pruned 1 stale cache** — § 2's "delete on upgrade" observed on a real fingerprint change. **(AOT.5)(b) ALSO CLOSED — `--serve`/`--daemon` UNDER a cache, through the launcher: 32 requests, all 46 errors / one md5 / ZERO in-process fallbacks; paired over three rotated server pairs, **request 1 is 1.66× (3/3), request 2 still 1.32× (3/3), requests 3-4 a wash** — round 839's "halves the first request, worth nothing warm" reproduced through the shipped launcher and REFINED (the cache shortens the C2 warm-up ramp by about one request, which first/warm-only sampling could not see). **Its first run was an A/A that looked exactly like an A/B:** `build/libs/*.jar` is **NOT byte-reproducible** (`./gradlew jvmJar --rerun` with no source change yields a different sha256 AND size, 5,639,210 -> 5,639,200 B), so this session's own suite run invalidated the cache trained 8 minutes earlier and both arms ran uncached at a plausible ladder — caught ONLY by printing the launcher's decision (`XTSC_AOT_VERBOSE=1` -> `aot SKIP no-cache-file`). **Train AFTER the last build; never measure a cached arm without printing the decision.** **Correctness:** all 168 compiles at **46 errors and one diagnostics md5** (`59d930db…`), no empty capture, no `more error(s)` tell; the 36 emitting runs additionally share **78 files and ONE whole-tree digest** (`0b59764c…`). **GATES: suite 13,909 / 0 / 3, unchanged — and NO PIN WAS ADDED, stated rather than omitted:** nothing under `src/` changed, the claim is a measurement, and its repeatability is the committed harness `scripts/aot-draw-variance.sh`; `cost_gate.py` and `huge_methods.py` not run and not applicable. `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 12._

_**Round 840(c) (2026-08-06) — (AOT.4)(c): THE CACHE IS NOW TRAINED *WITH EMIT*, WORTH −5.4% ON AN EMITTING COMPILE FOR NOTHING ON THE CHECK-ONLY PATH — AND THE ROUND'S MORE VALUABLE RESULT IS A FALSE POSITIVE IT CAUGHT IN ITS OWN FIRST BATCH.** `xtsc-aot train` ran `--noEmit`, so the shipped cache carried no C2 profile for the Transformer or Emitter at all; round 839 priced training with emit at ~800 ms and left it unclaimed. **54 whole-project compiles of the 78-file profile, paired within-rep, rotated, daemons stopped, box unwatched: emit 14/15 pairs, median −1,132 ms = −5.4%; `--noEmit` 10/15, −150 ms = −0.8%, a wash. There is no trade** — the check-only path pays nothing. Round 839's MECHANISM is confirmed by differencing each rep's emit run against its own `--noEmit` run: the emit tail goes **3,148 → 2,216 ms (−932 ms)**, i.e. back to the 2,139 ms an UNCACHED run pays, which is exactly "the cache now carries an emitter profile" and not "longer training also warms the checker" (that would have moved the check-only arms, and it did not). Predicted ~800 ms, measured 932. **THE FALSE POSITIVE, which is the part to remember: batch 1 read the CHECK-ONLY path as 5/5 sign-consistent wins, median −1,161 ms (−6.6%), with the winner in the drift-disadvantaged position 4 of 5 times** — every internal check a single batch can offer said "real". Batch 2 (3/6, +192 ms) and a verification batch (2/4) refuted it. **Rotation and pairing control DRIFT; only REPLICATION controls the DRAW** — now a CLAUDE.md entry, and a caution over every 5/5 and 6/6 win rate this arc has quoted. **Packaging: acceptable only via a new `--outDir`.** `train` runs against the user's own project, so emitting into its `outDir` would clobber a real `dist/` or die on a read-only tree; so `ProjectCompiler.build(outDir = …)` is exposed as `--outDir` (CWD-relative like tsc's, inert under `--noEmit`, deliberately NOT threaded through `--incremental`/`--watch`) and `train` emits into a `mktemp -d` removed on every exit path — verified end to end: no `dist/` in the profile, no leftover temp, cache self-verifies `USE`, 4/4 paired emit wins. The fail-safe contract is deliberately untouched: **the training WORKLOAD is not in the fingerprint and must not be** — the manifest binds a cache to the code it was trained from, not to how that code was exercised — so existing `--noEmit`-trained caches stay VALID, merely ~5% slower on emit until retrained. Cost: +2.9 MB cache (+5.6%), +1.5 s training. **Correctness:** all 54 runs one sorted-diagnostics md5 (`59d930db…`, 46 errors, the digest rounds 828/832/839 recorded) and all 30 emitting runs one whole-tree sha256 (`a3ccd863…`, 78 files) with `dist/` deleted and re-hashed each time. **GATES: suite 13,903 → 13,908 / 0 / 3** (+4 `ProjectOutDirTest`, +1 script-contract pin), `cost_gate.py` every counter **+0.00%**, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` **0 over the limit** — both required and run, because this one touched `commonMain`. **NOT measured:** a cache trained under `--workers 4`; `--serve`/`--daemon` under a cache through the launcher; `--watch`/`--incremental` under a cache; the other seven profiles; a project whose emit share exceeds this profile's ~13% (the direction should hold and the share should grow — an argument, not a measurement). Commit `e355a990`; `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 9._

_**Round 840(d) (2026-08-07) — (AOT.4)(c)'s SECOND HALF CLOSES AS A MEASURED NEGATIVE, AND IT BOUNDS THE PRINCIPLE 840(c) JUST ESTABLISHED.** Training the AOT cache under **`--workers 4`** buys the parallel path **0.9%** — inside the band — and **costs the sequential default 3.5%**, so `xtsc-aot train` STAYS SEQUENTIAL, now pinned (`AotCacheGuardTest.the trainer trains sequentially`, ablation-verified after committing: adding `--workers 4` to the trainer fails exactly that pin and no other). 60 compiles, two caches differing ONLY in training worker count, **two independent 5-rep rotated batches that AGREED at every level** (seq +501/+679, w4 −110/−114, w8 −854/−610 ms). Paired: seq **+545 ms (+3.5%), 2/10**; w4 −112 ms (−0.9%), 8/10; w8 −732 ms (−4.7%), 8/10. **THE TRANSFERABLE RESULT: 840(c)'s "train under the mode you actually run" is a claim about ONE DIMENSION AT A TIME, not a law.** Emit was free because the Transformer/Emitter profile was simply ABSENT, so adding it took nothing away; worker count is the SAME checking code under a different thread structure, so the shared JEP 515 profile is a genuine trade. (c)'s fingerprint reasoning — the training WORKLOAD stays out of the manifest, which binds a cache to the code it was trained from, not to how that code was exercised — is untouched and endorsed by this round. **A ROUND-839 CLAIM IS RETRACTED:** "`--workers 8` under a cache is WORSE than cached sequential" does NOT reproduce under rotation (15,483 vs 15,761 ms, paired 6/10, −0.8%) — 839's ladder was BLOCKED runs, not an interleave. What survives is weaker and still useful: w8 buys **nothing** over sequential under a cache, where uncached it bought 1.13×. **Cached `--workers 4` remains the fastest configuration this project has** — 1.290× over cached sequential, **10/10** paired. **Correctness:** all 60 runs 46 errors and one md5 (`59d930db…`), 10 runs per worker level, i.e. double the standing "≥5 runs per level" `--workers` rule. **GATES: suite 13,908 → 13,909 / 0 / 3**; `cost_gate.py`/`huge_methods.py` not run and not required — docs, one comment in `scripts/xtsc-aot`, and one `src/jvmTest` pin only. **NOT measured:** `--workers 2/8` training; **training-run VARIANCE** (one training run per arm — batch 2 replicated the measurement, not the draw); an emitting workload under either cache; `--serve`; the other seven profiles. Commit `654ac969`; `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 10._

_Round 838 (2026-08-05) — **(NARROW.2)(a)+(b): TWO CORE NARROWING DEFECTS, AND BOTH OF ROUND 837'S DIAGNOSES ARE RETRACTED AND REPLACED BY MEASURED ONES.** (a) was handed down as "there is no `instanceof` arm in `extractNullNarrowing`". There does not need to be: a user-declared `class C` narrows correctly through the flow walker at every consumer probed (assignment, argument, property access), and the whole defect was `resolveInstanceOfRhsType`'s `SymbolFlags.Class` gate one function away — **every ambient constructor in the lib is written `interface Error { … }` + `declare var Error: ErrorConstructor`, so `Error`/`Date`/`RegExp`/`Map`/`Set`/`Promise` narrowed NOTHING in either branch while a user-written class narrowed fine.** Fixed with tsc's `getInstanceType` (the `prototype` property, then a lone construct signature's return), deliberately bounded: no `[Symbol.hasInstance]` leg, no `mapType` over a union-typed RHS, no signature erasure, and where tsc falls back to `emptyObjectType` we return null — a `{}` narrow would be a new and wrong type at every consumer. (b) was handed down as "a guard's narrow LEAKS out of its `if` into the next sibling `if`". **It is not a scoping leak — the `if` scoping is correct and a UNION subject behaves perfectly.** It is one relation call: both single-type NEGATIVE branches (a type-predicate guard's and `narrowByInstanceOf`'s) decide "the subject IS the target, so the false branch is impossible" with the ASSIGNABLE relation, and `any` is assignable to everything — so the else branch of a guard on an `any` subject was `never`, the flow join of (`Foo` from then, `never` from else) produced `Foo`, and it ACCUMULATED across sibling guards (`Error | Function` after two). **Nothing in the output ever names `never`, which is exactly why it presented as a leak.** tsc survives it by construction (its assumeFalse filter goes through `isTypeSubsetOf`, plain identity for a non-union candidate). The POSITIVE branch is untouched — that is (c). **PINS, BOTH DIRECTIONS ON REAL BINARIES, 14 across two classes.** (a): against the rebuilt pre-change binary exactly the **4 positives fail and the 3 controls stay green**; against a single-mistake ablation returning the constructor type where the resolution has no answer, exactly `a value that is not a constructor does not narrow` fails and the other 6 stay green. (b): against the rebuilt pre-change binary exactly **3 positives fail**; against a both-branches ablation, `the positive branch on an any subject still narrows` fails. **A fourth pin written as a positive was MEASURED not to discriminate and is RENAMED to say what it actually tests** — narrowing the leaked `Function` by `is Error` happened to answer `Error` anyway. **No control exists for "this subject still collapses to `never`": a `never` narrow is unobservable in this compiler** — assignable to every probe target, silent at a property access, and an argument position reads the declared type. **GATES:** suite **13,900 / 0 / 3** (13,886 + 14 pins, **0 regressions**, `xml.etree`); **the 8-profile grid was run TWICE against a rebuilt before-arm — after (a) and again after (b) — 46x7 + harness 94, every sorted capture md5-IDENTICAL on all eight, both times**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, 0 over the limit; `cost_gate.py` PASS on both commits, `output.errors` 46 unchanged, largest movement -0.01% (`narrow.walks` -2, `typeNode.bypassed` -9, `globals.lookups` -27 after (b) — the profile sites where a negative branch no longer washes a subject to `never`), rebaselined with `--update` in each commit. **NO CONFORMANCE MOVEMENT, and that was predicted:** all three `types/any` narrowing cases still emit nothing, gated entirely on **(c)** — `checkMemberAccessMissing` reading a narrowed `any` receiver — which stays UNCHECKED and carries the round's FP warning._

_Round 837 (2026-08-05) — **OWNER DIRECTION: CONFORMANCE BREADTH. THE HANDED-DOWN TARGET WAS RE-COSTED AND OVERRULED ON EVIDENCE, AND THE ROUND'S SECOND DELIVERABLE IS THAT CONFORMANCE BREADTH IS NOW MEASURED TO BE OUT OF CHEAP WORK.** Round 836 priced `types/any`'s three NARROWING cases as "ONE mechanism: narrowing FROM `any`, exempt `Function`/`Object`". Re-created the survey harness from its own description (~110 lines, **40 s per measurement against ~14 min for a suite arm**; it reproduced round 836's row exactly) and probed the compiler with deliberate mis-assignments — **it is THREE gaps**: a type-predicate guard ALREADY narrows an `any` subject (only tsc's `Function`/`Object` exemption is missing, two lines); `instanceof` narrowing reaches NOTHING (no arm in `extractNullNarrowing` at all — even an `Error | string` body-local is un-narrowed in the then-branch); and **the narrow never reaches the PROPERTY-ACCESS walker when the declared type is `any`**, which is what actually blocks all three cases and means widening `checkMemberAccessMissing` over the shape tsc's own sources use constantly. A fourth defect found and not fixed: a guard's narrow **LEAKS out of its `if` block into the next sibling `if`**, precisely the five-sequential-`if` shape the fixture is built from. **TOOK THE THREE BOUNDED CASES IN THE SAME CATEGORY INSTEAD — `types/any` 6 -> 3 failing, all three closed on errors AND emit:** TS2347 through `new` (tsc reaches `resolveUntypedCall` from `resolveNewExpression` too, so `new x<any>(x)` is as much an error as `x<any>(x)` — same narrow gate as the call leg, not `calleeType === anyType`); `void` and `typeof undefined` assumed-initialized for TS2454 (tsc's guard is `type.flags & (AnyOrUnknown | Void)` OR facts carrying `IsUndefined`; deliberately NOT folded into the shared `typeIncludesUndefined`, whose other callers are TS2564 sites where a `void` property DOES want the diagnostic, and deliberately SYNTACTIC because resolving inside a candidate COLLECTOR makes the set a function of resolution order); and TS2631 in an assignment target. **THE CORPUS CAUGHT THE THIRD BEING WRONG AND IT IS THE ROUND'S GOTCHA:** keying on `SymbolFlags.Module` came back 13,885 / **5** — `Module` is the UNION of `ValueModule` and `NamespaceModule`, and tsc gives the two DIFFERENT diagnostics at the same syntax (instantiated ⇒ the write is the error, TS2631; non-instantiated ⇒ no value meaning, so the value-position TS2708 fires first). **PINS, BOTH DIRECTIONS ON REAL BINARIES:** 16 new + 2 corrected; against the rebuilt pre-change binary **exactly the 8 positives fail and all 8 controls stay green**, and the `ValueModule` ablation is the round's own first attempt, whose control fixture is byte-identical to `assignToModule.ts`. Four TS2347/TS2454 controls are recorded as ARM pins, not claimed as coverage. **GATES:** suite **13,886 / 0 / 3** (13,870 + 16, 0 regressions, `xml.etree`); `cost_gate.py` PASS, 46 errors unchanged, every counter +0.00% except `globals.lookups` +198 (+0.03%) from the new `new`-site resolution, rebaselined in the same commit; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, largest 5,149; **8-profile grid against a REBUILT before-arm, md5-identical on all eight**, the before-arm reproducing round 836's digests exactly. **RECOMMENDATION: leave conformance breadth as a target.** ~9 bounded subtests remain across nine categories and **none completes a category**; rounds 833-837 landed five features and seven cases and adopted zero categories. The concrete alternative is **(NARROW.2)** — an `instanceof` arm, block-scoping the guard narrow, and letting `checkMemberAccessMissing` see a narrowed `any` receiver — whose first two steps are core correctness gaps, not fixture-chasing._

_Round 836 (2026-08-05) — **OWNER DIRECTION: CONFORMANCE BREADTH. We carry no `NodeFlags.OptionalChain` bit, so the predicate is a descent through the chain's own links: a `?.` anywhere BELOW makes the whole target a chain (`a?.b.c` is one), a PARENTHESIS terminates it (`(a?.b).c` is not), outer wrappers skipped first as `skipOuterExpressions(expr, Assertions | Parentheses)` does. `checkDestructuringPrivateIds` already enumerated exactly tsc's target positions, so it carries the new leg as `checkDestructuringAssignmentTargets`. **`propertyAccessChain.3` and `elementAccessChain.3` (22 diagnostics each) now match their baselines exactly, ORDER included — and BOTH CASES STAY RED on their own `?.`-downlevel EMIT subtest, which is the survey's whole point: the errors half of a case can go green while the case does not.** Exposure is bounded by construction: every one of these codes reports code tsc itself rejects, so tsc's sources cannot contain one — and the grid measured **zero sightings on all 8 profiles**._

_**PINS, BOTH DIRECTIONS, ON REAL BINARIES.** 15 pins. Against the **rebuilt pre-change binary** exactly the **10 positives fail**, 5 stay green. ONE MISTAKE AT A TIME, each its own build: skipping PARENTHESES inside the chain descent fails exactly `a parenthesis terminates the chain`; answering "the subtree contains a `?.`" fails exactly `an optional chain inside the index is not a chained target`. **The other 3 pins are NOT ablated and are recorded as ARM pins, not claimed as coverage.** **GATES:** suite **13,870 / 0 / 3**; `cost_gate.py` **PASS, all 20 counters +0.00%**, `output.errors` 46, no rebaseline; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, largest 5,149; **8-profile grid vs a REBUILT before-arm — 46x7 + harness 94 on both arms, no truncation tell, sorted-capture md5 IDENTICAL on all eight** (compiler `59d930db849399aea5e03e25fedb8e4e`)._

_Round 832 (2026-08-05) — **(JIT.2b), OWNER-DECIDED 2026-08-05 "BUILD THE INVALIDATION, THEN SHIP": THE JDK 25 AOT CACHE NOW SHIPS BEHIND A FAIL-SAFE GUARD, ROUND 828'S SILENT-WRONG-ANSWER MODE IS CLOSED, AND THE 1.638x SURVIVES AS **1.68x (3/3 PAIRS)**.** The hazard is the whole item: the JVM has NO invalidation — with `Checker.class` removed from the jar and a fresh mtime, the plain run dies with `NoClassDefFoundError` and **the cached run exits 0 printing `OK — 0 errors`**, `-XX:AOTMode=on` included. **THE CONTRACT — fail safe, with no opt-out:** `scripts/xtsc` passes `-XX:AOTCache=` only when a recomputed manifest matches the stored one **byte for byte** — sha-256 of every classpath entry *in order*, the JDK build (`$JAVA_HOME/release`'s digest), OS/arch, main class, a launcher-version constant, and the cache file's own size and digest; a pin holds the other direction so a `touch` does not throw the 1.68x away. **THE COST WAS MEASURED FIRST AND SHAPED THE DESIGN: ~80 ms = 0.8%** of the ~10,000 ms delta, affordable only because the digests and `stat`s are **batched into one process each** (the per-entry loop cost 80 ms by itself, purely in `fork` — which is what would have forced the stat-based fast path and with it the one hole that matters). **VERIFICATION:** round 828's stale-jar scenario now **exits 1 with `NoClassDefFoundError`** (`SKIP no-cache-file`) where the unguarded run exits 0 with the wrong answer, and with the stale pair PLANTED under the new fingerprint's filename — defeating the name layer so only the content check can act — **`SKIP manifest-mismatch`**, `status` naming the differing line; So this is **not** round 801's `created 1143, materialized 1143`: about half genuinely evaporates. **IT CLOSES ON PRICE:** per branch the deferrable part is `decl` alone (12,168 resolves / 563-705 ms, **199-252 ms deletable**) = **0.8-1.0% of a ~25 s compile, below `ab-interleaved.sh`'s ±2.0% cold band and at the floor of `ab-warm.sh`'s ±1.0%** — unmeasurable by this repo's own protocols, against name resolution program-wide plus a WHICH-pass-resolves-first program-order dependency (rounds 754/776/778). **AND THE BIGGEST-LOOKING SLICE IS A TRAP: the `typealias` branch reads 99.7% "never needed" by the map criterion and is the TS2589/TS2615 depth-bail DETECTOR** — the bail is observable only *while* `getTypeOfSymbol` runs, so not resolving those symbols deletes a diagnostic rather than deferring one (the B57.x corpus pins are exactly file-level aliases nothing later reads). **WHAT DID NOT WORK — the first census was wrong by 4x and its own arithmetic said so:** keying the deletable set on entries that were STORED reported 1,399 symbols / 49-59 ms while 67.3% of the resolves stored nothing at all and were structurally invisible to it. **A produced-vs-consumed census must be keyed on the CALLS, never on the survivors.** Residue stated, not implied: the ~200-250 ms `decl` slice is recoverable in principle and left unclaimed **on price, not on possibility**. Gates: suite **13,816 / 0 / 3** (+4 `FltmCensusTest` pins), `cost_gate.py`, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0`. `docs/perf/setup-phase-and-huge-methods.md` § 27._

_Round 827 (2026-08-04) — **(NATIVE.1) THE P0 IS FIXED: THE NATIVE PIPELINE HAD BEEN RUNNING ON THE DEFAULT 8 MB MAIN STACK AGAINST THE JVM'S 256 MB, AND THE ID HANDOFF IS NOW EVIDENCED BY OBSERVED IDS RATHER THAN BY A GREEN SUITE.** `DeepStack.native.kt` was literally `= block()`; it now does `pthread_attr_setstacksize(256 MB)` + `pthread_create` over a `staticCFunction`/`StableRef` + `pthread_join`, with the block under `runCatching` because **a Kotlin exception must never unwind out of a `staticCFunction` into C**, degrading to the old pass-through if thread creation fails. **256 MB COSTS NOTHING, VERIFIED NOT ASSUMED** (the orchestrator's guess that a zero-swap box would pay for it eagerly is FALSIFIED): a glibc pthread stack is anonymous `mmap` with no `MAP_POPULATE` — reserved virtual, committed on first touch — and `/usr/bin/time -v` max RSS is **110,856 KB** (test binary) / **107,320 KB** (CLI on a real project). **THE HANDOFF IS SHOWN:** probe on a real native run gave `typeIds before=191 insideStart=191 insideEnd=196 after=196` and `intrinsics outer=[1,4,10] inside=[1,4,10] sameIdentity=true` — seeded at the caller's 191 (a dropped seed gives 1) and written back at 196, **the exact invariant round 825's bug violated from the other side**. `DeepStackHandoffTest` (4 pins) **is a seam pin because it failed against the mistakes it names, one at a time**: write-back removed 4/4, seeding removed 4/4. **RETURNED TO commonTest:** `CfaTooLargeBailTest` (native 8 tests, 41.0 s) and `Inv4SpineAccessorModifierDeepChainTest` (1 test, 31.0 s); **`DeepExpressionChainTest` stays JVM-only** and the returned chain pin's KDoc records that `none { 2589 }` is **VACUOUS on native**. Suites **native 13,707 / 0 / 3** (13,689 + 5 + 13 = exactly 13,707, so the run count itself proves nothing was masked) and **JVM 13,812 / 0 / 3**. **THE HONEST LIMIT, and why the fourth-axis CLAUDE.md entry was corrected rather than deleted: an overflow is now ~32x RARER, NOT CATCHABLE** — the boundary guard is still inert natively, so a deep enough shape still kills the process. **What did not work:** the native suite ran 30m25s against 21m01s, of which the returned pins explain 73 s and **the rest is UNATTRIBUTED** (no controlled A/B; per-compile thread creation is named as a candidate, not claimed, and is irrelevant to a CLI run doing one compile per process); and the pins' warm-up threshold was `> 100` against an observed margin of 191 — 1.9x, too thin, lowered to `> 20`. Commits `f7ebc4cf`, `21e21091`, `5417ae20`._

_Round 826 (2026-08-04) — **THE LADDER RE-TAKEN ON THE FIXED BINARY: THE RACE WAS *DEFLATING* THE PARALLEL ARMS, w4 IS 1.361x, (M2) STEP (b) PRICES SMALL ENOUGH TO DECLINE — AND THIS ARC'S CROSS-ROUND ABSOLUTE COMPARISONS TURN OUT NOT TO BE QUOTABLE.** Ladder (n=6, rotated interleave, paired, every level sign-consistent): seq **23,183** | w2 **17,770** (-22.54%, 6/6) | **w4 17,039 (-27.10%, 6/6, 1.361x [1.289,1.414])** | w8 **18,663** (-17.67%, 6/6); caveat stated by the agent itself — the id-slice fix and round 825's `prewarmParsedLibFiles` were not A/B'd apart. **AMDAHL, WITH A RETRACTION OF ROUND 824's READING:** seq/w2 -> 1.876x, seq/w4 -> **1.546x**, seq/w8 -> 1.287x; **only seq/w4 reproduces, the fitted divisible share falls MONOTONICALLY with N (an N-growing overhead no 2-parameter model expresses), so round 824's "the w2/w4 fits agree, the model is coherent through w4" is RETRACTED.** Realistic ceiling **1.361x at w4, and worker count is EXHAUSTED there.** **(M2) STEP (b) PRICED, AND ITS FRAMING WAS WRONG IN A LOAD-BEARING WAY:** the per-worker re-bind and ~318 collectors run CONCURRENTLY, so they cost CPU (+27.6% at w8) and RSS (2.8x) but only ~1x WALL, and cores never saturate — (b) buys 1.361x -> ~1.448x realistic = **1.0-2.0 s of a 23.2 s compile (4-8.8%)**, and beating ~1.55x needs the collectors made DIVISIBLE, not de-duplicated. **RECOMMENDATION: do NOT attempt (b) for wall time** — 4-7% against work that makes binder/checker share state across threads, the exact area that just produced a race latent since round 754; **re-queued as a MEMORY item** (RSS is the binding constraint if parallel ever becomes default). **THE METHODOLOGICAL FIND, reaching beyond this item: THE SEQUENTIAL ANCHOR IS UNSTABLE ACROSS ROUNDS AT ~13% WITH BYTE-IDENTICAL CODE AND WORKLOAD** (23,183 / 25,299 / 26,145 / 26,518 ms = 12.8% spread) — round 824's "3.3% from the anchor" reconciliation was LUCK, and **only WITHIN-round paired deltas are quotable.** **What did not work:** the 3-parameter `R + P/N + C*N` fit is exactly determined on three points and came out unidentifiable (per-rep R ranged 1,569-14,929 ms, 10x). _Round 825 (2026-08-04) — **(PERF.HW.a) IS DIAGNOSED AND FIXED, FOR REAL THIS TIME: THE `--workers` RACE IS A SINGLETON ID-SPACE COLLISION, AND THE VERIFICATION IS 48 RUNS AGAINST ROUND 754's ONE.** **PROVED, not inferred:** `runInDeepStackWorkers` gave every worker the **SAME** `WORKER_ID_BASE`, which is sound only if nothing SHARED is ever minted from a worker's own sequence — and **nothing in the sequential prefix of a parallel compile reads an intrinsic type**, so `TypeKt`'s static initializer first ran INSIDE whichever worker reached `anyType` first, AFTER that worker had rebased. Probed directly (kept as `XTSC_WORKER_PROBE=1`), w4 on the HEAD binary: all four ranges began at 1e9 and **`anyType=1000000005` / `stringType=1000000008` / `neverType=1000000014` sat INSIDE three of the four workers' id ranges** — and type ids are the keys of `relationComparisonStack`, `relationResultCache`, `referenceCacheLong`, `resolvedPropertyTypes`, so those workers confused the singletons with their own 6th/9th/15th type. **Which worker wins the class-init race is scheduler-dependent — that is the nondeterminism.** This is INV.6(6c0)'s hazard from a direction its entry does not name: no id CROSSED a boundary, a SHARED value was minted from a worker-local counter. **ONE mechanism, not two** — round 824's disjoint w2/w4 and w8 FP families are the same bug at different blast radii, both vanishing under the same fix. **FIX:** a **DISJOINT per-worker slice** (`WORKER_ID_BASE + i * WORKER_ID_STRIDE`, 10 M, ~100x the observed 105 k-symbol peak), which **makes ANY lazily-initialized shared singleton harmless**, plus `forceIntrinsicTypeInit()` on the caller thread; separately `RealLibSnapshots.prewarmParsedLibFiles` closes a proven `HashMap` `getOrPut` race the count detector cannot see (**not** credited with the flicker fix — commit 1 alone measured clean). **VERIFICATION: 48 post-fix runs, every sorted capture byte-identical (a SINGLE md5) and identical to sequential** — seq 46x3, w2 46x6, w4 46x6, w8 46x8, against HEAD's w2 47x2 / w8 56x5. Gates: suite **13,808 / 0 / 3** (+5 `ParallelWorkerIdSpaceTest` pins), `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% every counter**, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` = 0. **HONEST LIMIT:** the clinit-ORDER half cannot be pinned in-process (by the time a test runs, `TypeKt` is already initialized at a low id, so the pin is vacuous); the disjoint-slice half — what actually makes the class harmless — IS pinned. **What did not work:** two failed repros that both look EXACTLY like "the race does not reproduce" (one aimed at `src/compiler` instead of the project root; one ran the w8 script WITHOUT `--workers 8`), and `pkill -f '…MainKt'` killing its own shell. **CARRIED FORWARD: round 824's timing ladder is NOT quotable** — it compared a correct sequential run against a sometimes-diverging parallel one, so 1.343x and the ~1.5x re-fit must be re-taken on this binary. Commits `9c69eaff`, `42d8f48c`._

_Round 824 (2026-08-04) — **`--workers N` IS A *RACE*; ROUND 754 NEVER CLOSED (PERF.HW.a); THE 1.25x AMDAHL CAP IS REFUTED BY COUNTEREXAMPLE. THE PERFORMANCE CASE FOR (M2) UNPARKS, THE CORRECTNESS GATE DOES NOT.** **The headline is correctness and it outranks the scaling table:** at a FIXED worker count the diagnostic count varies run to run — **seq 46x8; w2 46x5/47x5; w4 46x3/47x3; w8 46x1/56x6**. Divergences are strictly ADDITIVE (`added=N, removed=0` everywhere — the mode never loses a true diagnostic, it invents false ones) and each OUTCOME is byte-reproducible (w8's 56-set matches by md5sum across captures), but **which outcome you get is not**; two DISTINCT FP families (w2/w4 = `debug.ts:601` TS2345; w8 = 10 other lines NOT including it). **Round 754's byte-identity was ONE DRAW OF A 50/50 COIN — this round reproduced the false green itself.** On the old box ~0.85 free cores effectively SERIALIZED the workers, so **the upgrade did not introduce the race, it made it OBSERVABLE**; `--partitionCheck` cannot see it (a static partition model cannot produce two answers for one worker count), and the detector that can is a COUNT DISTRIBUTION over >=5 runs. **THE JVM CORE TAX IS NOT A CONSTANT — IT SCALES WITH `nproc`, WHICH REWRITES THE GATE ITSELF: 4.17 of 8 cores vs 3.15 of 4**, because `CICompilerCountPerCPU` defaults true (JIT CPU ~21.7 -> ~43.8 s; `-XX:CICompilerCount=2` drops the run 4.20 -> 2.55 cores with self time FLAT, reproducing round 618/740). So free cores went 0.85 -> **3.83, not 4.85** — and **3.83 SUFFICED: the answer arrived at 8, not the stated >=12.** **LADDER (self ms, n=5, rotated interleave, paired):** seq **26,145** (3.6%) | w2 **21,324** (**-19.05%**, 5/5) | **w4 19,472** (**-23.84%**, 5/5) | w8 **21,605** (**-17.02%**, 5/5) — every level sign-consistent, none straddling zero as round 740's w4 did; box verified clean (8 real cores, no SMT, no cgroup cap, steal 0.000/113 samples). **The SHAPE changed, not just the magnitude: w4 is now the BEST level and w8 — a +19.4% REGRESSION on 4 cores — is a 17% WIN**, with nothing saturating (6.44 of 8 cores). **THE 1.25x CAP IS DEAD, refuted by counterexample: w4 measured 1.343x**; re-fits give 1.584x (seq/w2) and 1.516x (seq/w4), agreeing within 8% where on 4 cores they disagreed 3.5x. **VERDICT: two of round 740's three reasons to not attempt (M2) have failed; the third is worse than believed — the mode is a RACE, not a fixed bug — so (M2) stays blocked on (PERF.HW.a), re-opened**, and step (b) is now worth ~1.5x rather than ~1.25x. **What did not work:** an UNSORTED flicker diff manufactured 3 phantom TS2591 extras that were pure line ordering, and **stage C's 8 extra runs are what turned "w8 is deterministically 56" into "w8 flickers" — one capture per level would have shipped the wrong classification, the SAME under-sampling error round 754 made.** No `src/` change, so `jvmTest` not applicable; 13,803 / 0 / 3 untouched. `docs/perf/worker-scaling-round824.md`, commit `c9258486`._

_Round 800 (2026-08-02) — **(IANY.1) THE 249 ms CALL-ARGUMENT ARM IS GATED: 93% OF ITS CALLEE RESOLUTIONS BOUGHT NOTHING.** Round 799 handed forward the arm at 249 ms over 31,575 edges (7.9 us each, 32% of `spineIanyEnterNode`) with its obligation stated exactly: the state it computes is a `kind = 0` one with no type in it, so the arm is pure cost whenever the argument's subtree holds no reader — and `rhsCanConsumeFnCtx`, the obvious reuse, is UNSOUND for it. `spineIanyArgSubtreeMayRead` is the predicate built to that obligation: reader set = ARROW / FUNCTION_EXPRESSION / OBJECT_LITERAL (every other reader sits strictly inside one of those three), descending the arms that INHERIT the state and every no-arm parent, STOPPING at a nested CALL/NEW (whose own arm shadows its whole subtree — and an UNREACHED call makes everything below unreached, reach being monotone). **It defaults to `true`, so it can only ever refuse to skip**, and a 32-step cap (0 hits) bounds it; measured cost **1.87 steps per edge**. Both arms twice on ONE binary (`--ianyArgGateOff`), identical boundary counts, the predicate priced INSIDE both: callee resolutions **20,812 -> 1,439 (-93.1%)**, the arm row 196/190 -> 93/96 ms, and — quoting the ENCLOSING total per round 798's own correction — the **HANDLER TOTAL 724/707 -> 602/617 = 106 ms, 6.2x the within-arm spread, ~0.36% of a check-only compile**; a `--passTiming` pair agrees (`checkSpine` 21,047.6 -> 20,949.4). **No wall-clock A/B**: 0.36% is a third of the +-1.0% warm band. **ROUND 788's LAW IS ANSWERED RATHER THAN ASSUMED** — `typeOfExpr.distinct` falls **6.18%** against `calls` **4.21%**, and distinct falling FASTER means **14,813 expression nodes are now typed NOWHERE in the compile**, i.e. the arm was their only consumer and the work is deleted, not moved (`narrow.walks` +0.00%, `typeNode.bypassed` +5 of 110,776). **TWO CORRECTIONS.** The cheap gate does not exist: `typed = false` occurs **15 times in 20,827** because at a reached argument edge the state is ALWAYS the call's own kind=1 frame. And **round 799's own second counter-shape is VACUOUS** — the ablation that drops the array-literal AND `as` descents fails the array pin and the equivalence pin but NOT the `as` pin, because `spineIanyEdge`, the REACH classifier, has no `AsExpression` arm: nothing below an `as` is walked, so there is no reader there to lose. **What did NOT work**: the first probe build crashed on CLAUDE.md's own declaration-order trap, and the first timing batch read every row ~270x too large because the build's Kotlin daemon was still resident — BUILD.1's trap one round after round 799 recorded it; the deterministic census was immune and carried the round. _Round 775 (2026-07-31) — **(AOT.4) THE CORPUS SUITE HAS NOW RUN ON KOTLIN/NATIVE FOR THE FIRST TIME — `linuxX64Test`, **9,161 tests completed, 2 failed**, 14m28s — AND THE SAME SESSION FROZE THE OWNER'S BOX FOR ~2 HOURS. BOTH ARE THE RESULT.** The evidence gap (AOT.1) carries ("the suite has never run against any native build") is closed for linux-x64. **THE LEVER WAS `Debug`, NOT MEMORY** — round 772's RELEASE test link died to the OS OOM-killer after ~22 min, while `compileKotlinLinuxX64` + `compileTestKotlinLinuxX64` cost **2m10s**, `linkDebugTestLinuxX64` **1m03s** and `linkDebugExecutableLinuxX64` **1m28s**; of the item's five "untried levers" exactly one mattered. **A duplicate-symbol link error came first, and only the native TEST link can see it**: `BenchMain.kt` declared a top-level `main` in the compiler's own package and K/N mangles that with no file component, so it collided with `Main.kt`'s (`ld.lld: error: duplicate symbol kfun:com.xemantic.typescript.compiler#main(...)`) — the main executable link never pulls in commonTest. Moved to `…compiler.bench`. **THE TWO NATIVE-ONLY FAILURES**: `enumNoInitializerFollowsNonLiteralInitializer` reported TS18056 where tsc and the JVM report TS1061 on `d = (c)! satisfies number as any` — i.e. `prevInitializer is Identifier` answered TRUE for a non-Identifier — fixed with the codebase's own M0.2 `kind` discriminator, which is provably JVM-equivalent (`Identifier` is the only class declaring `kind = SyntaxKind.Identifier`), **but the K/N-miscompile diagnosis is SUSPECTED, NOT CONFIRMED: no minimal repro was obtained and the fix was never re-verified natively**; and `CfaTooLargeBailTest.a deep branch chain trips the depth limit` exits the test process, almost certainly platform-inherent (a native stack overflow is a hard crash) and unverified. **THE COST**: the host was unresponsive for ~2 hours, and the reusable lesson is that **`-Xmx4g` does not bound a native build** — the konan/LLVM backend and the linked test executable are separate processes outside every JVM heap, and with ZERO swap the kernel can only reclaim file-backed pages (including running processes' text), so the box thrashes instead of OOM-killing. **Local Kotlin/Native and GraalVM builds are now FORBIDDEN by owner directive (new CLAUDE.md rule); native moves to CI** — `.github/workflows/native.yml` gains a `kotlin-native` job (16 GB runners, `-Dorg.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx6g` because K/N compiles inside the GRADLE daemon), **UNTESTED, since it cannot be run from here** — and build.gradle.kts registers linuxX64 only under `-PenableNativeTargets=true`, verified configuration-only (`tasks --all` lists **0** linuxX64 tasks by default, **27** with the property), so `./gradlew build` is untouched. Suite **13,223 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**, zero regressions. **The cost gate FAILED, and the failure is NOT this round's** — `typeNode.bypassed` 95,909 -> **104,162 (+8.61%)**, `mapped.keyed` 24,693 -> **25,583 (+3.60%)**; falsified before being accepted by REVERTING the `Checker.kt` hunk, recompiling and re-running, which gave **byte-identical** counters. **NOT rebaselined** — `--update` would erase the only evidence that anything moved. Queued as **(COST.2)** with a decisive test._

_Round 774 (2026-07-31) — **(AOT.2) THE WARM A/B DRIVER LANDS (`scripts/ab-warm.sh`), AND ITS OWN A/A VALIDATION FOUND THAT ROUND 771's ±1.0% WARM BAND IS A PROPERTY OF A BOX NOBODY IS TOUCHING: THE SAME BINARY AGAINST ITSELF READ **−6.70%** WHILE I POLLED THE RUN'S LOG AND **−0.36% / −0.54% / +1.02%** WHEN I LEFT IT ALONE** (process-median sd **278 ms** vs **48 ms**; round 771's own was 187 ms). The driver is the warm counterpart of `ab-interleaved.sh`: one fresh JVM per SAMPLE running `BenchMain`, the sample being the MEDIAN of that process's iterations (never the iterations themselves — they share JIT state and heap, so pooling them would fake the sample count), interleaved A,B,A,B so both arms meet the same drift. **The statistical stance is copied from the cold driver verbatim; only the band changes** (1.0% vs 2.0%), which is why an A/A correctly reads NOISE-DOMINATED in BOTH runs — so the discriminator between a good run and a poisoned one is the per-arm sd the driver now prints, not the verdict, and the rule recorded is to discard any warm run whose arm sd exceeds ~1%. **Self-falsification is wired and was TESTED BY FAKING IT**: the driver parses every iteration's `files`/`errors` and aborts (exit 3) on any disagreement or on a process that yields no iterations; both paths were exercised with a stub `java` emitting a deliberate 78/46 → 78/47 drift and emitting nothing. All 96 real iterations reported 78/46. **The classpath contract is the non-obvious part**: `BenchMain` lives in `commonTest`, so the run needs the test classes while the A/B must swap only MAIN classes — `<arm main dir> : build/classes/kotlin/jvm/test : <deps>` — which means the shared test dir comes from the CURRENT build and a change altering the API `BenchMain` calls cannot be A/B'd warm at all; and there is no B-side extra-args slot, since `BenchMain` takes no compiler flags (a flag comparison stays a cold job). The dep tail is cached in `build/bench/cp-warm.txt` and used only while newer than build.gradle.kts — the committed `build/bench/cp.txt` is STALE (kotlinx-io 0.9.0 / kotlin-stdlib 2.4.0 where the build resolves 0.9.1 / 2.4.10), which is exactly the trap that guard exists for. **The prediction I got wrong is the round's output**: I read the first run's spread as "this box is worse than round 771's" and was one commit from writing that down; the cause was my own ~100 polling shell commands, which is round 740's "a single xtsc run already takes ~3.15 of 4 cores" arriving from the other side. **No arc item was re-opened** — doc § 4's caution stands (measurable is not worth landing; round 736's identity pre-test was rejected as UNSOUND, not small). Script + docs only, no `src/` change, so no suite gate; suite unchanged at **13,223**. Artifacts: `scripts/ab-warm.sh`, `docs/perf/aot-native-image.md` § 4a, `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` § 6._

_Round 773 (2026-07-30/31) — **(SERVER.1) STAGE 1: A PRE-WARMED COMPILE SERVER MAKES THE WARM JVM THE FASTEST ARTIFACT WE SHIP — 11,907 ms THROUGH A REAL UNIX SOCKET, AGAINST THE AOT BINARY'S 13,350 ms AND THE COLD CLI'S 26,415 ms, WITH OUTPUT IDENTICAL TO THE DIRECT CLI'S (all 46 diagnostics).** `xtsc --serve` holds one warm JVM; `xtsc --daemon <args>` sends a request over a Unix domain socket. Four requests converge on round 771's independently measured **11,580 ms** warm steady state: **26,415 / 14,311 / 11,933 / 11,907 ms**. **It composes with (AOT.1) rather than competing** — the GraalVM image is the ideal thin CLIENT (milliseconds to start) while the JVM SERVER keeps C2 peak. **TWO INVARIANTS, both written into the source.** Requests are served **SEQUENTIALLY on one thread**: Symbol/Type ids are thread-local (INV.6(6c0)) and `--workers` is already measured producing 62 diagnostics where sequential produces 46, so a thread-per-connection server would reopen that bug class **to save nothing** — the compile is the cost, not the accept. And a request runs the **ORDINARY `main()`** with stdout captured, so server output is CLI output by construction rather than by testing; reusing one JVM across compiles was already proven safe by `BenchMain`'s 12 in-process rebuilds all reporting 78 files / 46 errors. **Stage 2 (retained program state) is deliberately ABSENT** — round 772 measured it worthless on this corpus, tsc's `export *` barrels making the reverse-dependency closure the whole program. **TWO BUGS FOUND BY TESTING, NOT INSPECTION**, which is the reusable part: a Unix socket path over ~108 bytes fails as a bare `SocketException: Unix domain path too long` naming neither path nor limit; and the check for it first landed as a `require`, which **CRASHED the client instead of letting it fall back** — the very contract `--daemon` documents. **What is NOT pinned is stated rather than implied**: 7 pins cover `CompileServer.respondTo` (the whole behaviour minus the socket) and are not bound in-suite on purpose, a thread parked in `accept()` inside a 13k-test suite being a flakiness source; byte-identical CLI parity is also unpinned, needing two full compiles in one method where the suite's default 512 MB test heap OOMs. Suite **13,216 -> 13,223 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**; warning-clean. **PROCESS: the authoring session ended at protocol step 5** (code committed, note/checkbox/STATUS undone) — the second time in four rounds, so the loop now closes the previous round's bookkeeping before picking new work._

_Round 771 (2026-07-30) — **OWNER-DIRECTED, NOT A QUEUE ITEM: THE JVM's OWN COST WAS NEVER MEASURED, AND IT IS 56% OF A COLD COMPILE. AOT RECOVERS ALMOST ALL OF IT — 1.97×, BYTE-IDENTICAL ON ALL 8 PROFILES, ZERO COMPILER CHANGES.** `bench-compile-tsc.sh` forks a fresh JVM per run, so its `--warmup N` warms the page cache and never the JIT: **all 341 archived CI rows are cold single-shots and xtsc's steady state was unmeasured.** Twelve iterations in one JVM (`BenchMain`, the harness that could always have answered this and never was asked): **iter 0 26,272 ms, iters 2+ median 11,580 ms — ~14.7 s of a 26.3 s compile is warmup**, and iter 0 reproduces round 739's independent 26,896 ms, which validates the harness. **This is round 740 from the other side**: that round saw 85.6 s of user CPU, ~2.2 cores of JIT and C2 never finishing `Checker.kt`, and concluded "not a single-thread lever" — correct, and it missed that the compile thread never REACHES compiled code. **A GraalVM CE native image (standalone, no build-system change) runs it in 13,350 ms = 1.97× the cold JVM**, 15% off JVM peak, RSS 392 MB against a 4 GB heap allowance, image build 2m 1s. **The gate is byte-identity on all 8 profiles, not a count** — sorted `--listAll` diff empty on every one, speedups 1.71–1.98×, and the error grid comes out **46/46/46/46/46/46/46/94**, reproducing the dashboard's known composition; **the saving is CONSTANT in absolute terms (12.4–16.0 s) across a 4× range of project size**, which is the signature of a fixed warmup cost. **`-O3 -march=native` measured 13,325 vs 13,335 ms — nothing** — so the residual 15% is the absence of PGO, which CE cannot do; that direction is closed. **The warm drift band is now calibrated and is the round's most reusable output: six A/A processes give ±1.0% = ±114 ms against the cold ±2.0% = ±536 ms, 4.7× more sensitive in absolute terms** — so arc items rejected as in-band ((CALL.4) 441 ms = 3.9× the warm band, `getTypeOfExpression` 7.1%, single-visit 5.8%) deserve re-examination, with the caveat stated loudly in the doc that **MEASURABLE IS NOT WORTH LANDING** (nothing got bigger, only visible; round 736's identity pre-test was rejected as UNSOUND, not small). For scale: the whole rounds-482–759 arc landed −11.42% and −4.53%; this is **−49% of a cold compile for zero compiler changes**, and orthogonal to all of it. For the port question it was run to inform: steady-state COMPUTE is 11.6 s, so the gap to tsgo is ~**5×, not 13×**, and a Kotlin port of tsgo would inherit the warmup tax AOT removes for free. **Evidence limits, stated rather than glossed: the corpus suite is a JVM harness and has never run against a native binary, so the 8-profile grid is the only behavioural evidence and is weaker than the suite**; one box; single run per profile; emit mode not measured natively. Nothing integrated — queued as **(AOT.1)** the shipped-artifact decision (Guardrails, owner's), **(AOT.2)** adopt the warm protocol, **(AOT.3)** emit-mode native + PGO. Artifact: `docs/perf/aot-native-image.md`._

_Round 770 (2026-07-29; bookkeeping completed 2026-07-30) — **(REL.4)(a) CAUSE 5, THE POST-SWITCH FALL-THROUGH — AND THE DEFECT WAS IN THE BINDER, NOT THE CHECKER.** Leaving a `default`-less switch, `bindSwitchStatement` joined the post-switch flow to a bare FlowCondition chain asserting "every case EXPRESSION is falsy" — the truth for the `switch (true) { case <cond>: }` idiom ONLY, since a discriminant switch's case expression is a VALUE (`SyntaxKind.A`), so every link narrowed nothing and `Debug.assertNever(x)` after an exhaustive `default`-less switch saw the WHOLE declared type. Fixed as tsc encodes it: a switch-clause flow with an **EMPTY clause range** (`createFlowSwitchClause(…, 0, 0)`, read as `clauseStart === clauseEnd` ⇒ treat as `default:`), layered **ON TOP** of the existing chain rather than replacing it, so every existing `switch (true)` answer is untouched; three checker readers now treat an empty range as "no case matched", i.e. the same position as a `default:` clause and hence the same subtraction. Round 768's label for this cause — "does not narrow at all … NOT enum-specific, flow-graph work" — is confirmed exactly: the pins cover enums, discriminated unions and `typeof` tags alike. **A SECOND DEFECT FELL OUT, found by a pin breaking rather than by inspection**: `narrowBySwitchOnTrue` keys purely on `clauseStart` and never consults `clauseEnd`, so the new empty range read as "clause 0 matched" and narrowed POSITIVELY on the way OUT, collapsing `switch (true) { case c1: … }` to `never` (`narrowByClauseExpressionInSwitchTrue3`); guarded, since the edge's own claim is already carried by the chain beneath it. The general invariant — four readers consult this node, and one keying on `clauseStart` alone is wrong the moment an empty range reaches it — is now in CLAUDE.md. **THE ROUND WAS SENT AT CAUSE 6 AND PIVOTED**: cause 6's machinery is `narrowByCallPredicate`'s over an INTERSECTION subject, which has no member list to peel, so it stays its own round — the pivot confirms round 769's sizing of it. Gates, all **re-run from scratch on a restarted host rather than taken from the round's own commit message**: suite **13,216 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (13,207 + 9 pins), compiler profile `--listAll` **46** and programFiles **78** unchanged, cost gate exit 0 with **16 of 20 counters bit-identical** and four moved (`narrow.walks` 71,326 -> **71,323**, `narrow.memoServed` 45,489 -> **45,488**, `globals.lookups`/`globals.misses` **+0.16% / +0.17%** from the new readers resolving case tags that used to bail) — inside tolerance, rebaselined. No corpus baseline moved, so no `LogicalParityDivergence`. **Every pin run against pre-fix `619641b2^`: the 5 TARGETS FAIL and the 3 NEGATIVE CONTROLS PASS**; the ninth passes on both sides by design — it guards the second defect, which the fix itself introduced and then fixed. **NOT MEASURED, and it is the round's open end: the scaffolded (REL.4) flip price.** Cause 5's two lines (`checker.ts:11536`, `37648`) are both in `src/compiler`, so the expectation is **compiler 56 -> 54 / services 60 -> 58** — a PREDICTION, and the unscaffolded grid at 46 cannot see it, because those lines exist only while the imported-namespace pair is scaffolded on. **PROCESS: three consecutive server-side API failures (500, 529, 529) killed the authoring agent after the work was complete and before it could gate**; the orchestrator inspected the diff for completeness and probe residue and landed `619641b2`, then the host restarted before protocol step 5, leaving the note, this bump, the queue update and the rebaseline undone — all four are the follow-up commit._


_Round 769 (2026-07-29) — **(REL.4)(a) THE NAMESPACE-SCOPED ENUM: ONE RESOLVER, 3 MORE `assertNever` LINES CLOSED, AND THE KEY-SPACE WIDENING PROBED BEFORE IT WAS FLIPPED.** `resolveEnumSymbolForDiscriminant` — the single name-based entry into the `"symId#member"` discriminant key space — resolved the enum name through `currentFileLocals` / `lookupPerFileForNode`, i.e. FILE-level lookup only, so an enum declared inside a `namespace` (tsc's `const enum ParsingContext` in `namespace Parser`, `SpecialSearchKind` in `namespace Core`) was invisible to EVERY narrowing direction at once. **THE PROBE CAME FIRST, the round-750/751 shape**: both keys minted side by side over the 8 profiles — of **14,507 (compiler) / 23,066 (services)** resolver calls, **0** names resolve BOTH ways (the widened space is DISJOINT, so no existing key could move), and for every enum the fallback newly reaches, its members' `parent` canonicalizes to the id the fallback returns — the key `getDeclaredTypeOfEnumMember` interns under — so the space stays unsplit (round 425). Filtered batch of the 66 enum/switch/`never`/exhaustion/discriminant classes **454 / 0**; corpus letters C/D/E/N **3,197 / 0** and M/P/S/T **1,933 / 0**; build warning-clean; cost gate 18 of 20 counters bit-identical, `globals.lookups`/`globals.misses` **+20 (+0.002%)** from the 130 newly successful resolutions — rebaselined in the same commit. +15 pins (`NamespaceScopedEnumNarrowingTest`). **Predictions 3 of 4** — the miss is the size of the newly-reached population (predicted >= 50, measured 2 before the flip), and the miss IS the finding: the early bail was hiding its own cause. NOT taken, deliberately: the qualified `N.A.X` direction from OUTSIDE the namespace (0 measured sites, so it would be an unmeasured widening of the same key space). The full suite is the owner's to run; expect **13,192**._

_Round 767 (2026-07-29) — **(REL.2) THE IMPORTED-NAMESPACE CALL-ARGUMENT BLIND SPOT SIZED BEHIND A SCAFFOLD, MEASURED AND REVERTED — NOTHING LANDED AND THE 8-PROFILE GRID IS BYTE-IDENTICAL TO ROUND 766's.** **The mechanism is TWO missing steps, not one, and my one-step read of it was wrong the same way round 766's was.** (1) `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess`'s namespace fallback fires only for a receiver carrying `SymbolFlags.Module`, and an import alias carries `Alias` — so `Debug.take` types as `anyType`, `getCalleeType` returns `anyType`, and `checkCallTypesInExpr` bails one line before the signature machinery. The barrel-aware resolver already exists and is deliberately FLOW-ONLY (`resolveImportedNamespaceSymbol`), **which is exactly why round 766 saw `Dbg.assertIsA(p)` narrow while `Dbg.take(arg)` went unchecked**. Each step alone is measurably INERT (step 1 alone leaves the grid at 46/46 while lifting resolved receivers 0 -> 3,659); only the pair moves anything. **THE PRICE, per profile, with the pair scaffolded on: compiler 46 -> 66, tsc / jsTyping / deprecatedCompat / typingsInstallerCore 46 -> 66, services 46 -> 71, server 46 -> 72, harness 94 -> 121** — `PropertyAccess` callees surviving the `anyType` bail go 3,652 -> 4,779 on compiler and 6,615 -> 8,166 on services, i.e. round 766's 1,108 / 1,518 `Debug.*` calls. **Every new line is an FP, in three causes read line by line:** ~75% are `Debug.assertNever(x)` whose argument does not narrow to `never` (4 bare enums, 6 enum-member unions / property-access reads, 1 element access, 4 node unions); 4 compiler / 7 services are `Debug.assertIsDefined` / `checkDefined` GENERIC INFERENCE (M3.1, not (REL.2)); 1 is cause (D) literal widening downstream. **The 763-766 arc does NOT go live under it** — `enumMinusMembers` stays 14 / 31 and round 766's never-arm stays 0 / 0, before and after — **but its subject matter becomes observable for the first time, and two sites name their own gap**: `checker.ts:38292` (two sequential `===`/`return` guards, only the FIRST subtracts) and `checker.ts:8056` (a ~13-arm ternary chain, only the innermost subtracts). **The global enum -> MEMBER rule is EXACTLY ADDITIVE with this**: alone it re-measures 46 -> 47 / 46 -> 51 (reproducing rounds 765/766, which validates the re-implementation), with the pair on it is 66 -> 67 / 71 -> 76 — the same +1 / +5 and the SAME five lines. Queued as **(REL.4)**, with the `assertNever` narrowing family to close FIRST.
