_Round 766 (2026-07-29) — **(REL.2) THE `never`-PARAMETER GAP IS CLOSED, AND THE ROUND'S REAL OUTPUT IS THAT ROUND 765's DIAGNOSIS OF IT WAS WRONG IN A WAY THAT MATTERS.** **The fix is one exception to one discard.** Round 441's `never`-parameter arm in `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` has ALWAYS performed the flow read — round 765's "an enum argument going to a `never` parameter never gets the flow read at all" is WITHDRAWN; what it does is `if (n === neverType) n else ctxApplied`, throwing away any result that is not exactly `never`. Post-764/765 a bare enum's narrow there is a proper member subset, i.e. tsc's answer, so the enum case now keeps it behind the round-746 owner rule shared with the subtractive and assert directions. It cannot manufacture a diagnostic: the substitute is a NON-EMPTY union of enum members and nothing non-`never` is assignable to `never`, so the TS2345 that already fired still fires and only its DISPLAY changes. **The gate's population was measured BEFORE the widening** (temporary counters, since removed): of **26,432** reference call arguments on the compiler profile (43,563 on services) the arm is reached **5** times, of which **0** are enum-flavored — the widening is narrow by construction and **fires 0 times on all 8 profiles**, which is stated plainly rather than hidden behind a green grid. Round 765's two arms also stay **0 before and 0 after**, so closing this did NOT turn the negative-direction arm live, which was the stated hope when the item was queued. **AND CHASING THAT ZERO UPSTREAM IS WHERE THE ROUND EARNED ITS KEEP.** `emitter.ts:1527` is the exact shape (`phase: PipelinePhase`, `default: return Debug.assertNever(phase)`), so a zero was not credible. Three probes, no compile between them: a SAME-FILE `namespace Debug` reaches the argument gate; a plain exported function imported from another file reaches it; **an exported `namespace Debug` imported from another file does not reach it at all — `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` never runs for that call**, in both the direct-import and `export * from` barrel forms. **That is 1,108 `Debug.<member>(…)` calls on the compiler profile and 1,518 on services whose arguments are never checked** (441 `Debug.assert`, 313 `Debug.checkDefined`, 110 `Debug.fail`, 62 `Debug.assertNever`) — the single largest measured blind spot on the dashboard, and why rounds 764-766's arms all measure 0 there. NOT a general resolution failure: the flow-walk assert path DOES resolve a namespace member. Recorded and queued to be SIZED behind a scaffold, not taken. **The global rule re-priced with the fix on (scaffolded, measured, REVERTED — not landed): compiler 46 -> 47 and services 46 -> 51, UNCHANGED, same five lines.** Arm-A grid **46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 94**, every profile byte-identical to round 764; all **20 cost counters BIT-identical** (`narrow.walks` 71,326 — the walk already happened), no rebaseline; corpus letters E/C/N/S/T **3,363 / 0**; the 13-class filtered batch **120 / 0**; warning-clean. **Every pin run against unmodified `6fa1d630`: the 7 TARGETS FAIL and the 7 CONTROLS PASS** (+14, `EnumNeverParamNarrowingTest`); the two interface controls were REWRITTEN after a first attempt used an `if` BLOCK, which types its body from `currentLocalTypes` and never reaches the arm. The stale-control audit round 765's rule demands found the shape in two existing classes, both asserting on the CODE only, so both survive — and both now read `'E.C'` / `'Kind.B'`, an improvement their assertions are blind to. **Predictions 6 of 6 on values, and prediction 2 was RIGHT FOR THE WRONG REASON** — its stated mechanism ("tsc's `assertNever` sites are exhaustive") is false; they are never argument-checked. Scoring the mechanism rather than the number is what found the gap. Expect **13,174** from the full suite._

_Round 765 (2026-07-29) — **THE CONSUMER QUESTION ROUND 764 COULD NOT SETTLE IS SETTLED, AND BOTH REMAINING NEGATIVE-DIRECTION SHAPES LANDED.** **PART 1: the answer is (b) — the narrowed type IS consumed, by a real relation question, and every consumer agrees.** Round 764's subtractive narrow fires 45 times across the profiles and changes no output; it established verdict-neutrality and said plainly that its counters could not tell "never consumed" from "every consumer agrees". Built the instrument it prescribed (scaffolding, since removed): `enumMinusMembers` TAGS every type it produces with the enum it came from, and the three consumers of a type — `checkTypeRelatedTo`, `getPropertyOfType`, `typeToString` — ask their question TWICE when the input is tagged, counting "consulted" and "differed" apart. **compiler: 14 firings, 12 distinct products, 4 consumed, 4 relation consults, 0 DIFFER, 0 property lookups, 0 displays; services / server / harness: 31, 22, 7, 10, 0, 0, 0.** The consult trace shows the agreement is not luck — **the target of every consultation is the ENUM ITSELF** (`SyntaxKind`, `StructureIsReused`, `UsingKind`, `NameValidationResult`, `ScriptElementKind`), and a sub-union of an enum's members relates to that enum by union membership, so the two answers cannot differ. The 8-of-12 and 15-of-22 distinct products never delivered and never read are reported separately as the inert share. **The instrument was falsified before its zero was banked**: recording a deliberately bogus baseline moves `rel.differs` 0 -> 4 of 4 consults — and the FIRST perturbation attempt, which replaced the product so nothing was tagged, could not have shown the opposite and was discarded for exactly that reason. **Consequence: the rule must NOT be deleted** — it is verdict-neutral only because enum -> MEMBER is still vacuous, i.e. because of the leniency (REL.2) exists to close. **PART 2: the `asserts k is K.A | K.B` and switch `default:` shapes on a bare enum now narrow**, and round 764's "switch machinery, not enum-specific" label is WITHDRAWN — round 425's `DefaultClause` arm has narrowed a literal-union subject all along (measured on the unmodified build, now pinned), and the gap was its `t !is Type.Union` branch, which a member-LESS enum `Type.Object` falls into. The assert half is an ORDER question: the non-union tail asks `t <: targetType`, vacuously true for an enum against its own members. Both share `enumTargetsAreOwnMembers`, the round-746 owner rule factored out of `enumMinusMembers`. **Both arms fire ZERO times on the profiles** — measured with a temporary counter and said plainly, so the green grid is a no-regression gate rather than the round-753 non-evidence; the 11 local pins and the corpus are the evidence. Arm-A grid **46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 94** with every profile's error lines byte-identical to round 764; corpus subset E/C/N/S/T plus the new pins **3,374 / 0**; build warning-clean. **Every pin run against unmodified `6b956c12`: the 5 TARGETS FAIL and the 6 CONTROLS PASS.** Cost: 18 of 20 counters bit-identical, `globals.lookups` +0.06% / `globals.misses` +0.07% (the new per-case member-type resolution in a `default:` switch), rebaselined in the same commit. **PART 3: the global rule RE-PRICED (scaffolded, measured, reverted — not landed) and the price has fallen again — compiler 46 -> 47 UNCHANGED, services 46 -> 52 becomes 46 -> 51, and the worklist is 5 lines, not 6: `importFixes.ts:1162` has CLOSED** without having been on anyone's list, the second time this arc has found the masked population larger than the enumeration of it. The remaining five are `scanner.ts:905` (cause D, and the only compiler-profile line), `importFixes.ts:1127` and `formattingScanner.ts:113` (cause D), and `completions.ts:2234`/`2239` (the property-read / generic-inference family) — so (D) is 3 of 5 and is still an arc. **PART 2b (after the owner's suite: 13,161 / 1 failure / 3 skipped, cost gate clean): the one failure is a STALE CONTROL of round 764's, not a regression in meaning** — it pinned that a bare-enum switch `default:` still answers the whole enum, which is exactly the gap Part 2 closed. **RETIRED rather than edited**, because the shape is now owned in its correct form by `EnumAssertAndSwitchDefaultNarrowingTest > a switch default on a bare enum subtracts the cased member` (the same source, inverted expectation); suite count **13,161 -> 13,160**. The gates missed it because the filtered batch was corpus letters plus the round's OWN class — **rule now in CLAUDE.md: a round that closes a gap a previous round pinned as a control must put that previous class in its batch, and must not plant such controls itself.** The audit it forced found **no second stale control**: no test anywhere pins the `asserts` half (round 764 recorded it in prose only), and of the seven enum/switch classes with absence-style assertions, all but three switch on a PROPERTY subject that cannot reach the new arm, two of the three are exhaustive, and the last was measured through the CLI to be unaffected. **That measurement found something worth more than the fix: an enum argument going to a `never` PARAMETER never gets the flow read**, so `default: Debug.assertNever(k)` in a partially-covered switch still reports the whole enum — isolated over four variants, the discriminator is the parameter type, not the enum or the switch — and **that is a large part of why the new arm fires zero times on tsc's own sources, since `Debug.assertNever` IS tsc's idiom for that position**. Sized, queued under (REL.2), not taken (it widens a corpus-pinned gate). Ten affected classes re-run **73 / 0**. **Predictions 5 of 7** — the misses are Part 2's "the switch fix helps every discriminant" (falsified by one probe run and now a pin) and Part 3's "the price is unchanged". The full suite is the owner's to re-run; expect **13,160**._

_Round 764 (2026-07-29) — **TWO PARTS OF (REL.2), AND EACH ONE'S RESULT IS A MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN A FEATURE.** **PART 1: round 763's +4.78% flow-walk bill is returned in full — `narrow.walks` 74,729 -> 71,326, three walks above the pre-763 71,323.** The call-argument narrowability gate's enum arm is now a SECOND CHANCE: it pays for the walk only on the REJECTING path, because if the DECLARED enum already satisfies the parameter no narrowing can change any verdict. **99.9% of round 763's added walks were on the ACCEPTING path** — in tsc's own sources an enum-typed argument overwhelmingly goes to a parameter of that same enum. The idiom is round 743's (a second chance can turn a rejection into an acceptance, never the reverse) and it is scoped to the enum sub-case alone; the Interface / `unknown` / `string` / `number` arms beside it are corpus-pinned and keep walking unconditionally. _Round 745 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1)(c) STEP 1: the relation judges an enum by VALUE, and `checkEnumLiteralAssignments` (B203) is RETIRED in the same commit.** Corpus **12,971 -> 12,982 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (+11 pins), compiler profile `--listAll` **at 46, diffed line-by-line** (composition unchanged: TS2591x43 / TS2304x2 / TS2584x1, no TS2322), cost gate **+0.00% on all 20 counters** — no rebaseline. **THE ABLATION IS THE EVIDENCE, BOTH WAYS.** Before: disabling B203 through PassLab left `enumAssignmentCompat5` emitting **nothing** — the relation could not answer. After: the relation alone emits all three baseline lines **byte-identically** (message, position and length), and with B203 still enabled the run shows **6 diagnostics, each of the three duplicated** — exactly the co-emission round 740 predicted, which is why the rule and the retirement are ONE commit. **THE RULE IS tsc's, VERBATIM:** the wide `number` stays assignable to any numeric enum or member (the bit-flag compatibility rule, so `a = n` is still legal), but a numeric LITERAL is assignable only to a member whose value it EQUALS, or to any member of an enum whose domain is not fully known. That last clause is `enumValueDomainIsComplete`, and it is where tsc's *computed* enum lives: **a member with no initializer in an AMBIENT non-const enum has NO value in tsc**, so `declare enum D { X, Y }; let d: D = 7` is legal — we auto-number those because the Transformer needs a value to emit, and B203, which had no ambient notion at all, reported it. The retirement therefore **removes an FP** as well as adding four shapes B203's top-level-var scan never saw (function bodies, string enums, negative literals, `const enum`). **WHAT DID NOT WORK, and it cost a profile run: the message needs the source's literal VALUE, and the obvious way to get it — admitting enums to `propTypeContainsLiteral` — is WRONG.** That predicate has ~25 consumers and most are not about display: several choose `literalTypeOfExpression` over `getTypeOfExpression` for the SOURCE, so `languageVersion = undefined!` (parser.ts:1790) reached the relation as the pre-assertion `undefined` and the profile went **46 -> 50**. Split into `ts2322KeepsSourceLiteral` (display) and `enumNumericLiteralSource` (source, restricted to a `Type.NumberLiteral` by construction) it is 46 again. An enum-MEMBER target also prints QUALIFIED now (`E.A`, not `A`) — `formatTypeForDisplay` reduces a `QualifiedName` to its last name, which is right for a namespaced interface and wrong for an enum member. **STILL LOAD-BEARING, unchanged:** `checkEnumToEnumAssignments` (all 12 of `enumAssignmentCompat3`) and `checkNamespaceEnumUnionAssignments` (2 of 5 of `enumLiteralAssignableToEnumInsideUnion`) — both are enum-to-enum VALUE identity, a different rule from this one._

_Round 744 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1)(b) LANDED: an enum MEMBER now relates only to the SAME member, and ALL FIVE `@Ignore`s are ON.** Corpus **12,967 -> 12,971 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (+15 pins across three commits, -5 skipped as the `@Ignore`s flip); compiler profile `--listAll` **BYTE-IDENTICAL at 46** — the whole output, not just the count. **The two blocking profile FPs were BOTH enum-free, and both reduced on the first attempt once the model stopped copying the diagnostic message.** `declarations.ts:846` reported `T | undefined` vs `T | StringLiteral` and was really about neither: narrowing a bare TYPE PARAMETER by a type predicate REPLACED it with the candidate instead of intersecting, so the branch join lost `T` — the failing constituent was `NoSubstitutionTemplateLiteral`, and an EMPTY guard body reproduces it. `utilities.ts:4175` was an intersection SOURCE carrying a union not DISTRIBUTING (`A & (B | C)` denotes `(A & B) | (A & C)`), invisible until the union constituents are written in a DIFFERENT ORDER on the two sides, which is exactly what tsc's `getNameOrArgument` does. Both are pre-existing defects independent of enums — verified against an ablation build of pristine main — so they landed on main as their own commits BEFORE (b). (b) additionally RETIRED the round-459 AST enum-member key gate (its three original pins keep passing unchanged: the ablation evidence that the type path really answers), added a CONSTRAINT check to round 481's bare-`Type.TypeParam` overload lenience, deleted (a)'s string TARGET leg, and made B266 retract the general TS2322 it now co-emits with. **(c) IS BLOCKED, MEASURED NOT GUESSED**: a PassLab ablation census of the three AST-only enum passes shows every one uniquely load-bearing — `checkEnumToEnumAssignments` owns all 12 of `enumAssignmentCompat3`, `checkEnumLiteralAssignments` all 3 of `enumAssignmentCompat5`, `checkNamespaceEnumUnionAssignments` 2 of 5 of `enumLiteralAssignableToEnumInsideUnion`. All three are the VALUE-equality rule (a) deliberately did not model, so (c)'s first deletion needs a value-aware disjointness rule first. Cost gate: A and B +0.00% on all 20 counters; (b) rebaselined one — `narrow.memoServed` +3.16%, the round-459 retire's trade, against `globals.lookups` **-1.64%** as `canonicalEnumSymbol`'s consult disappears._

_Round 743 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1) PART 1 DONE: the overload picker was doing TWO things wrong, and neither is about enums.** Corpus **12,949 -> 12,956 / 0 failures / 8 skipped** (+7 pins), compiler profile `--listAll` **BYTE-IDENTICAL at 46** across both commits. **(1) The B136 concrete-overload SWAP re-picked an overload `resolveCallOverload` had ALREADY REJECTED.** When the chosen signature's return still carries an un-inferred type parameter, the swap looks for a non-generic overload with a concrete return — and took the FIRST such overload **without ever asking whether it accepts the arguments**, which is how `factory.createToken(SyntaxKind.ReadonlyKeyword)` selected the correct generic `<TKind extends ModifierSyntaxKind>` overload and was then swapped back to `createToken(token: SyntaxKind.SuperKeyword): SuperExpression` (checker.ts:7997). The omission was invisible for the Array predicate pair the rule was written for, where the non-generic overload accepts whatever the generic one does. The type-based verdict is now extracted as `signatureAcceptsArgs` so any site adopting a signature asks the SAME question. **THE REDUCTION NEEDED THE GENERIC OVERLOAD** — two same-arity NON-generic overloads distinguished only by an enum-member parameter already selected correctly via the round-459 AST gate, which is why round 742's two earlier reductions came back clean and blamed the barrel and the enum size. **(2) Overload selection could not see an ASSERT narrow.** `getTypeOfIdentifier` answers from `currentLocalTypes` and the declaration tables: an `if (isFoo(x))` narrow is recorded there, an `asserts` narrow lives ONLY in the flow graph behind `getNarrowedTypeForReference`, which every emission site opts into individually and selection never did — so `Debug.assert(isKeywordOrPunctuation(kind)); tokenToString(kind)` picked the `string | undefined` overload (parser.ts:2494). Fixed as a SECOND CHANCE on the rejecting path only, so it can only turn a rejection into an acceptance. **Both reductions are ENUM-FREE**, which is what makes the pins bite on main. Cost gate: +0.16% on commit 1; on commit 2 `narrow.memoServed` +9.93% and `typeNode.bypassed` +23.26%, **justified and rebaselined** — 87% of the extra narrowing requests are served by the round-664 memo (`narrow.walks` only +0.83%) and the wall is flat interleaved (26,098/26,593 vs 26,312/26,394 ms). **PART 2, (REL.1)(b): 4 profile FPs -> 2, still parked, now on `wip/round743-rel1b`** (all five `@Ignore`s flip ON there). Remaining: `utilities.ts:4175` (intersection-over-union distribution) and `declarations.ts:846` (`if (!input) return` failing to strip `undefined` from a `T | undefined` where T is a constrained type parameter). **Neither reduced — a hand-built model of each shape passes**, which per round 742's rule means a missing ingredient, not an absent bug. (c) the scaffolding deletions stay open._

_Round 742 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1)(b0) DONE: an enum-member EXPRESSION is a member type, not `any` — and that was the blocker under (b), not the relation.** Step (a) minted the member type but only ANNOTATIONS could reach it: an enum's own `Type.Object` carries no member table, so `getPropertyOfType` missed `E.A` and `computeRawTypeOfPropertyAccess` fell through to its `anyType` tail. That is why round 741 could flip three of the four `EnumMemberRelationTest` expectations from the relation but not `const e: E.X = E.Y` — the relation was being handed `any`, however sharp its rule was — and why round 740's `take(Ext.Dts)` control was vacuous. **Corpus 12,940 -> 12,949 / 0 failures / 8 skipped (+9 pins), compiler profile `--listAll` back to 46 (byte-identical output), cost gate max +0.35% on the first commit (rebaselined) and +0.00% on the second.** **THE AUDIT, NOT SITE-BY-SITE FIREFIGHTING.** Round 741's gotcha says every classifier whose rule is "an enum value is a number/string at runtime, never an object" must accept `Enum or EnumMember`; six more sites moved to `isEnumFlavoredObjectType` and the two arithmetic classifiers now answer PER MEMBER from the member's own constant. **Two were WRONG in a direction a mechanical widening would have missed:** `comparabilityCategory`'s blanket `EnumLiteral -> number` would have called a STRING member a number and FP'd a cross-category TS2365, and the discriminant filter's "an object can never strictly-equal a primitive literal" rule silently stopped excluding enums the moment a discriminant was a member type. **THREE PRE-EXISTING BUGS THE `any` HAD BEEN MASKING — fixed at the root, because they ARE the content.** (i) `isComparableType` never resolved a TYPE PARAMETER's constraint, so `token >= SyntaxKind.FirstToken` with `TKind extends SyntaxKind` FP'd 6 TS2365 (the sibling rule had always been in `isValidArithmeticOperand`; only the relational path lacked it). (ii) The arithmetic pass's first-wins local recording refused a nested body's own `const state = exception.state` because an ENCLOSING body's `let state: Identifier` was still in the INHERITED locals map — `spineArithInheritedName` decides inheritance by instance identity against the innermost saved map, so first-wins still holds within a scope. (iii) `checkVarDeclAssignabilityCore` has its OWN inline literal-widener, separate from `widenType`, and it is what the TS2322 assignment check reads as the target's declared type — so `let flags = TransformFlags.None; flags = TransformFlags.ContainsESNext`, the way tsc starts every flags accumulator, became an error; the same widening must also DISTRIBUTE over a union (`let variance = cond ? VarianceFlags.Invariant : ...`). Those two widening paths alone were **23 of the 28** FPs that (b)-on-top-of-(b0) showed. Member types also now print QUALIFIED (`SyntaxKind.Identifier`), as tsc does — the bare member name is also an unrelated interface's name. **(b) IS PARKED AGAIN, on `wip/round742-rel1b`, where ALL FIVE `@Ignore`s flip ON** (including the fourth, which (b0) unblocked, and the fifth, which the deletion of step (a)'s string TARGET leg unblocks — tsc rejects `string -> Ext.Dts`). **What blocks it is EXACTLY round 741's four FPs** (checker.ts:7997 TS2769, parser.ts:2494 TS2345, declarations.ts:846 TS2322, utilities.ts:4175 TS2322): that the count returns to FOUR after a change putting member types into every enum-member expression in the program is itself evidence for round 741's read that these are relation/narrowing gaps passing vacuously, not enum work. **Diagnosed, not fixed: checker.ts:7997 is an OVERLOAD-PICK bug with the enum as witness** — `factory.createToken(SyntaxKind.ReadonlyKeyword)` picks the FIRST overload (`token: SyntaxKind.SuperKeyword`) and returns `SuperExpression`; reduced to a standalone repro, two same-arity overloads whose parameters are DIFFERENT enum members pick the first, while the same pair distinguished by ARITY picks correctly. (c) the scaffolding deletions stay open._

_Round 741 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1) STEP (a) DONE: the enum-MEMBER type exists, and it changed no answer.** `SK.A` used to resolve to **`anyType`** — `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker` had no `SymbolFlags.EnumMember` branch — so `kind: SK.A` and `kind: SK.B` were literally the SAME `Type` instance and the relation was never asked a question it could get wrong. It now mints a distinct `Type.Object(Object or EnumLiteral)` per member, **interned on `"<canonicalEnumSymbol.id>#<memberName>"`** (a per-SYMBOL mint would hand two non-equal types to one member — the exact catastrophe `canonicalEnumSymbol`'s own doc records — because the same enum arrives as the merged global, a file-local and a barrel-resolved alias), and the enum's own type is flagged `Enum`, so **`TypeFlags.EnumLiteral` has a writer for the first time and the widening rule written for it at Checker.kt:143100 finally executes.** **Behaviour-preserving by construction and by measurement: corpus 12,935 -> 12,940 / 0 failures / 8 skipped (+5 pins, +1 `@Ignore`), compiler profile `--listAll` BYTE-IDENTICAL at 46 errors (whole output), cost gate max +0.40% (rebaselined, justified: unions that used to collapse to one `anyType` member now carry their real constituents).** The entire measured gap was **one rule family, enum member <-> its base primitive, in both directions** — and the mechanism is one line: `isNumericEnumObjectType`/`isStringEnumObjectType` classify by `SymbolFlags.Enum` on the type's symbol, which a MEMBER symbol does not carry, so neither pre-existing rule could fire. An ablation build (legs deleted) fails all four leg pins. The legs are deliberately **value-blind**: `let a: E.A = 2` where `A === 0` is still `checkEnumLiteralAssignments`' error, and the relation co-emitting it is the 4 spurious TS2322 round 740's probe measured. **The one knock-on was not union collapse but a CLASSIFIER: `typeof x === "object"` asks `symbol.flags.hasAny(SymbolFlags.Enum)`, false for a member, so `ModuleKind.ESNext` survived a narrow it must not survive (program.ts:1341 TS2339). Closed with `isEnumFlavoredObjectType` at both sites of that family — recorded as a CLAUDE.md gotcha, because every "an enum value is a number/string, never an object" classifier now has to accept `Enum or EnumMember`.** Both round-740 latent hazards closed too (`SK.A` was looked up under the bare `"A"` in `currentTypeParamScope`/`currentTypeAliasArgs` and in the round-444 `globals[name]` recovery). Also found: one of round 740's four positive controls (`take(Ext.Dts)`) is VACUOUS — an enum-member EXPRESSION types as the enum, never as a member — which is why the new pins annotate through `declare const d: Ext.Dts`. **(b) WAS attempted and is PARKED on `wip/round741-rel1b-disjointness`: the one-line rule flips 3 of the 4 `@Ignore`s ON but costs 4 profile FPs (46 -> 50) in one family — an enum-member union that used to collapse to a single `anyType` member now has real constituents, so a relation/narrowing path that passed VACUOUSLY stops passing (overload pick, assertion-narrow, intersection-over-union). The 4th pin cannot flip from the relation at all: an enum-member ACCESS EXPRESSION types as the ENUM, so only ANNOTATIONS reach the new type. And the obvious hypothesis for the FPs — one member splitting into several `Type` instances because `canonicalEnumSymbol` cannot canonicalize a module-scoped enum post-INV.3(d) — is measured FALSE: tsc's structural `isEnumTypeRelatedTo` verdict leaves the profile at the same 4.** (c) the scaffolding deletions stay open._

_Round 740 (2026-07-28) — **(PERF.HW) DONE: the cores are REAL and the item's premise is still wrong — a SEQUENTIAL xtsc run already consumes 3.15 of the 4 cores, so 79% of the machine is spoken for before the first worker exists.** The item asked "does this VPS have real cores, so M2 is worth reviving", with unpark condition ">= 8 real cores". **The box: `nproc` 4, AMD EPYC-Rome @ 2445 MHz, 4 distinct `core id`s with ONE thread sibling each (no SMT), NO cgroup `cpu.max`, and steal time 0.0 mean / 0 max over 72 vmstat samples.** Steal alone does not settle it (a hard quota need not be accounted as steal), so the cores were tested directly with a tiny-working-set pure-CPU loop: **1.00x / 1.56x / 3.45x / 3.61x at 1/2/4/8-way — four real, independent, unthrottled cores.** **THE SCALING TABLE** (compiler profile, `--noEmit`, -Xmx4g, 3 reps, ROUND-ROBIN interleaved across levels rather than round 666's blocks): **w1 27,126 ms | w2 24,452 (per-rep median -11.67%, wins 3/3) | w4 25,976 (-4.24%, wins 2/3, per-rep deltas STRADDLE ZERO = undecided) | w8 32,212 (+19.37%, wins 0/3)**, drift band re-derived from w1's own reps at **+-2.87%**. That reproduces round 666 (seq 27,873 / w2 24,669 / w4 flat) and adds the w8 point. **THE EXPLANATION, which no round had measured: 85.6 s of USER CPU for a 27.1 s wall = 3.15 cores in a run everybody calls single-threaded.** Attributed by starvation: `-XX:CICompilerCount=2` drops it to 2.34 cores / 62.8 s user (**JIT ~21.7 s of CPU**), `-XX:ParallelGCThreads=1 -XX:ConcGCThreads=1` to 3.06 (**GC only ~2.7 s**) — C2 compiling a ~110k-line `Checker.kt` never finishes inside a 27 s run. **Self time is FLAT across all four configurations (26.6-27.8 s), so the JIT is not stealing from the compile thread** (round 618's "flag hunting is dead" holds) — it is consuming the cores a WORKER would need, leaving ~0.85 free. Every level then saturates at the same **~3.6-core ceiling** (3.49/3.57/3.65) and what actually changes with worker count is TOTAL WORK: **user CPU w1 85.6 s -> w2 85.3 (+0%) -> w4 92.6 (+8%) -> w8 117.7 (+37%)**, because each worker re-binds every file and runs all ~318 collectors. w2 divides without adding and banks -11.7%; w4/w8 add CPU to a machine with none left, and it converts straight into wall. **NOT a JIT artifact — tested and negative: freeing ~0.34 cores at w4 (`CICompilerCount=2`, 3.62 -> 3.28 cores) left the wall unmoved (25,870 -> 25,574).** Four INDEPENDENT concurrent compiles took 3.85x each (aggregate throughput 1.03x) — which is **82% parallel efficiency** once the 3.15 figure is applied (4 x 85.6 core-seconds on 4 cores floors at 86 s; measured 105 s). **The box parallelises fine; our compile does not, because one copy already occupies 79% of it.** Amdahl: the w1/w2 fit gives P = 5,348 ms divisible (**19.7%**), R = 21,778 ms — infinite-worker floor **-19.7% (1.25x) ever**; the w1/w4 fit gives P = 5.7%, disagreeing 3.5x, so per the rule fixed before the run the model is contention-broken beyond w2 (and 19.7% vs round 666's 23% is NOT resolvable — P is twice a delta whose per-rep spread is 1,554 ms). **ALSO FOUND, a correctness gap: `--workers N` IS NOT BEHAVIOUR-PRESERVING — 62 diagnostics vs sequential 46.** The 16 extras are one family in one file (`utilities.ts:11349..11410`, TS2322 `EvaluatorResult<T>` not assignable to `EvaluatorResult`), **identical at w2/w4/w8** and deterministic across reps — the round-609 signature (a program-wide collector iterating the INV.6 partition view instead of `binderResults`), not a race. **VERDICT: M2 stays parked, and the unpark condition is REWRITTEN** — ">= 8 real cores" is necessary but insufficient; the measured requirement is >= 8 cores *net of the ~3.2 the JVM's own JIT/GC consume*, i.e. realistically **>= 12**, and that tax is FIXED per JVM (it does not grow with workers), so a larger host simply out-sizes it. Revival order: close the divergence, shrink R, only then re-probe. Predictions **5 of 6** (P5 falsified: w8 peak RSS 2,240 MB fits -Xmx4g easily, GC 1.1 s of the 5.4 s regression — **no level was skipped for want of RAM**; memory was never the constraint). No `src/` change -> no suite run (12,927 stands). Artifact: `docs/perf/worker-scaling-round740.md`. **PART 2, (REL.1) DECOMPOSED AND SIZED (fix deliberately NOT attempted): the blast radius everybody feared is ONE corpus baseline, and the root cause is not "the relation is lenient" — it is `anyType`.** The item said "every enum-typed comparison in the corpus goes through this, TS2322/TS2367/TS2345 baselines included". Measured: **12,927 tests, 1 failure.** **ROOT CAUSE LOCATED:** `getTypeFromTypeReference` reduces `SK.A` to the BARE member name `"A"`, resolves it to a `SymbolFlags.EnumMember` symbol, and `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbolWorker` **has no branch for that flag** — it falls to `else -> anyType`, so `SK.A` and `SK.B` are *the same `Type` instance* and the relation was never asked a question it could get wrong. **Corollary: `TypeFlags.EnumLiteral` is SET NOWHERE — all ~11 read sites are dead, including a widening rule at Checker.kt:143100 that is already written and waiting for a flag that never arrives.** **MEASURED with a throwaway 3-edit probe, then REVERTED** (proof: `git diff src/commonMain` empty + profile `--listAll` byte-identical at 46 errors + cost gate all 20 counters +0.00%): corpus **1 failure**, compiler profile **46 -> 52 (+6)**, **no perf cost** (26,192 ms in the 26.5-27.1 s band). **AND THE ONE FAILURE IS THE MISSING LEG, NOT THE ADDED ONE** — 4 spurious `Type 'number' is not assignable to type 'A'`: a numeric enum member must stay assignable FROM `number` and from a literal equal to its value. The profile's +6 is the same family (`Extension.Dts`, a STRING member, not assignable to `string`). **The entire measured gap is ONE rule family: enum member <-> its base primitive — so this is a SESSION, not an arc.** Decomposition written into the queue item: (a) mint the type + the base-primitive legs, behaviour-preserving by construction; (b) add disjointness (acceptance = the four `@Ignore`s flip on); (c) delete the scaffolding one walker per commit. **Deletable: `discriminantPropAnnotation`, `kindDomainProvesNotSubtype`, `kindDomainKeysExceed` and THREE whole AST-only passes** (`checkEnumLiteralAssignments`, `checkNamespaceEnumUnionAssignments`, `checkEnumToEnumAssignments`), plus twelve AST key-space helpers; **but `canonicalEnumSymbol` SURVIVES** — its duplicate-`Symbol`-instance problem is independent of the relation and a naive per-symbol mint reproduces the catastrophe its doc records. Two latent hazards recorded (`SK.A` is looked up under the bare name `"A"` in `currentTypeParamScope`/`currentTypeAliasArgs` and falls back to `globals["A"]` — invisible only because everything collapses to `any`). Suite **12,927 -> 12,935 / 0 / 7** (+8 `EnumMemberRelationTest`, 4 `@Ignore`d pins naming the item + 4 NOT-ignored positive controls that are the FP firewall for step (a))._

_Round 739 (2026-07-28) — **(BENCH.1) DONE: the yardstick was broken somewhere else, and round 738's own correction is RETRACTED.** 738 inferred that "every published xtsc-vs-tsc `--no-emit` ratio compared our check+emit against tsc's check-only, so the honest gap is ~2.15x". **Reading the scripts instead of assuming: `bench-3way.sh` runs xtsc, tsc AND tsgo WITH EMIT**, so the published 2.4x was already like-for-like and `skipEmitOutputs` (set only from `ProjectCompiler`'s own `noEmit`) cannot move it. 738's arithmetic applied our emit fraction while implicitly taking tsc's as ZERO — the ratio's FLOOR, not its value, and the error ran in the direction that flattered us. The gate does not move the published column, as predicted from the code path.** **HONEST PUBLISHED RATIO, with basis: 2.28x median over ALL 341 CI runs / 2.40x over the last 30, EMIT mode, wall clock — per-row spread 1.87x-2.72x, because xtsc is ONE cold JVM run per row against tsc's median of three, so no single row is the ratio.** SECOND BUG, same yardstick: the LOC parse also matched `throughput: N LOC/s` and took it, so **all 340 archived reports published THROUGHPUT as their LOC count** ("7,004 LOC" for a 194,702-LOC program). LANDED: `bench-3way.sh` now measures BOTH modes on all three compilers (`--modes`), LOC/wall parses fixed, `bench-history/README.md` restructured into a marked two-mode table over a labelled pre-739 archive, all 8 profiles re-baselined check-only (compiler **26,518 ms**) with a MODE-DISCONTINUITY block in each TSV — `emitted=0` alone does not distinguish "did not emit" from "emitted and discarded". NOT POSSIBLE HERE: the 3-way itself (no node/tsc/tsgo, no network — CI installs them), so the check-only ratio stays a BOUND until the next CI run; mixing a local wall against a CI tsc number would be the very error this round fixes. ALSO: **`bench/` is gitignored** — that "dashboard" is local-only. **And (FRONT.1)'s own headline, honestly: three estimates of the same quantity are 2,623 ms (phase), 2,298 ms (mode A/B) and -3,570 ms (738's cross-binary A/B, -11.42%); two of three cluster at 8-9%, so state it as ~8-9% with 11.42% the high end.** No `src/` change -> no suite run (12,927 stands). _Round 736 (2026-07-27) — **(CALL.3) DONE, and the M5 attribution arc has its FIRST LANDED WIN: `-4.53%` median wall on the compiler profile, B wins 6/6 interleaved pairs, outside the +-2% drift band by 2.3x.** Round 735 handed forward 394 narrowing walks costing 1,485 ms and forbade designing before two numbers existed; both were measured with a new opt-in probe (`NarrowSections`/`NarrowProbe`, `--narrowSections{,Coarse}`, behaviour-free when off). **(i) ARRIVALS vs DISTINCT: a `>= 1 ms` walk arrives at 1,900 flow nodes but only 214 DISTINCT ones — revisit factor 8.85 against 1.48 for a typical walk. The tail was never a bigger graph; it is the same small graph walked nine times. (ii) THE PER-ARRIVAL SPLIT: 51% of the entire narrowing population is `applyConditionNarrowing` (1,412 ms / 759,784 calls / 1,858 ns), and the tail's arrivals are 6.3x costlier purely because of their MIX — `FlowCondition` is 41% of tail arrivals against 18% overall, `FlowBranchLabel` 22% against 9%, while cheap `FlowCall` pass-throughs fall 57% -> 19%.** **THE CAUSE WAS ONE CONDITION.** The intra-walk memo `NarrowFlowMemo.served(id, depth)` answered only when `depth <= storedDepth`, so a flow node reached again by a LONGER path recomputed its whole antecedent subtree — **631,585 arrivals compile-wide, 426,753 of them at `FlowCondition` nodes, against 290,011 serves.** That condition guards exactly one thing (a deeper entry has less budget before `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH`=2000), and that is DECIDABLE, not approximable: `depth` influences the result through no other channel, and only non-truncated results are ever stored, so an entry now carries `hi` = the max depth its own subtree reached and serves a deeper probe iff `depth + (hi - storedDepth) < maxDepth`. The one non-obvious part: a served hit must fold `depth + servedHeight` into the CALLER's height or the disjunct goes unsound under nesting. Mirrored into `narrowTypeFromFlowFollowLoopEntry`. **EFFECT: invocations 1,455,915 -> 659,592 (-55%); arrivals 4,759,476 -> 3,500,214 (-26%) with DISTINCT nodes UNCHANGED at 3,212,764; `applyConditionNarrowing` calls 759,784 -> 333,031 (-56%); the `>= 1 ms` tail 429 -> 230 walks and its arrivals 815,259 -> 34,490 (-96%).** Distinct unchanged to the node is the signature of a correct memoisation change. Context that made the argument checkable: maxDepth reached is 249 of 2000 and depth/budget/cycle truncations are 0/0/0. **WHAT DID NOT WORK, both priced: (1) a "does this condition mention the name" pre-test — 95.6% of `applyConditionNarrowing` calls return their INPUT unchanged, but they are the CHEAP tail at 949 ns against 21,708 ns for the calls that narrow, so the whole population is ~410 ms net = 1.3%, INSIDE the band before the pre-test's own cost; (2) memoising the fast-forward chain's pass-throughs — `FlowCall` is 57% of arrivals and the biggest miss population in the table, and also the cheapest at ~131 ns each, so its entire revisit share is <=120 ms.** Corpus 12,899 -> **12,910 / 0 / 3** (+4 `IntKeyMapTest` height pins, +7 `NarrowMemoDepthTest`); profile `--listAll` **byte-identical A vs B** (46 errors); cost gate `output.errors`/`spine.nodes`/`narrow.walks`/`typeOfExpr.distinct`/`mapped.*` all +0.00% and **four counters FELL and were rebaselined in the same commit** (`typeNode.cacheable` -10.6%, `typeNode.cacheHits` -14.6%, `globals.lookups` -8.4%, `globals.misses` -8.5%, all downstream of the 56% cut). Full derivation: `docs/perf/narrow-walk-attribution.md`. Follow-on queued as **(CALL.4)**._

All 108 `src/lib` files are embedded now (566 KB → 3.71 MB of lib source; dom.generated alone is 2.35 MB, 66% of it MDN doc comments), and `keyToDistFileName` maps the `*.generated` SOURCE names back to the names tsc distributes (`dom.generated` → `lib.dom.d.ts`) so a lib-declaration diagnostic cannot print a file name that exists in no TypeScript distribution. **THE BUILD COST WAS REAL AND IS NOW PAID: all 3.71 MB in ONE generated file with ONE `buildMap { }` lambda fails with "Not enough memory to run compilation" after 7m34s at the 5 GB (BUILD.1) gives the Kotlin daemon** — the emission is split over 16 `RealLibFilesPart*.kt` of ~250 KB which the facade concatenates once and cuts apart by recorded char lengths, and a COLD `compileKotlinJvm` then takes 2m25s (under the ~6.5 min BUILD.1 records for the old single-file shape), so the split is not a workaround, it is cheaper. Corpus 12,857 → 12,876 / 0 / 3 (+19 pins). Cost gate PASSES with EVERY counter at +0.00%, and the dashboard is unmoved (compiler 46, services 46, harness 94): the bench profiles pin `"lib": ["es2020"]` and the corpus keeps the embedded lib, so neither requests a host lib. **THE DECISIVE PROBE IS A MEMBER PROBE** — "does `HTMLElement` resolve" passes just as happily when the whole DOM is missing, because an unknown name degrades to `any` and `any` is silent; all 6 targets (unknown member reported, member type honoured, method arity enforced, across DOM/webworker/scripthost) were verified FAILING on unmodified HEAD with all 7 controls passing. **One honest limitation found and pinned as such:** the DOM TS2339 target uses `Screen`, not `HTMLElement`, because the walker only reports a missing member once it can enumerate the receiver's complete member set and gives up on a lib interface with a heritage clause (the pre-existing B153 limit, FN-not-FP) — HTMLElement is pinned on the axis that does discriminate for it, the type of its own members. (LIB.1)(c) — report a REQUESTED lib that is unavailable — is the remaining leg._

The same builder also zipped the surviving symbols POSITIONALLY against the declaration list, shifting every parameter type by one. Round 460 had fixed both at the function-DECLARATION site only; the arity rule is now one `requiredParameterCount` helper at all 19 signature builders (16 were counting `this`).
