_Round 729 (2026-07-27) — **(LIB.1) IS DONE: the real-lib false-positive count is ZERO.** Arm C 16 → 13, and all 13 remaining are the env-legit TS2591 — the burn-down that started at 35 (round 717) is finished. Corpus 12,822 → 12,842 / 0 / 3 (+20 pins); cost gate PASSES UNCHANGED TO THE DIGIT at every step (largest counter −0.68%, an improvement, nothing rebaselined); embedded-lib compiler profile still 46. **Round 728's reading of parser.ts:3558 as a TYPE-PARAMETER NAME COLLISION is DISPROVEN** — instrumenting the inference showed the constraint check failing IDENTICALLY under both names (candidate `Intersection[TypeParam(T)#38, {m:1}]` vs constraint `NodeX` either way); the renamed variant was only ACCIDENTALLY clean, its equally-un-inferred return type swallowed downstream. The real cause is round 725's rule at a THIRD site: `tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs` answered "does the inferred type satisfy the callee's constraint" with a bare `checkTypeRelatedTo`, which cannot see through an intersection to its TypeParam constituent — and on failure it bails WHOLESALE, so the callee's return type comes back UN-INSTANTIATED and the mismatch surfaces as a TS2322 whose two sides print nearly the same text. **The second commit is the session's real find: `Exclude<T, U>` was an IDENTITY FUNCTION.** The distribution loop evaluated each constituent's branch under the UNSHIFTED alias-argument map, so `T` in a branch still meant the WHOLE union and every non-matching constituent handed it all back. Fixing it closed utilities.ts:4258 AND utilities.ts:12082 — the `assertType<never>` site round 728 failed to reproduce in two attempts, because `HasInferredType` is built with `Exclude`. **TWO COMPANION DEFECTS surfaced by the FIRST arm-C measurement of that change, neither optional:** (a) enum-member types do not discriminate in our relation AT ALL (`const k: SK.Identifier = SK.PrivateIdentifier` is silent), so sibling AST interfaces differing only in `kind` read as mutually assignable and a working `Exclude` promptly DROPPED them — `Exclude<PropertyName, PrivateIdentifier>` lost `Identifier` too, two brand-new FPs at factory/utilities.ts:1056/1061 — closed by applying round 472's `.kind` DOMAIN veto inside the conditional evaluator, with the underlying relation gap QUEUED, not fixed; (b) a USER type alias shadowing a lib one merges onto ONE symbol and the generic substitution path took `firstOrNull`, i.e. the LIB's, so a local `type Omit<T,K> = {[P in keyof T]: T[P]}` resolved through `Pick<T, Exclude<keyof T, K>>` — its own pin had been passing only because Exclude was inert. **THE METHOD, six-for-six: instrument the emission site and print STRUCTURE.** The display string lied again — `NodeArrayX<T>` on both sides of the TS2322 — and one dump of type ids, kinds and constituents named the true cause after a whole prior round of matrix-narrowing had named the wrong one. Note the wrapping trap: a `Checker.kt` stack-trace line number is mod 65536, so frame `:32118` is really line 97654. **A `git stash` A/B against unmodified HEAD is worth its two compiles** — it showed the discriminating control's MESSAGE changing (`NodeArrayX<T>` → `NodeArrayX<T & { m: 1; }>`), which is the only proof that inference BOUND the intersection rather than being waved through, and it showed three other "controls" to be vacuous on both sides._

_Round 728 (2026-07-27) — **THREE causes cracked in one session, and the three that remain are now ISOLATED IN LIB-FREE REPROS instead of described. Real-lib false positives 7 → 3 (arm C 20 → 16, MEASURED both sides; TS2349 ×2 → 0, TS2769 ×1 → 0, TS2322 ×3 → ×2, not one other code moved); corpus 12,804 → 12,822 / 0 / 3 (+18 pins); cost gate PASSES with the largest counter −0.68% (an improvement), nothing rebaselined; embedded-lib compiler profile still 46.** Triage-by-cause is now five-for-five, and again the histogram lied: three of the seven sites reproduced in 5–8 line synthetic probes on the FIRST attempt, none needing the real libs. **(1) TS2349 ×2** — the B516 union-of-callables gate required every parameter to be REQUIRED (`minArgumentCount == parameters.size`), so every lib method with a trailing optional — `forEach(callbackfn, thisArg?)` above all — fell through to the incorrect "none of those signatures are compatible with each other". tsc's `combineSignaturesOfUnionMembers` demands nothing of the kind: longest parameter list, position-wise intersection, `minArgumentCount` = the MAX. Only three corpus baselines pin that message and all three survive on other gates. **(2) TS2322 ×1 — round 718's defect in the mirror**: a mapped type has FOUR modifiers and the materializer recorded three (`readonly`, `-readonly`, `-?`) — never the plain `?`, so `Partial<T>`'s member of a REQUIRED source property stayed required and tsc's own `lookupFromPackageJson(): Partial<CreateSourceFileOptions>` was rejected for omitting `languageVersion`. The marker is a `SymbolFlags` BIT, not the id-keyed set its `-?` sibling uses: the arm that must consult it is the HOT one (every declared-required property reaches it). **(3) TS2769 ×1, found by a probe MATRIX** — five variants of `new Intl.Collator(locale, { usage: "sort", … })` showed single-call-signature, two-optional-params and single-construct-signature all CLEAN; only the OVERLOADED forms reproduce, because overload resolution compares each candidate against the RAW argument type while the single-signature path contextually types the literal. The `overloadingOnConstants2` rule now has a per-property analogue, evaluated only inside the already-failing branch (round 720/725's free shape). **THE THREE THAT REMAIN, now diagnosable rather than described:** parser.ts:3558 is a TYPE-PARAMETER NAME COLLISION — a four-case matrix pins it to "the argument's element type is an intersection whose TypeParam constituent SHARES ITS NAME with the callee's type parameter"; rename either side and it is clean, and `NonNullable` is incidental (`T & {}`), so round 726's reading of this site is disproven. utilities.ts:4258 is `Exclude<…>` never being evaluated outside `resolveAssertTargetTypeNode` (repro needs an INTERSECTION member; the gating trap is that `isLibOnlyTypeName` is TRUE for an UNDECLARED name). utilities.ts:12082 did NOT reproduce in two shapes — and the same file's other `assertType<never>` does not false-positive, so the discriminator is `HasInferredType` itself, not exhaustive-switch narrowing. **CONTROL DISCIPLINE:** cause (1)'s discriminating control runs in the direction round 727 recommended — combining makes `number & boolean` = `never`, so a bad argument must report **TS2345, a diagnostic that did not exist on HEAD**; a fix that makes a diagnostic APPEAR is far easier to prove than one that removes a class of them._

_Round 727 (2026-07-27) — **TWO families cracked in one session, both by triaging all eight remaining sites by CAUSE before touching anything. Real-lib false positives 14 → 7 (arm C 27 → 23 → 20, MEASURED at every step; TS2339 ×4 → 0 and TS2345 ×4 → ×1, not one other code moved); corpus 12,795 → 12,804 / 0 / 3 (+9 pins); cost gate PASSES with the largest counter −0.68% (an improvement); embedded-lib compiler profile still 46.** THE DEFECT: `narrowByCallPredicate`'s SINGLE-TYPE positive branch compared the reference against the guard target AS A WHOLE (`candidate <: t ? candidate : t <: candidate ? t : candidate`), so when the target is a UNION and neither whole-union relation holds it handed back the ENTIRE candidate union. tsc's `getNarrowedType` never compares against the whole candidate — it `mapType`s over it and keeps, per constituent, whichever of the two is the subtype, dropping constituents unrelated in both directions. The live shape is esDecorators.ts `visitAssignmentElement`'s `isAssignmentExpression(node, true) && isNamedEvaluation(node, …)`, where `NamedEvaluation` is a 9-member union of unrelated `X & { … }` intersections; a union's property set is the INTERSECTION of its members', so every later `node.left`/`node.right`/`node.operatorToken` FP'd. The fix filters the constituents on the previously-`else` path only (both relating branches stay byte-identical) and keeps the whole-union fallback when nothing survives. **STATED PLAINLY because it is invisible in the numbers:** in this shape exactly ONE constituent survives, and our TS2339 walker does not fire on an intersection (or plain-interface) receiver at all — so part of the suppression comes from a diagnostic class that does not exist. The narrowing itself is independently correct, dumped via revealing TS2322s, and where two constituents survive the result is a union on which TS2339 does fire; that is what the second pin exercises. **PROBE DISCIPLINE, first attempt useless:** two negative controls came back silent WITH the fix and read as "it deleted a diagnostic class" — they had landed on that same intersection gate and would have been silent on HEAD too. The discriminating control keeps TWO constituents alive, so a dropped constituent's property becomes legal while a property on only one survivor STAYS an error; both targets were then verified FAILING on unmodified HEAD. **THE SECOND FAMILY, same session, same method:** the TS2345 ×3 are `(x ||= new Set()).add(path)` — the REAL lib's `SetConstructor { new <T = any>(…): Set<T> }` let the CONSTRUCT SIGNATURE's own type parameter escape, so the receiver was `Set<Path> | Set<T>` and the B516 union-of-callables rule CORRECTLY intersected the two `add` parameters into `Path & T`; the combining rule was never the bug, the leaked TP was. Fixed by substituting an uninferred signature type parameter's DECLARED DEFAULT — TypeScript's own rule — only where one exists and only at the return reference's top level. The corpus cannot see it (the EMBEDDED lib's `SetConstructor` is non-generic `new(): Set<any>`), which is what made it safe; `new Map()` does not reproduce even under real libs, because `MapConstructor` has a non-generic `new()` overload that wins. Its pin's discriminating control runs the OPPOSITE way — with `new <T = number>()` the substitution makes a diagnostic APPEAR, which is far easier to prove than one that removes a class of them. **OPERATIONAL:** two builds lost to OOM under 1 GB available, one a full `jvmTest` that ran 7m31s and wrote ZERO result XMLs — read an empty results directory as "the build died", not as "everything failed"._

_Round 726 (2026-07-27) — **the TS2322 family cracked by DUMPING the relation instead of guessing a third time. Real-lib false positives 18 → 14 (arm C 31 → 27, BOTH SIDES MEASURED; TS2322 ×7 → ×3 and not one other code moved); corpus 12,788 → 12,795 / 0 / 3 (+7 pins); cost gate PASSES with the largest counter −0.68% (an improvement); embedded-lib compiler profile still 46.** THE DEFECT: `getTypeFromTypeReference` instantiated `Iface<A>` into a `Type.Reference` only when EVERY type argument resolved, and otherwise fell through to `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(Iface)` — the RAW OPEN GENERIC, which carries its OWN type parameter and is a different type from any instantiation of it. Nothing relates a `Type.Reference` to that, so every comparison against such an annotation FP'd. On the real libs the unresolved argument is the function's OWN type parameter: a return annotation is resolved with `currentTypeParamScope == null`, so its `T` comes back errorType — which is exactly why the message read `Type 'NodeArray<T>' is not assignable to type 'NodeArray<T>'`, identical text, because the DISPLAY renders the annotation while the COMPARISON used the raw generic. THE FIX mirrors tsc, whose `errorType` is Any-flagged and instantiates regardless: substitute `any` for the unresolved positions and instantiate anyway (the unresolved name is already reported by TS2304 — this only stops the cascade). Fixed sites: parser.ts:3583, watchPublic.ts:371/383, utilities.ts:12378. **WHY TWO ROUNDS OF GUESSING MISSED IT, and the transferable lesson:** the display string was the trap all along, and three separate synthetic probes (overloads, callback-parameter inference, cross-file generics, real libs on) came back CLEAN — the shape only becomes visible when the interface reaches its type parameter through a GENERIC BASE (`extends ReadonlyArray<T>`, real `NodeArray`'s shape); a flat `interface Box<T> { v: T }` relates to its own raw form anyway, so a pin built on one is silent BEFORE and AFTER and proves nothing. **THE INSTRUMENTATION LESSON that cost two compiles:** a debug helper reading a `private val` declared AFTER the class's `init` block reads NULL, because `init` runs the passes — the probe printed nothing and looked like "wrong code path". Hooking `Diagnostic`'s constructor instead named the true emission site in one run. **PINNED by three targets and four negative controls** — a resolvable-but-wrong argument still errors, an unresolvable argument does not mask an unrelated source, does not mask missing members (TS2740 still fires), and the good case stays silent._

_Round 725 (2026-07-27) — **round 724's confirmed defect FIXED, and it cost nothing. Real-lib false positives 22 → 18 (arm C 35 → 31, BOTH SIDES MEASURED; TS2344 ×4 → 0 and not one other code moved); corpus 12,781 → 12,788 / 0 / 3; cost gate +0.00% on all 20 counters; embedded-lib compiler profile still 46.** THE DEFECT: the real lib declares `NonNullable<T> = T & {}`, so a `Visitor<NonNullable<TIn>, …>` type argument resolves to `Intersection[TypeParam(TIn), {}]` — and `checkConstraintsForTypeArgs` applies the constraint chain only to a BARE `Type.TypeParam` arg (and, since 440-b, a `Type.Union` arg). Wrapped in an intersection the constituent's constraint is never consulted at all, which is why round 724's obvious hypothesis ("strip the nullish part of `Node | undefined`") was DISPROVEN by the case where TIn is already non-null: nothing to strip, same failure. The relation cannot cover it either — the engine has no TypeParam-source-via-constraint rule on purpose (round 456 measured adding one as net-zero and reverted it), so this is the THIRD arm of a per-emission-site rule that already had two. THE FIX evaluates only inside the already-failing branch, hence free: take each TypeParam constituent's constraint (`T ⊆ constraint(T)`) and compare it, with a SEPARATELY GATED second step dropping nullish members when an `{}` constituent is present, since `X & {}` is tsc's non-nullish marker. **THE TRAP, pinned by four negative controls:** a blanket `is Type.Intersection -> continue` passes every target case and silently deletes a real diagnostic class — controls cover `A & B` with no type parameter, `NonNullable` of an unconstrained parameter, `NonNullable` of a parameter constrained to an unrelated type, and a nullable-constrained parameter used WITHOUT `NonNullable`. Fixed sites: parser.ts:3491/3492, visitorPublic.ts:124/144; the 18 left are TS2322 ×7, TS2345 ×4, TS2339 ×4, TS2349 ×2, TS2769 ×1. **OPERATIONAL, correcting round 720's rule for subagents:** a subagent cannot wait on a detached build — `nohup … &` hides it from the harness and ending a turn terminates the agent rather than parking it; run gradle in the FOREGROUND, one command per call. Combining both spawned two concurrent builds racing on one output directory. And a `ps` pattern for a gradle client must match `gradle-wrapper.jar`, not `GradleWrapperMain` — the wrong one reports "nothing running" while a build is live._

_Round 723 (2026-07-27) — **the round-722 defect FIXED, and it took two false positives with it, not one. Real-lib FPs 24 → 22; corpus 12,775 → 12,781 / 0 / 3; cost gate tripped, justified, rebaselined in the same commit.** THE FIX: `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` named a computed key via `computedLiteralKey(n) ?: continue`, and that helper accepts only string/numeric literals — so `[Symbol.iterator]: …` was silently DROPPED (not mis-typed: absent) and the target correctly reported it missing. New `computedSymbolKey` names a DOTTED path as `[Symbol.iterator]`, matching how symbol members are named everywhere else (it is what TS2739 prints); wired into BOTH the PropertyAssignment and MethodDeclaration branches — the method form handled no computed names at all. **Deliberately narrow:** a bare `[foo]` dynamic key is still skipped, since naming it would let any literal satisfy a symbol-keyed target — pinned by its own control, because the over-broad version would have passed everything else. **It fixed MORE than predicted:** TS2739 ×1 → 0 AND TS2322 8 → 7, so one structural gap was surfacing under two codes — the by-code histogram overstates how many distinct causes remain in the other 22. **COST.1 did its job:** failed on `mapped.keyed` +3.12% / `mapped.hits` +6.66% (+744 keyed lookups, +372 hits, ≈1.6 ms) — the honest price of typing members previously discarded, accepted and rebaselined; the percentages look large only because that counter is small._

_Round 722 (2026-07-27) — **a CONFIRMED, isolated defect behind (LIB.1)'s TS2739, with a four-line repro and a live control, parked on `wip/round722-symbol-keyed-members`; main clean at 12,775 / 0 / 3.** THE DEFECT: tsc's `core.ts` builds a `Set<TElement>` object literal ending `[Symbol.iterator]: () => {…}, [Symbol.toStringTag]: …`, and we report TS2739 "missing `[Symbol.iterator]`, `[Symbol.toStringTag]`" — reading the source confirms the literal DOES declare them, so **computed well-known-symbol keys in an object literal are not registered as members**. The control that omits the key still reports it missing, so the probe discriminates. **SECOND HYPOTHESIS ELIMINATED:** an interface extending `ReadonlyArray<T>` IS self-assignable; with round 721's result, both explanations for the parser.ts:3583 / watchPublic.ts TS2322 are now closed — dump the real relation failure rather than guess a third time. **THE TIME-KEEPING ERROR, correcting my own rounds 718–721 notes:** a backgrounded `sleep` is PREEMPTED by the next command, so the "16-, 25-, 40-minute compiles" never happened — real time passes only when a wait is launched and the turn ENDS. Gradle started 01:48:27; after two nominal 20-minute waits the clock read 01:49:12. The same build then finished in **2m 57s**. What survives: the heap ladder was independently proven (4g fails with an explicit "Not enough memory to run compilation", 5g completes) — the heap really was too small AND my clock was wrong._

_Round 721 (2026-07-27) — **a NEGATIVE result that kills the most attractive hypothesis about (LIB.1)'s remaining 24 real-lib false positives, and the first fast build cycles since the heap fix (a filtered test run: 1m 56s). Corpus 12,770 → 12,775 / 0 / 3; no production code changed.** THE HYPOTHESIS looked strong: three of the eight remaining TS2322 print source and target as the SAME TEXT (`NodeArray<T>` → `NodeArray<T>`; `WatchCompilerHostOfFilesAndCompilerOptions<T>` → a union whose FIRST MEMBER it is), and "a generic reference does not relate to itself" would explain all three plus be a perf win. **It is wrong:** five probes — self-return, constrained self-return, member-of-union, return-through-a-local — all pass, with a live control that still errors. Pinned as `GenericSelfAssignabilityTest`. **THE SURVIVING LEAD is symbol-keyed members:** these FPs exist only under real libs, the same run reports TS2739 "missing `[Symbol.iterator]`, `[Symbol.toStringTag]` from `Set<T>`", and `NodeArray<T> extends ReadonlyArray<T>` carries exactly those in the real lib but not the curated one — so probe that under `@useRealLibs`. **OPERATIONAL:** an IDLE daemon from the previous build squats on its heap for a 2-hour timeout — 4.33 GB held while the next build tried to start, leaving 297 MB free and starving it; stop previous daemons before a from-scratch compile. And a `pkill -f <name>` matching your own waiting tasks kills those too._

_Round 720 (2026-07-26/27) — **the owner approved the (BUILD.1) heap raise and everything stuck landed at once. Corpus 12,765 → 12,770 / 0 / 3; real-lib false positives 35 → 24; cost gate reports ZERO movement on all 20 counters; embedded-lib profile unchanged at 46.** THE FIX (round 718's work, finally gated): `Required<T>` is `{ [P in keyof T]-?: T[P] }` and the parser recorded `-?` as a plain `?`, so it behaved as `Partial<T>` — inverted. The mechanism is not type-level: TS2722 gates on `isOptionalProperty`, and a homomorphic mapped member carries its SOURCE declaration, so the source's `?` is what the scan sees; fixed exactly as M1.10 fixed `-readonly`, with a `mappedRequiredMemberIds` side-channel probed only on the already-optional branch — hence zero cost. Real-lib arm C 48 → 37, TS2722 ×11 → 0, no other code moved. **BUILD.1 settled with a ladder:** 2g GC-thrashes forever, 3g dies mid-compile, 4g fails in ~6 min with an explicit `Not enough memory to run compilation`, 5g succeeds in ~6.5 min; Gradle's own daemon cut 2g → 1g because 5g + 2g oversubscribed the 7.7 GB box and the kernel killed the compile daemon silently. **TWO OF MY OWN DIAGNOSES WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED:** round 719 blamed a local bench loop (those commits are `github-actions[bot]` — remote CI), and rounds 718–719 recorded "40-minute compiles" that were ~6-minute ones **restarted by my own polling**, since each Bash call preempted the previous backgrounded one and killed the build with its shell. Rule: launch long builds DETACHED (`nohup … &`) and wait on ONE timer; if what you have awaited for 25 minutes is 90 seconds old, you are killing it._

_Round 718 (2026-07-26) — **a complete fix for 11 of (LIB.1)'s 35 real-lib false positives — diagnosed, written, and NOT LANDED, because four cold compiles in one session and none would gate it. Parked on `wip/round718-required-minus-optional`; main clean and unchanged at 12,765 / 0 / 3.** THE BUG: `Required<T>` is `{ [P in keyof T]-?: T[P] }`, and the parser records `-?` as a plain `?` — so `Required<T>` behaves as `Partial<T>`, inverted. `-readonly` got its own flag in M1.10; the `?` analogue never did. It costs 11 FPs because tsc declares `tracker: Required<Pick<SymbolTracker, "reportInferenceFallback">>`. **THE MECHANISM IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE:** TS2722 reads like a type question, but the emitter gates on `isOptionalProperty(propSym)` and the codebase deliberately never adds `| undefined` for optional-member access — what bites is the M1.10 trap repeating, a homomorphic mapped member CARRYING ITS SOURCE DECLARATION, so the source's `?` is what the scan sees. Fix is M1.10's mirror: a `mappedRequiredMemberIds` side-channel probed only when the declaration says optional. **TWO DEAD ENDS, BOTH CAUGHT BY CONTROLS:** hand-rolling the mapped types locally reproduces NOTHING (we emit nothing for user-defined mapped types — the controls came back empty and three target assertions were passing vacuously, and would have 'confirmed' no fix at all); asserting assignability through `Partial` measures an axis we do not model. Only `@useRealLibs` + TS2722 reproduces, verified against unmodified HEAD with the target failing and the control passing. **WHY IT DID NOT LAND:** `-Xmx2g` hung, `-Xmx3g` died and restarted after 16 min, only `-Xmx4g` got the main compile through — so (BUILD.1) is escalated from nuisance to the binding constraint on the edit-test loop, its proposal revised 3g → 4g, with a bigger box offered as the alternative. **Also:** the embedded lib declares NO utility types at all, which is why this family is invisible by default — the name degrades to `any`, and `any` is silent._

_Round 717 (2026-07-26) — **the two GATE items landed: logical parity became a MECHANISM with two build-failing controls, and cost became a counter diff. The determinism check on that gate caught a racy counter on its first use — the very counter (DISPATCH.1) was told to build its table from. Corpus 12,765 / 0 / 3, unchanged; generated corpus byte-identical.** **(PARITY.1):** `docs/logical-parity.md` carries the form-vs-meaning procedure as two ALLOWLIST tables (7 meaning axes / 6 form axes with their equivalence obligations; anything in neither is MEANING by default), and `logicalParityDivergences` in build.gradle.kts is the single source of truth — the generator emits a switched-off baseline `@Ignore`d so it stays VISIBLE as skipped (a vanished test hides behind an unchanged total), regenerates the ledger into the doc so it cannot drift, and FAILS the build on either rot mode: a baseline matching no generated test, or a `pinnedBy` class that does not exist. That second control is what makes "replace it with a test pinning the logic" mechanical rather than aspirational. Empty list is the healthy state and the generated tree is byte-identical with it. **(COST.1):** `scripts/cost_gate.py` — 20 deterministic `--passTiming` counters diffed against `docs/perf/cost-counters.txt`, failing above ±2%; counters, never wall time. Baseline: 46 errors, 856,962 spine nodes, 696,933 getTypeOfExpression calls over 250,057 distinct nodes, 69,903 narrowing walks, 89,883 bypassed type-node resolutions, 1,377,511 globals lookups at 98.9% miss. It also pins the compiler's ANSWER, because a cost drop that changes the output is not a win. **THE FINDING:** two runs of the same binary agreed bit-for-bit on 19 of 20 counters and disagreed on the AST census (857,350 vs 854,550) — `indexSourceFile` runs on the crawl's concurrent parse threads while `PassTiming.nodeKindHistogram` is a plain HashMap, so it loses increments and always undercounts; harmless for "which kinds dominate", fatal for the exact per-kind population DISPATCH.1 needs. Excluded from the gate, warned at the source, flagged on that item. **Plus a build trap worth half an hour:** the documented `pkill -9 KotlinCompileDaemon` ritual makes the next compile COLD, which does not fit the inherited `-Xmx2g` and HANGS rather than failing (350% CPU, RSS at the ceiling, zero classes written for 14 min); `-Dkotlin.daemon.jvmargs=-Xmx3g` finished it in 2m 33s — queued as (BUILD.1), owner-gated. Warning-clean restored (3 stale Checker.kt warnings)._

_Round 735 (2026-07-27) — **(CALL.2) DONE: the prior holds by 48x, and its supporting evidence named the wrong term.** `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` was split into 17 disjoint timed sections plus 8 nested sub-measures (`ArgSections`, opt-in `--argSections`, behaviour-free when off). The item's falsifiable expectation — "most of the 61 us is argument TYPE computation, not `checkTypeRelatedTo`" — **HOLDS: 924 ms of the function's 1,624 ms raw is the `argType` computation, against 19 ms for the whole `checkTypeRelatedTo`+TS2345 section (10 ms for the relation call itself).** But the prior reasoned from `getTypeOfExpression` (3,911 ms, recompute x2.7), and **inside this function that is only 196 ms (12%) while FLOW NARROWING is 600 ms (37%)** — 9,615 walks at 62 us each (B469 union-arg site 284 ms/2,339; M3.4 site 316 ms/7,271), of which **86% (8,299) return the INPUT type unchanged for 237 ms**. **So (CALL.2) does NOT reach ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK 0.1 stage 3 — this function types each argument exactly ONCE and makes 5.3% of the compile's `getTypeOfExpression` calls at the compile-mean cost, so it is not a recompute site. It reaches stage 4.** **THE ROUND'S REAL PRODUCT is compile-wide: of 70,037 narrowing walks, 394 (0.56%) cost 1,485 ms — 47% of ALL flow narrowing and 4.9% of a 30.5 s compile**, the first single target measured ABOVE the +-2% drift band (~610 ms) since round 731, by 2.4x (WK_NARROW 336, WK_NARROW_LOOP 47, WK_BASE_EXPR 11). **Three mechanisms for those 394 were hypothesised and ALL THREE are disproved by the same run:** they do not TRIP (`narrowWalk tripped: 0`), they do not exhaust the 1,000,000-visit budget (630,641 arrivals total, 1,601 each, max 19,515), and the walk memo cannot reach them (`walkMiss cold=69,968` of 70,037 — essentially every walk is a first sighting). What is left is arithmetic: all walks average **372 ns per node arrival**, the monsters **2,354 ns** — 13x more arrivals AND 6.3x more expensive arrivals. **NOTHING was optimised**, per the same restraint as rounds 733/734. Also recorded: only **10,146 of 38,247 loop iterations (27%) ever reach the assignability check**, yet all 37,379 pay the full `argType` computation, because every intervening block consumes it. **Calibration, third round running: the in-situ unrolled empty span read 391 ns against a differential of 89 ns — 4.4x too high** (734 saw 306 vs 86); the fix is now a MODE, `--argSectionsCoarse` (anchors only), so ON-vs-COARSE prices a boundary rather than a code path — total probe inflation 55 ms of 1,624 = 3.4%. Follow-on queued as **(CALL.3)**. Full derivation: `docs/perf/argument-check-attribution.md`. Corpus 12,892 -> **12,899 / 0 / 3** (+7 pins); cost gate all 20 counters +0.00%; profile `--listAll` identical in production, `--argSections` and `--argSectionsCoarse` (46 errors)._

_Round 737 (2026-07-27) — **(TYPE.1) DONE: ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK section 0.1 STAGE 3 IS STRUCK — its MECHANISM is exactly right and its SIZE is wrong by 3.2x.** The 701,463 `getTypeOfExpression` calls were attributed BY CALLER for the first time (`--typeOfExprCallers`, opt-in, behaviour-free when off; only the OUTERMOST call walks the stack, so a whole expression subtree is attributed to the handler that ASKED for it and the recursion cannot inflate a caller's own factor). **The claim "several handlers independently type the same node" is CONFIRMED and pervasive: 177 sites initiate typing, 45.2% of the 254,069 typed nodes (114,750) carry MORE THAN ONE origin — modal count three, maximum 17 — and 75.8% of all calls land on them. The x2.76 factor decomposes as 2.05x CROSS-HANDLER times 1.34x recursion, and per-caller factors are 1.00-1.11 everywhere: no handler re-types anything by itself.** **But the money is not there.** A PERFECT per-node cache — the ceiling for this stage in ANY shape — saves **823 ms = 2.9% of a 28.7 s compile**; single-visit discipline **670 ms = 2.3%**; the largest handler-pair merge in the compiler **166 ms = 0.58%** against a re-derived +-2% band of ~590 ms; and the SOUND memo re-measures at **46 ms**. **NOTHING WAS LANDED**, correctly. **Two corrections forced: (1) the often-quoted "getTypeOfExpression = 3,911 ms" is a DOUBLE COUNT** (`typeOfExprNanos` charges a subtree once per nesting level) — the true total cost of all expression typing is **2,439 ms = 8.5% of checker-init**, which was always this stage's ceiling; **(2) 74.4% of the calls are OUTERMOST**, so recursion never was the explanation. **Section 0's law again, in its sharpest instance and with no cache in it: sorted by COUNT the co-occurrence head is 141,388 repeat typings worth 71 ms (0.5 us each) — the property-access receiver trio at 90,948 repeats for 51 ms, `getTypeOfObjectLiteral` re-entering itself at 30,040 for 6 ms — while sorted by TIME the head is 2,603 repeat typings worth 166 ms (64 us each). The two orderings share nothing; what is redundantly recomputed is what was already cheap.** Prediction scored: P1 FALSIFIED (>50% nested; really 25.6%), P2 HELD, P3 HELD (top-3 pairs 41% of the redundant time), P4 FALSIFIED (top-5 origins 52.5%, not >=60%). Method note: the probe costs **16.7 us per attributed outermost call** (`StackWalker`), 190x round 735's 89 ns timestamp read, so the round is deliberately COUNT-heavy — every deterministic counter is byte-identical to the plain run and to `cost-counters.txt`. Corpus 12,910 -> **12,916 / 0 / 3** (+6 `TypeOfExprCallerAttributionTest` pins); profile `--listAll` byte-identical production vs attribution (46 errors); cost gate all 20 counters +0.00%. Full derivation: `docs/perf/type-of-expression-attribution.md`. Follow-on queued as **(TYPE.2)** — `checkVarDeclAssignability` under `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor`, the largest single typing origin (431 ms over 11,933 initializers = 36 us each) inside the third-largest spine handler (2,900 ms) that no round has opened._

_Round 747 (2026-07-28) — **(REL.1)(c) STEP 3: the relation PRINTS the enum, and B463's TS2416 (`encmCheckClassesAndOverloads` piece B) RETIRES with the display rule.** Compiler profile `--listAll` **at 46, diffed LINE-BY-LINE** against round 746's (composition unchanged: TS2591x43 / TS2304x2 / TS2584x1, no TS2322); filtered suite over the SIX generated classes that carry every TS2416 baseline plus the new pins — **3,910 tests, 0 failures**, `interfaceExtendsClassWithPrivate2` included; +8 pins. **THE ABLATION.** `enumAssignmentCompat7`: 3 with the pass, **1 with it PassLab-disabled pre-747 and that one printing `(param: E) => void` on BOTH sides** (the verdict was already the relation's since 746 — only the display was not), **1 byte-identical to the baseline with the rule alone**, **4 with both live and the guards removed — the TS2416 DUPLICATED, both copies byte-identical**, and **3 byte-identical once piece B is deleted** (message, chain, position, length). **THE RULE IS ONE FLAG.** `enumDisplayFullyQualified` is tsc's `TypeFormatFlags.UseFullyQualifiedType` restricted to enum names and consulted by `typeToString` at ANY depth, so an enum nested inside a rendered FUNCTION type qualifies too; `enumQualifiedRelationDisplays` is the `getTypeNamesForErrorDisplay` retry for a pair that is not itself enum-flavored — same string on both sides ⇒ re-render both — SPLIT from round 746's `enumCollisionQualifiedDisplays` because that one is fed displays which may come from ANNOTATION TEXT. It is **self-gating**: with no enum in the pair the re-render returns the same string and the caller keeps its originals, which is why a colliding INTERFACE pair is deliberately untouched. Applied at BOTH relation errors of the chain, because tsc retries at each independently. **Retiring it also removes a DIVERGENCE**: B463 rendered every enum through `EnumAsgInfo.qualifiedDisplay`, which qualifies UNCONDITIONALLY, so it printed `(param: third.Other) => void` where tsc prints `(param: Other) => void` — measured as a pin that fails on unmodified `fa3308a4` in the OTHER direction, the third retirement in this arc to also remove a wrong output. **B583 DID NOT RETIRE, and round 746's read of it is CORRECTED: it is blocked on RESOLUTION, not on a display rule.** Ablated, the relation reproduces SIX of `enumAssignmentCompat6`'s eight diagnostics byte-for-byte and emits NOTHING for the other two — because an `enum` declared inside a function/arrow body is never conventionally bound (B83.5) and is therefore invisible in TYPE position: a unique name degrades to `any` (silently — the INV.4(c)(iii) family still finds it through the INV.2(c) lexical scopes, so there is no TS2304), and **a name that SHADOWS an outer one resolves to the OUTER symbol**, which is a wrong answer rather than a missing one. `import("f").DC` is the SECOND blocker behind that. Measured ownership: over the same 3,904 corpus tests, B583 PassLab-disabled fails exactly ONE — `enumAssignmentCompat6`, and only its two `f.ts` lines. Both halves pinned (`EnumShadowedInFunctionScopeTest`), the retirement target landed early._

_Round 760 (2026-07-29) — **THE 8x2 DASHBOARD RE-MEASURED END TO END FOR THE FIRST TIME SINCE ROUND 730, AND IT BARELY MOVED — five profiles are unchanged CODE FOR CODE across ~50 commits.** Arm A (embedded libs, `types: []`): compiler / tsc-cli / jsTyping / deprecatedCompat / typingsInstallerCore **46** (`TS2591x43 / TS2304x2 / TS2584x1`, composition identical to round 730), services **47**, server **47**, harness **95**. **The only movement anywhere on the grid is ONE line, not three**: services / server / harness are exactly the three profiles containing `src/services`, and each carries the byte-identical `completions.ts:2237` TS2345 — UP, so a regression (tsc reports zero on its own source). Every other line on the grid, both arms, is env-legit. **Arm C is recorded with its caveat rather than compared**: round 730 measured it against the real `@types/node` v22.20.1, `bench-compile-tsc.sh` `rm -rf`s that directory on any run without `--node-stub`, and there is none on this box — this round's arm C uses the all-`any` STUB, which can only suppress, so 13 -> 1 is a FLOOR and is NOT claimed as an improvement. **The reduction is 12 lines and the fix is not the reduction.** Four ingredients, each shown necessary by ablation: an ENUM discriminant, TWO guards on one reference, an EARLY RETURN in the first, the guards declared in ANOTHER file. Measured with a `const s: string = node` probe (a washed reference is SILENT, because `never` is assignable to `string`): after `!isMod(node)` the type was `never` and is now `Node`; inside the next guard it was `never` and is now `Ident`. **Round 753's stated premise is falsified, and the comment saying so was written the same round** — `getDeclaredTypeOfEnumMember` mints member types with NO members, so `Node <: Mod` relates VACUOUSLY; round 472 vetoed the resulting `never`-collapse, round 753 deleted the veto claiming (REL.1)(a)/(b) had taught the relation to decide for itself. It had, for member-vs-member; `checkTypeRelatedToCore` already recorded that the enum-vs-MEMBER direction was left open. `enumMemberDomainProvesNotSubtype` restores that one verdict at that one site. **The global rule was WRITTEN, COMPILED, MEASURED AND REVERTED — a sized refusal with the size**: closing enum -> MEMBER properly costs **compiler 46 -> 52, services 47 -> 57**, because the vacuous `true` masks that many flow-narrowing gaps (a guard used as a TERNARY CONDITION; `===` narrowing across `||`), so the unit of work is the NARROWING FEATURES, not the relation rule — queued as (REL.2) with all six sites. **The real root cause is a SUBSTITUTION defect and it is not enum-specific — queued as (REL.3)**: `interface FwdA extends Fwd<K.A>` where `Fwd<T> extends Box<T>` resolves `.v` to **`any`**, while `BoxA extends Box<K.A>` resolves it to `K.A` — one heritage hop substitutes, a second hop through an intermediate FORWARDING its own type parameter does not, which is tsc's `AbstractKeyword -> KeywordToken<TKind> -> Token<TKind>` exactly, so every keyword node's `kind` is `any`. That is why the landed veto leaves the profile line in place and the 8 profiles are byte-identical — stated as a LIMIT ON THE EVIDENCE, not a credential. **The emit path, un-exercised for 22 rounds, is confirmed working**: the compiler profile with emit ON writes **78 `.js` files**, exactly the `emitted=78` of every pre-738 TSV row, with the same 46 diagnostics; tree sha256 recorded in the artifact. Every pin run against unmodified `aef21e76`: **three TARGETS FAIL, three NEGATIVE CONTROLS PASS**. Filtered batches **858 + 1052** hand-written and **the ENTIRE generated corpus, all 25 classes**, 0 failures; the two changed files compile warning-clean. **Predictions 3 of 6** — the two misses are the ones that moved the map. The full suite and the cost gate are the owner's to run. Artifact: `docs/perf/dashboard-round760.md`._

_Round 763 (2026-07-29) — **(REL.2)(B)+(C) LANDED, AND BOTH LABELS ARE AGAIN SHARPER THAN THEY READ: (C) IS NOT A DECOMPOSITION AT ALL, AND THE TWO `completions.ts` LINES ROUND 762 FILED UNDER (B) ARE NOT (B).** The scaffolded global-rule price falls **compiler 46 -> 52 becomes 46 -> 47, services 46 -> 57 becomes 46 -> 52** — **5 of the 11 unmasked worklist lines closed** (`parser.ts:2629`, `3762`x2, `8444`, `8728`), and the compiler profile is now ONE line away from the rule being free. Arm-A grid **46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 46 / 94**, every profile's error lines **byte-identical to round 762**; corpus all 25 generated classes **8,837 / 0**; filtered narrowing/enum/guard/switch batch **918 / 0**; build warning-clean. **The global rule was NOT landed** — it was scaffolded behind a temporary flag purely to re-price, and removed. **(C) IS NOT ABOUT DECOMPOSITION.** An enum's own type is a member-LESS `Type.Object`: it is neither a `Type.Union` nor a `Type.Interface`, so it fell through the call-argument site's NARROWABILITY GATE and the flow read was never attempted. `narrowByCallPredicate`'s single-type arm answers `K.A | K.B` correctly the moment it is reached — the fix is one clause in one gate, not a member union. That is why an `if` BLOCK narrowed an enum correctly all along (its body types from `currentLocalTypes`) while a ternary, an `&&`, an early-return guard, an assert call and a `switch` all failed: those are exactly the positions that need the flow read. **(B) IS INDEPENDENT OF (C), which round 762 said it was not**: `k === K.A` fails even when the reference is ALREADY declared `K.A | K.B | K.C`, so it never needed a union to filter — `literalTypeOfExpression` simply has no answer for an enum-member reference, and `narrowUnionByLiteral`'s two comparators had no enum arm. **THE INSTRUMENT WAS THE ROUND.** Five probe generations narrowed it to "flow fails for a non-union enum, succeeds for a union", and every structural reading of the guard code said that was impossible — a one-line trace then showed `narrowByCallPredicate` was **never called** with the enum type at all. The lesson is the one this arc keeps paying for: when the code says the answer should be right and the output says it is wrong, the missing step is upstream, and a `println` settles in 90 seconds what reading cannot. **`completions.ts:2234`/`2239` did NOT close and are not (B)** — 2234 is a property read on a union of generic instantiations (`Modifier.kind` answers `SyntaxKind`, the constraint of `Token<TKind>`), 2239 is generic inference through `tryCast` losing `KeywordSyntaxKind`; neither is narrowing. So the remaining worklist is **4 x cause (D) + 2 property-read/inference lines**, and (D) is still the arc it was sized as. Every pin run against unmodified `d47ffdc0`: **9 TARGETS FAIL, 5 NEGATIVE CONTROLS PASS**; +14 pins (`EnumFlowNarrowingRel2Test`), all reading the narrowed type out of a PRIMITIVE probe target so silence can never be mistaken for narrowing. **Predictions 4 of 6.** The full suite and the cost gate are the owner's to run._

The 403 ms estimate is **not confirmed at the wall**; the deterministic counters are the whole measured result. Worth carrying: a warm rebuild is 11.9 s against a ~27 s cold compile, so a saving the JIT has already compiled is a smaller FRACTION of a warm run than of a cold one. _Round 794 (2026-08-01) — **(ENGINE.2f): THE UNION LOOP-ENTRY RETRY IS SUBSTITUTED, NOT SKIPPED — 916 OF ITS 1,859 CALLS SERVED FROM THE PLAIN WALK'S OWN RESULT, `narrow.walks` −916 EXACTLY, ~30 ms (0.11%) — AND THE ROUND'S NEGATIVE RESULT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX.** The prize was re-measured at HEAD before any code (law 1) and had survived rounds 792-793 intact: **1,859 reaching calls, 66 ms, 49% loop-free**. Each is now `pass("init:<name>")`, so the partition is **exhaustive BY CONSTRUCTION** (round 801's bind shape — the residue is still printed and must stay ~0, so nothing needs calibrating). **The row falls 975 → 144 ms and `init:buildFileLocalTypeMaps` alone is 636 ms = 65% of the phase and 2.2% of the compile**, against 89 ms for `trackAllImportReferences`, 36 ms for `computeAllEnumValues`, and under 2 ms for eleven of the sixteen rows; it is also the ONLY setup pass doing type-system work (80 `getTypeOfExpression` calls, every other zero — pinned as a shape). **NO LEVER LANDED ON IT, DELIBERATELY**: `getTypeOfSymbol` MEMOISES, so a deferral MOVES the work (round 788's law, which cost round 801 its own lever), and the census is queued as (SETUP.2) with the falsifier written — if `distinct` does not fall faster than `calls`, the item closes. **THE SECOND RESULT: HotSpot's `DontCompileHugeMethods` is a PRODUCT flag defaulting to `true` with `HugeMethodLimit` = 8,000 bytecodes, and a static `javap` census (`scripts/huge_methods.py`, landed) finds 19 of 13,910 methods OVER it — so they are NEVER JIT-compiled and run interpreted for the whole process.** `checkMemberAccessMissingCore` **46,567** (5.8×), `checkArgumentsAgainstSignatureCore` 23,890, `checkVarDeclAssignabilityCore` 19,296, `checkAssignmentExpressionCore` 18,100, `checkSingleCallExpressionTypesCore` 15,567, `checkPropertyAccessInExpr` 9,062 — **and `forEachChild` 9,750**, the traversal primitive all ~400 tail passes run through. **Rounds 787–800 opened five of those one at a time and each concluded "no concentration"; a uniformly interpreted function is exactly that.** `-XX:-DontCompileHugeMethods`: 4 interleaved pairs, **A 25.448 s vs B 24.655 s = −3.1%, B wins 4/4**, all eight runs at 46 errors; arm A sd 0.81%, **arm B sd 1.46% — above the ~1% quietness criterion, so the SIGN is certain and the MAGNITUDE is ±~1.5%**, quoted as (JIT.1)'s upper bound and NOT as a banked win. _Round 822 (2026-08-04) — **THE BOX CHANGED (4 cores / 7.7 GB -> 8 cores / 15.6 GB, swap still ZERO), NATIVE BUILDS WERE RE-AUTHORIZED BY THE OWNER, AND THE KOTLIN/NATIVE GATE IS LIVE AGAIN AFTER 10 DAYS DARK — WITH A FOURTH DRIFT AXIS THAT IS THE ONLY ONE NOT SELF-ANNOUNCING.** Owner decisions first (`9d0694b9`): **(JIT.2) NO launcher flag** — the -3.1% that motivated `-XX:-DontCompileHugeMethods` was measured when 19 methods sat over HotSpot's limit and round 821 took that census to 0, so it now buys nothing and would only make C2 compile a 46,000-byte method; what IS approved is **(JIT.2a), a round MEASURING the JDK 25 AOT cache**. **(JIT.3) WON'T DO** — `HugeMethodLimitTest` already runs the identical whole-program census inside `jvmTest`, so `check` is covered transitively. **THE HEAP FLOOR IS 4g, FOUND BY PROBING DOWN RATHER THAN INHERITING ROUND 772's 6g** — K/N compiles inside the GRADLE daemon, so the binding setting is `org.gradle.jvmargs`, which (BUILD.1) had cut to **1g** for the old box: **that 1g, not the hardware, was starving native, and what dissolved round 772's link blocker is heap going DOWN, not up** (4g is observed-to-succeed, NOT proven minimal — 3g was never probed; and the real margin is outside the heap, the daemon reaching **8.5 GB RSS against a 4g cap** because konan/LLVM allocates there). **DRIFT WAS 17, NOT THE ~169 OF ROUND 682**: axis 1 **17 violations in 7 files, every one the SAME character — a comma** (zero `(`, `)`, `&`, `@`); axes 2 (`kotlin.assert`) and 3 (JVM-only stdlib) **both 0**, structurally closed because agents copy neighbouring idiom, while `,` survives as the one illegal character occurring naturally in English prose and CLAUDE.md's advice named *parentheses*. **THE FIND — A FOURTH AXIS THAT INVERTS THE GATE'S RISK PROFILE:** the three documented axes are COMPILE-TIME and each announces itself as a build error; the fourth compiles clean and **KILLS THE TEST PROCESS**. A `commonTest` driving multi-thousand-frame recursion overflows an uncatchable native stack — `runWithDeepStack` is a pass-through there and `StackOverflowError` is a never-thrown stub, so the `init` boundary guard that converts an overflow into TS2589 (the very invariant those tests pin) is INERT — and **every alphabetically-later class then silently never runs: the first full run lost 4,414 of 13,689 tests to ONE test**. Three pins moved to `src/jvmTest`, nothing weakened. **DEPTH IS NOT THE PREDICATE** — 30,000-term chains, a 3,000-statement flow chain, a 60k-entry `IntKeyMapTest` and the corpus's 6,452-term `binderBinaryExpressionStress` all PASS natively; what crashes is what RECURSES. **This is also a PRODUCTION defect on native, queued as the P0 (NATIVE.1)** — a native `runWithDeepStack` actual (large-stack pthread), which must carry INV.6(6c0)'s thread-local id write-back or round 607's 51 silent corpus failures return. **MEASURED:** `compileKotlinLinuxX64` 2m06s, `compileTestKotlinLinuxX64` 1m39s, **`linuxX64Test` 25m33s then 21m01s at 13,689 / 0 / 3**, peak system 12.5 GB, floor available 3.14 GB. **JVM gate 13,803 / 0 / 3, exactly baseline.** Commits `52c94887` / `fdc0c84c` / `6e448818` / `a2ed6e41`._

Training: **28.06 s / 48.8 MiB** (one ordinary compile + ~2.9 s assembly). **RECOMMENDATION: ship, gated on ONE condition — invalidation is ours to build; queued as (JIT.2b) BLOCKED-PENDING-USER.** Residue stated, not implied: emit mode, the other 7 profiles, the corpus suite under a cache, cross-JDK/cross-machine portability, `--workers 4` training. _Round 834 (2026-08-05) — **OWNER DIRECTION: CONFORMANCE BREADTH. THE `object` KEYWORD GETS A REAL RELATION RULE: the one rule the whole compiler held about `object` was tsc's WRITTEN AS ITS NEGATION, which is a different predicate, and correcting it plus the three gates that hid the corrected verdict turns **two more `nonPrimitive` cases GREEN (9 -> 7)**. **THE CATEGORY STILL CANNOT BE ADOPTED, SO IT WAS NOT** — and the honest re-costing below says it is not a one-round category either. Suite **13,847 / 0 / 3** (13,833 + 14 pins, 0 regressions); **all 8 profiles byte-identical against a REBUILT before-arm binary**._

_**THE PER-SUBTEST CENSUS, RE-DERIVED RATHER THAN INHERITED** (round 833's own lesson): the category is 16 files, 1 whole-file-skipped by tsgo, **25 subtests, 9 failing, 0 emit failures** — round 831's counts exactly. But the per-CASE diffs, which no prior round captured, show the 9 are **six unrelated gaps wearing an `object` costume**, and only three of them are about `object` at all. **THE DEFECT, and it reaches further than the keyword:** `isSimpleTypeRelatedTo`'s NonPrimitive leg read `!sf.hasAny(TypeFlags.Primitive or Null or Undefined or Void)` where tsc's rule is the POSITIVE `s & TypeFlags.Object && t & TypeFlags.NonPrimitive`. Those differ because **`TypeFlags.Primitive` omits every LITERAL bit** — so a `Type.StringLiteral`/`NumberLiteral`/boolean-literal source, a bare `Type.TypeParam`, `unknown`, and the instantiable `keyof`/indexed-access types ALL satisfied `object` silently. The furthest consequence was a **false positive one type away from any `object`**: `T[P] extends V | object ? 1 : 0` answered TRUE for a numeric-literal `T[P]`, so a mapped type computed `1 | 1` where tsc computes `1 | 0`. **WHAT LANDED — four changes, every one keyed on the target's `NonPrimitive` flag:** (1) the relation leg inverted to tsc's positive form; (2) a `structuredTypeRelatedTo` arm deciding a `Type.TypeParam` source by its CONSTRAINT (unconstrained `T` rejects, `T extends object` and `T extends {}` accept); (3) `canUseTypeEngine` opened for a `Type.TypeParam` source against a NonPrimitive target (the B60.8 rule below it admits only a CONSTRAINED one — precisely the case that is not an error); (4) a new `isArgCheckableType` = `isSimpleCheckableType` + NonPrimitive, used at **exactly two** argument-position gates. **`nonPrimitiveInFunction` and `nonPrimitiveInGeneric` are GREEN.** **WHAT DID NOT WORK, all three found by measurement and reverted or narrowed:** (a) widening `isSimpleCheckableType` ITSELF — it has 45 consumers, several of them dedicated walkers whose entire FP firewall it is, and B498's `f<object>(123)` walker immediately co-emitted a DUPLICATE TS2345 at one position; hence the separate predicate and its two named call sites, with a pin (`reported exactly once`) that only an explicit type argument can discriminate. (b) opening `canUseTypeEngine` for a UNION source too: it correctly rejects `object | null -> object`, but the same gate then FPs for a union the flow HAS narrowed and we have not (`var e: object | null; e = a; a = e` is legal in tsc) — it bought 3 diagnostics in a case that stays red for 12 other reasons, so it was dropped and the gap is recorded, not hidden. (c) the **one regression the full suite found** was the co-emission CLAUDE.md predicts: `checkSymbolAsWeakTypeArg`'s KDoc claimed "purely ADDITIVE" because the general check skipped `object` params — no longer true, so 8 of `dissallowSymbolAsWeakType`'s 12 diagnostics doubled. Fixed by the prescribed retraction keyed on (code, fileName, start, MESSAGE), with the stale KDoc corrected; the walker keeps its two uniquely-owned TS2769 chains._

_**THE RE-COSTING, which is the round's deliverable as much as the code: `nonPrimitive` needs SIX more sub-steps and two of them are not `object` work at all.** The 7 residual cases: **`nonPrimitiveConstraintOfIndexAccessType`** wants 10x TS2322 `string -> T[P]` — pure INDEXED-ACCESS assignability, we emit nothing, and `object` appears in 1 of its 10 constraints; **`nonPrimitiveAndTypeVariables`** (1 diff) is blocked by the GENERAL `TypeParam -> TypeParam` lenience (`srcConstraint == null || tgtConstraint == null -> return true`) plus a union DISPLAY-ORDER divergence (tsc sorts by type id and prints `object | U`; we preserve source order); **`nonPrimitiveNarrow`** (3) and **`nonPrimitiveStrictNull`** (12) want flow narrowing on `object` — `typeof x === 'number'` to `never`, and 8 TS18047/18048/18049 nullish-access diagnostics; **`nonPrimitiveAccessProperty`** (2) wants TS2339 on an `object` receiver (through the repo's most FP-sensitive walker); **`nonPrimitiveUnionIntersection`** (4) wants `getIntersectionType` to REDUCE (`object & string` -> `never`, `object & {}` -> `object`) plus excess-property checking through such an intersection — bounded but it changes intersection identity and display; **`nonPrimitiveAssignError`** (1) is the only cheap one left, a message shape (TS2741 with the source displayed as its apparent `{}` + TS2728, where we print TS2322). **So, like round 833's `typeSatisfaction`, the 0-emit-failure count overstated tractability — and for the same reason: a category's name describes its FIXTURES, not its gaps.**_

_**GATES.** Suite **13,847 / 0 / 3** from a wiped results dir, `xml.etree` (13,833 + 14 `NonPrimitiveObjectTypeTest` pins, **0 regressions**). `cost_gate.py` **PASS — every counter +0.00%**, `output.errors` 46 unchanged, so no rebaseline was needed or taken. `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, **0 over the limit** (largest 6,353). **THE 8-PROFILE GRID WAS RUN AGAINST A GENUINELY REBUILT BEFORE-ARM**, not against recorded counts: `Checker.kt` backed up, reverted, rebuilt, all 8 captured, restored, rebuilt. Every capture non-empty with no `more error(s)` truncation tell; counts 46x7 + harness 94; **`added=0 removed=0` and the sorted-capture md5 IDENTICAL on all eight**, the compiler profile at `4caacf248ce417899c2972c16a82f1ed` — the digest rounds 828, 832 and 833 recorded. A count match is not a diff (round 811), which is why the before-arm was rebuilt rather than quoted._

_Round 840 (2026-08-06) — **(AOT.4)(a): THE SHIPPED LAUNCHER COULD NOT REACH THE WARM SERVER IT SHIPS.** `scripts/xtsc` and `scripts/xtsc-aot` both ran `com.xemantic.typescript.compiler.MainKt`, while the mode dispatcher is `com.xemantic.typescript.compiler.server.XtscMainKt` — so `--serve` and `--daemon` were **unreachable through the command users are told to run**, and round 839's warm request (~7.3 s against ~13.6 s for a cached one-shot) was measured by invoking `java` by hand. Round 839 recorded the gap in (JIT.2b)'s residue; this round closes it. The dispatcher's `main` is a strict SUPERSET of the plain one (`--serve`, `--daemon`, else `com.xemantic.typescript.compiler.main(args)` verbatim), so a one-shot run gains two class loads and nothing else. **BOTH scripts had to swap together, and that is what the new pins exist to catch:** the main class is the `mainclass` field of the AOT fingerprint block, so a HALF-swap trains a cache under a fingerprint the launcher can never look up and every launch then reads `SKIP manifest-mismatch` — **silently, forever, with the lost 1.638× as the only symptom**. The fingerprint move is a deliberate, designed invalidation and is fail-safe: round 839's shipped cache now reads `SKIP no-cache-file`, **exit 0**, plus the retrain hint; `XTSC_AOT_LAUNCHER_VERSION` was deliberately NOT bumped, because `mainclass` is already its own field and that constant is reserved for command-line changes no other field records. **VERIFIED END TO END THROUGH THE LAUNCHER** (2-file scratch project): `--serve` logged `request 1 served in 1541 ms` / `request 2 served in 218 ms`; two `--daemon` runs produced output **diff-identical** to the one-shot `--noEmit --listAll` at 1,523 ms cold / 217 ms warm and 0.25 s client CPU; and the AOT path still works — a fresh `train` produced a 37,457,920-byte self-verified cache, `status` → `USE …`, a cached run diff-identical to plain at **487 ms vs 1,553 ms**, with `-Xlog:class+load=info` showing **795 of 795** `com.xemantic` classes from `source: shared objects file`, `XtscMainKt` and `CompileServer` among them (served, not merely opened). **PINS: +2 in `AotCacheGuardTest` (now 10), ablated ONE MISTAKE AT A TIME** over a copied `scripts/` dir via `XTSC_TEST_LAUNCHER`, never the tree itself: half-swap → **9 of 10** fail; both back on `MainKt` (round 839's actual state) → **exactly 1**, `the launcher reaches the server dispatcher`; a typo'd class in both → the same 1. **HONEST ABOUT COVERAGE:** `the launcher and the trainer name the same main class` is **not** uniquely discriminating (the fixture builds its manifest with `xtsc-aot` and decides with `xtsc`, so any disagreement already breaks its `USE` precondition) — it is the pin that *names* the two differing classes, said in its KDoc rather than claimed. Side effect recorded: `scripts/aot-corpus-suite.sh`'s both-arms failure baseline moves **10 → 12** (the new pins read the classpath LAYOUT, like the existing 8). An aside: the first socket attempt failed because a 117-byte scratchpad path exceeds `sockaddr_un`'s ~100 — the dispatcher refusing it BY NAME was itself the first proof the dispatcher was reached. **GATES: suite 13,900 → 13,902 / 0 / 3** from a wiped results dir (`xml.etree`), the +2 exactly the new pins, 0 regressions; `cost_gate.py` and `huge_methods.py` **not run and not required** — shell scripts, `src/jvmTest` and docs only, no `src/commonMain` or `src/jvmMain` change. **NOT VERIFIED, named rather than implied:** `--serve`/`--daemon` UNDER a cache; training with `--serve` in the workload; whether the two extra class loads shift any timing; and the shipped `~/.cache` cache was NOT retrained on the 78-file compiler profile (28 s + ~51 MB). **A NEW OWNER-GATED FINDING:** `build.gradle.kts` sets `nativeImageMainClass = …MainKt` while `XtscMain`'s KDoc says the native image is meant to *be* the thin client — a SECOND instance of exactly this gap, filed as (AOT.4)(b), put to the owner, and **approved and landed the same round** (below). Commit `454bcd12`; `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 8._

_**Round 840(b) (2026-08-06) — (AOT.4)(b), OWNER-APPROVED: THE SAME GAP IN THE GRAALVM NATIVE IMAGE, AND ON THE SHIPPED BINARY IT WAS NOT AN UNREACHABLE MODE BUT A SILENT WRONG SUCCESS THAT WROTE FILES.**  The owner lifted the Guardrails block for this one `build.gradle.kts` property; every other build-system change stays gated, and the approval reached the implementing agent RELAYED through the orchestrating session rather than witnessed directly — recorded because Guardrails turn on exactly that distinction, and the change is one revertible string. **The gap was measured on the artifact, not inferred from the source:** the stale 2026-07-30 `build/native/xtsc` (built from `MainKt`) run as `--serve --socket /tmp/r840b.sock` bound **no socket**, took the socket path as the PROJECT, and **emitted 173 output files at exit 0**; `--daemon` identically. So the prediction ("the mode is unreachable") was too kind — it silently does something else and writes to disk. **The one GraalVM risk settleable without a build was settled:** `javap -c CompileServer` shows all four kotlinx-serialization resolutions are the compiler-plugin intrinsic (`invokevirtual …$Companion.serializer()`), no reflective `serializer(KType)`, and serialization was already reachable from the old entry point via `TsConfigLoader` — so the swap adds no new reflection. **NOTHING NATIVE WAS BUILT OR RUN, and that is the live part of this entry:** GraalVM is not installed on this box, so `./gradlew nativeImage` has never run against the dispatcher (**the image may not even build**), `--serve` has never run on a native image at all, `UnixDomainSocketAddress`/`ServerSocketChannel(UNIX)` under closed-world analysis is unknown and **no metadata was invented for it**, image size/build time move by an unmeasured amount and direction, and the **13,350 ms** native figure quoted throughout the docs is still a `MainKt` measurement. The pin asserts the build script and `scripts/xtsc` name the SAME class — composing with (a)'s executing pin, which is what proves that class actually dispatches — and its KDoc says plainly it cannot see whether the image builds or serves; ablation: reverting the constant fails that pin **and only it** (1 of 11), while editing `compileTsProject`'s unrelated `mainClass` fails none, checked via the XMLs rather than `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` because a build-script edit can leave `jvmTest` UP-TO-DATE and make a green result vacuous. `scripts/aot-corpus-suite.sh` both-arms baseline 12 → 13. **GATES: suite 13,902 → 13,903 / 0 / 3** (638 XMLs, `xml.etree`), the +1 exactly the pin, 0 regressions; `cost_gate.py`/`huge_methods.py` not run and not required — no `src/commonMain` or `src/jvmMain` change, and test generation is untouched. **Process lesson, now a CLAUDE.md entry:** piping a gradle run through `| tail`/`| grep` buffers until the pipeline EXITS, so a run that dies mid-flight leaves a **zero-byte log** — no `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, no `tests completed`, nothing to tell a killed run from one that never started, which defeats the round-831 rule to read the log before believing an empty results dir. This file's own "How to run tests" incantation carries that hazard. Commit `e7d933d2`._

_Round 839 (2026-08-05) — **OWNER REDIRECT: LEAVE M3.0 CONFORMANCE, RETURN TO M5 / PERFORMANCE. (JIT.2b)'s RESIDUE IS CLOSED — THE SHIPPED AOT CACHE IS NOW VALIDATED IN EVERY MODE WE SHIP, AND THERE IS NO CACHED-vs-UNCACHED DIVERGENCE ANYWHERE.** Rounds 828/832 measured and shipped the JDK 25 AOT cache on ONE mode of ONE profile (`--noEmit`, compiler profile) and recorded four things as explicitly unvalidated. All four ran this round, identical command shape in both arms (`docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 7). **33 whole-project compiles, 8 server requests, 4 emitted output trees and 2 full corpus-suite runs agreed — every `--noEmit --listAll` capture, cached and uncached, sequential and `--workers 2/4/8`, one-shot and server, shares ONE sorted-diagnostics md5 (`59d930db…`, 46 errors).** **(a) EMIT** — the one that mattered, because `--noEmit` never runs the Transformer or Emitter and `xtsc-aot train` trains with `--noEmit`: `dist/` wiped and re-hashed per run, **78 output files sha256-identical file-for-file across all four runs**, diagnostics diff empty, **1.49×** (26,109 → 17,448 ms) against **1.66×** check-only on the same binary — and the emit TAIL costs MORE under the cache (3,134 vs 2,310 ms), because a cached run reaches the emitter after 14 s of warm-up instead of 24 s and the cache carries no emitter profile; that prices "train with emit" at ~800 ms, ~5% of a cached emit run. **(b) THE CORPUS SUITE THROUGH A CACHED JVM** — Gradle's test worker cannot carry an AOT arm (the cache binds to a classpath whose dump-time form must be a PREFIX of the runtime one, and Gradle builds the worker's), so `scripts/aot-corpus-suite.sh`, landed this round, drives it in one plain `java` process over the trained jar + its 7 deps in that exact order: **638 classes, 13,897 run + 3 ignored = the gate's 13,900, and the per-class result files diff EMPTY between the arms, failure lines included**. The cache was proven IN USE and not merely opened — **922 `com.xemantic` classes loaded with `source: shared objects file`**, round 828's 921/926 population. The 10 failures present identically in BOTH arms are jar-vs-exploded-dir harness artifacts (`AotCacheGuardTest` ×8 "URI is not hierarchical", `HugeMethodLimitTest` ×2 "main classes are not a directory"), both green under Gradle; no corpus baseline failed on either arm. **(c) `--workers`** with the DISTRIBUTION the standing rule demands (rounds 754/824 each shipped a false green from one draw): 20 cached runs (5 each at seq/w2/w4/w8) + 9 uncached, 46 errors and one md5 in all 29. Cached medians seq 14,514 / w2 12,779 / **w4 12,343** / w8 16,368 ms; uncached seq 22,975 / w4 18,966 / w8 20,342 — blocked, not interleaved, so directional only, but **the two levers OVERLAP** (cached-w4 vs plain-seq is **1.86×**, not the 2.26× the headline ratios would multiply to) and **`--workers 8` under a cache is worse than cached SEQUENTIAL**, a reversal of the uncached order. **(d) `--serve`** — the cache halves the FIRST request (23,536 → 13,987 ms) and is **worth nothing warm** (7,268 vs 7,310 ms): both remove interpreter warm-up, so they are the same lever seen twice. Gap found on the way: **`scripts/xtsc` cannot reach `--serve` at all** (its main class is `MainKt`; the dispatcher is `server.XtscMainKt`). **WHAT DID NOT WORK:** driving the suite through Gradle — `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` reaches the worker only via the daemon's environment and the worker's classpath is Gradle's, not the trained one, so the cache would have been silently unused and the run would have looked exactly like a green one (hence the class-load count above); and a standalone suite run without `kotlin-power-assert-runtime-jvm`, which read **4,998 failed in BOTH arms** — a same-in-both-arms result is not automatically a valid one. **NOT REACHED, named rather than implied:** a cache trained under emit or under `--workers 4`; `--watch`/`--incremental` under a cache; the other seven dashboard profiles; and the launcher's `--serve` gap. **GATES: no `src/` change this round** (docs + two new scripts), so the suite / `cost_gate.py` / `huge_methods.py` gates were not required — though the corpus suite was in fact run twice, standalone, with zero non-artifact failures on either arm._

_**Round 843 (2026-08-07) — (WARM.1)(a): THE FIRST WARM-JVM ATTRIBUTION THIS REPO HAS EVER TAKEN, AND IT CORRECTS THE ARC'S HEADLINE ARTIFACT NUMBER BY 40%.** Owner directive: prioritize profiling-based perf on the WARMED-UP JVM, now that a client-server architecture exists. **The premise was verified, not assumed: every per-pass table in `docs/perf` was produced by a COLD one-shot `MainKt` JVM** — four independent strands (each table's own "median of 3 probe-free runs" line, the literal command at `setup-phase-and-huge-methods.md` § 644, `cost_gate.py`'s recipe, and `BenchMain`'s inability to accept a flag) — so the cost map every queue decision rests on describes an artifact we no longer ship. **THE LADDER, re-measured today on one binary and one profile:** probe-free cold **22,971 ms** (n=3); warm `BenchMain` median **6,917 / 7,143 ms**; the real `--serve` ladder **22,753 → 10,898 → 7,754 → 7,606 → 7,447 → 7,410 → 7,447 → 7,100 ms** — two INDEPENDENT warm harnesses agreeing at ~7.0-7.4 s against a record of **11,580 ms** (`CompileServer.kt`'s own KDoc, `aot-native-image.md` § 1/§ 4, § 0.1's endgame framing). **Over the same interval cold improved 12.6% and warm improved 39.5%.** Leading HYPOTHESIS, labelled as one and NOT confirmed this round: the **(JIT.1)** huge-method arc — a method over `HugeMethodLimit` is never JIT-compiled, so removing one pays in STEADY STATE, and rounds 802-821 were measured only with the COLD instrument, where round 803's own falsifier read `NOISE-DOMINATED`. Queued as **(WARM.1)(b)**: a warm A/B of a pre-802 binary vs HEAD would settle it, and if confirmed that arc bought ~3× what it was credited with. **THE INSTRUMENT PRICES ITSELF — the central methodological result:** `--passTiming` costs **~2,840 ms cold (+12.4%)** and **3,450-3,945 ms warm (~+50%)**, so every absolute ms in every `docs/perf` table is probe-inflated — harmlessly cold, dominatingly warm — and **a cold-table share is NOT comparable to a warm-table share; only the RATIOS are.** One deduction needs no assumption: the warm instrumented front end is 907.9 ms in total and cannot hold a 3,945 ms probe, so **>=77% of the probe is inside checker-init**; where inside is unmeasured, and the doc brackets `checkSpine`'s warm share two ways (72% vs 83%) and refuses to pick — queued as (WARM.1)(c), the blocker on sizing any warm lever. **FINDINGS THAT SURVIVE (ratios, both arms inflated):** the front end warms **~4.0×** against the checker's **~2.2×**, so the checker's share RISES warm (86.2% → 91.8%), opposite to what § 0.1's cold budget suggests; the narrowing tail FLATTENS — `>=1ms` walks 134 → 37, their share of narrowing ~47-50% → ~32-34% over IDENTICAL 17,851 walks and 583,779 visits (the buckets are time-keyed), so round 735's "extreme tail" replicates exactly as a **COLD** property and is scope-qualified, not retracted; and per-kind warm-up is **not ordered by cost** (`FUNCTION_DECLARATION` at 64.9 µs warms 2.86×, `BINARY_EXPRESSION` at 30.8 µs warms 2.08×) — what IS sign-consistent is IDENTIFIER's top-12 share falling 9.1% → ~7.1% while the five statement anchors rise 48.4% → ~50.4%. **A REAL DEFECT THE CLIENT-SERVER ARCHITECTURE EXPOSED, found while building the instrument:** `Main.kt` enabled `PassTiming` and never disabled it, and `CompileServer.compileCapturing` calls that same `main()` per request in one long-lived JVM — so **one `--daemon --passTiming` request permanently instrumented every later request**, and worse, `--globalsAmp N` leaked a mode making every globals lookup re-read its key N times forever while `--verifyMappedCache` leaked one recomputing every served cache hit. Fixed by mirroring what `SpineDispatch`/`SpineSections` already do. **The same loop still leaks `ParallelCheckMode.workers`, `FlowScan.legacy`, `ArgNarrowGate.mode` and three more that CHANGE COMPILER BEHAVIOUR — a correctness hazard under `--serve`, deliberately not fixed blind, queued as (SERVE.1).** New instrument: `BenchMain` takes an opt-in 4th argument running ONE instrumented rebuild strictly AFTER the measured loop (so `medianMs` stays probe-free and the probe's cost is the difference), aborting non-zero if its files/errors disagree. **GATES: suite 13,909 → 13,911 / 0 / 3** from a wiped results dir (`xml.etree`), the **+2 exactly the two new pins**, 0 regressions; `cost_gate.py` **every counter +0.00%**; `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0 — both required and run, `commonMain` changed. **NOT measured, named rather than implied:** no warm A/B was run and nothing was optimized this round; the probe's landing site inside checker-init; one profile only; and **no tsc number** — node is not installed on this box, so every parity statement in the new doc is DERIVED from a CI ratio and labelled as such. `docs/perf/warm-jvm-attribution.md`._

_**Round 858 (2026-08-08) — THE THIRD "HARNESS LOADS THE WRONG ARTIFACT" AUDIT IN SIX ROUNDS, AND THE FIRST WHERE THE MEASUREMENTS SURVIVE INTACT: THE WARM ARC STANDS, `build/bench/cp.txt` NOW HAS ZERO READERS, AND BATCH 2 KILLED THIS ROUND'S OWN HEADLINE NUMBER.** Round 857's parting observation — `cp.txt` is the pre-split, pre-bump dependency list — audited per reader. **`ab-interleaved.sh` and `cost_gate.py` SOUND** (both resolve FRESH through the init script; neither ever read it). **`ab-warm.sh` SOUND TODAY, GUARD INCOMPLETE**: its `cp-warm.txt` content is CURRENT (2.4.10 / 0.9.1 / 1.11.0) and its guard currently fails anyway, but it compared the cache only against the **module's** `build.gradle.kts` while the versions live in `gradle/libs.versions.toml` — **blind to exactly the change that produces a stale tail**. **`aot-draw-variance.sh` BROKEN**: `<core jar>:$(cat cp.txt)` plus a main class (`XtscMainKt`) that lives in the DAEMON module and is absent from it — verified `ClassNotFoundException`, dead since the split like `scripts/xtsc` was until round 857, but **failing LOUDLY, so it could never produce a wrong number**. **The documented "pre-split refusal" never guarded `cp.txt`** — it greps the INIT SCRIPT, and in `ab-warm.sh` sits in the resolve branch, so a cache hit skips it. **The one thing that touched published numbers:** `round847`/`round849`'s harnesses ran WARM arms on the current tail and COLD arms on the stale one, so their cold/warm RATIOS compared two tails. **THE DECISIVE EXPERIMENT — behaviour IDENTICAL:** same binary/profile, only the tail swapped, rotated interleave; all 12 timed compiles `46 errors` and **all four `--listAll` digests `59d930db…`** (round-841 lineage), and both tails link. **AND THE TIMING CLAIM DIED IN BATCH 2, which is the round's best result:** batch 1 read stale 23,633 → fresh 24,420 ms, **+3.63%, 3/3, both arm sds <1%** — one batch from being written up as real and used to "correct" two rounds; batch 2 read **+0.33%, 2/3** with deltas +79/−184/+317, and the **fresh arm itself moved −2.7% between batches on a byte-identical configuration**, so batch 1's drift simply landed on one arm. **Round 840(c)'s rule, second confirmation this session and the first where obeying it REVERSED the conclusion.** **So, plainly: the warm arc STANDS and nothing needs re-taking** — 845/846/848/850/851 never touched the stale tail, 847/849's cold arms did but the difference is behaviourally nil and not a demonstrated timing effect, and **round 845's (JIT.1) −33.6%, 4/4, 1.505× is untouched by construction** (both arms built in a throwaway worktree from pre-split commits). **THE GUARD:** `scripts/lib/dep-classpath.sh`, one shared resolver refusing a cache unless non-empty, newer than EVERY build-definition input (`libs.versions.toml` first) and naming only entries that exist; all readers rewired, `aot-draw-variance.sh` additionally given round 857's staged-lib-dir treatment. **ABLATION: three arms, each failing EXACTLY ONE uniquely-its-own pin** — and dropping `libs.versions.toml` reddens only the libs-versions pin **while the module-build-file pin stays GREEN**, which is precisely the state `ab-warm.sh` was in. **GATES: suite 14,036 / 0 failures / 3 skipped** (14,030 + 6 new pins). `docs/perf/classpath-readers-audit.md`._

_**Round 870 (2026-08-09) — (WARM.17): THE WARM LEAF PROFILE RE-TAKEN, AND ITS OWN VALIDITY CHECK PASSED — THE TWO FAMILIES ROUNDS 868/869 FIXED ARE GONE FROM ITS TABLE (**barrel search 471 -> 61 ms**, **annotation frames 153 -> 40 ms**), NOTHING NEW ROSE INTO THEIR PLACE, AND THE TOP SURVIVING CANDIDATE IS TAKEN.**_ Same recipe, two processes, and — what makes the tables comparable at all — round 868's own dumps RE-AGGREGATED with the same script, now committed as `scripts/leaf_owner_profile.py` (it REFUSES a `jfr print` dump truncated to 5 frames, filters to the compile thread, charges stdlib leaves to the nearest non-stdlib OWNER). `checkSpine` INCLUSIVE reads **75.28%/74.34%** against this arc's known ~74%. The annotation-frame delta of **113.3 ms** is a genuine cross-instrument agreement with round 869's independent amplifier (**129.7 ms**), 13% apart. **THE DENOMINATOR TRAP, NAMED: the JFR window is a fixed 90 s of steady state, so a share is a share of WALL TIME, not of a rebuild — an unchanged per-rebuild cost READS HIGHER by 1.099 once the compile got faster**, which is why every cross-round row is quoted in ms/rebuild and why the rows that 'moved up' (`ctaFnBodyFrame`, `ctaSpineEnter`, `lookupPerFileForNode`) did not move at all. Shape unchanged: HashMap/HashSet **26.6/27.3%**, `java.util.regex` **0.04/0.13%**. **THE CANDIDATE:** `computeTypeParamInfo` (1.47/1.35%), flagged as unpriced by round 868; `ciaMutualFnDecls$resolve` is bigger in one process and DISCARDED for not replicating (1.40% vs 0.38% on the same binary). **PRICED INDEPENDENTLY** by a counter+span census (`FrontEnd.TPI`), deterministic to the unit over two processes: **82 ms = 1.16% of a warm rebuild** — and **the memo is not the problem, it answers 96.2% of the 28,663 questions**. The cost is one of the miss's three lookups: **2,992,718 symbols iterated to reach 2,184 module-flagged ones — 99.93% of the scan re-decides a question that is a property of the binder tables and not of the name asked**. **THE FIX** (`buildModuleSymbolScanIndex`, commonMain, `internal`, own file) hoists the module-symbol list out of the miss, preserving file order, within-file order and DUPLICATES (one `Symbol` really is in two files' `locals` — `mergeModuleAugmentations` creates that); the `exports` probe stays LIVE because it is a `var` the checker writes to. **Row CONTROLLED (round 793): calls / misses / file probes / module probes / answered / null are IDENTICAL to the unit and only the scan width moves — 2,992,718 -> 2,277 (1,314x), 82 -> 15 ms.** Captured **0.95%**; the 15 ms residue is **0.21%, below the floor, deliberately not taken** — and the candidate's 1.16% is what justified building, said in both directions. No whole-rebuild A/B quoted (rounds 858/869 § 13). **PINS + ABLATION:** `ModuleSymbolScanIndexTest` (11), NINE single-mistake ablations; A1/A2 a deliberate PAIR because `SymbolFlags.Module` is the UNION of `ValueModule` and `NamespaceModule` and either half alone compiles; **A6 initially left every pin GREEN and an 11th pin was CUT to give it a failure of its own** (round 869's rule); one pin recorded as structurally UN-ABLATABLE and A5/A8 as one discriminated position, not two. **GATES: suite 14,139 -> 14,150 / 0 failures / 3 skipped**, `cost_gate.py` **+0.00% on all 20 counters**, `huge_methods.py --fail-over 0` exit 0, **8-profile grid added=0 removed=0 on all eight with BOTH arms rebuilt from source and diffed in both directions**. `docs/perf/warm-leaf-profile.md` §§ 14-20._
